# Verification appendix (2026-09-24)

After the design panel produced the unified plan, 13 load-bearing claims were each checked by two independent skeptics: one checking primary sources (official docs, source code, release pages, registries, GBATEK), one attacking the claim as an engineer with non-mutating experiments on this machine. Three critics then reviewed the whole plan from the beginner, parallel-execution and DS-engineering angles. `PLAN.md` already incorporates the corrections below; this file is the audit trail.

No claim was refuted outright. Most were *partially true*: the decision stands but a flag, path, number or procedure needed correcting. Nothing here required installing software; items marked as residual risk are settled by the Phase-0 spikes in `PLAN.md`.

| # | Claim | Source check | Engineering check |

## 1. Wonderful/BlocksDS silent install into C:\msys64

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** The Wonderful Toolchain bootstrap installs silently with `wf-bootstrap-windows-x86_64.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /DIR=C:\msys64` into the existing MSYS2 (it is an Inno Setup installer defaulting to C:\msys64, files under C:\msys64\opt\wonderful), and `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools`, `wf-config repo enable blocksds`, `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm`, `wf-pacman -S --noconfirm blocksds-toolchain blocksds-docs` complete under a non-interactive `C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc` when the environment replicates wonderful_shell.cmd (MSYSTEM=UCRT64, MSYS2_PATH_TYPE=inherit, PATH prefixed with C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin, BLOCKSDS=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/core, BLOCKSDSEXT=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/external, WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN=/opt/wonderful). BlocksDS is at version 1.24.0 (2026-09-21) with GCC 16.2.0, binutils 2.47, picolibc 1.8.12.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** 1) Installer: `wf-bootstrap-windows-x86_64.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /NORESTART /CURRENTUSER /DIR=C:\msys64` — `/CURRENTUSER` is required for a prompt-free install because the .iss omits PrivilegesRequired (Inno default `admin` => UAC elevation prompt even under /VERYSILENT); `PrivilegesRequiredOverridesAllowed=commandline` permits it and C:\msys64 is writable by Authenticated Users (icacls). Without it, run the installer from an already-elevated process. Files land in C:\msys64\opt\wonderful (confirmed).
2) Package steps must tolerate wf-pacman's self-update: the bootstrap bundles wf-pacman 7.1.0-1 while the repo has 7.1.0-3, and wf-pacman's core-update patch makes `-Syu` upgrade only itself, print "Run wf-pacman again in order to finish the upgrade." and exit 0 WITHOUT installing the requested targets. Corrected sequence under `C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc` with the wonderful_shell.cmd env:
   wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools      # first run: upgrades wf-pacman only, exit 0
   wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools      # second run: installs wf-tools (provides /opt/wonderful/bin/wf-config)
   test -x /opt/wonderful/bin/wf-config     # guard before continuing
   wf-config repo enable blocksds
   wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm
   wf-pacman -S --noconfirm blocksds-toolchain blocksds-docs
   (Robust form: repeat `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools` until wf-config exists or the output no longer contains "Run wf-pacman again".)
3) Version facts stand as claimed: BlocksDS 1.24.0 (2026-09-21; packages blocksds-toolchain/blocksds-docs 1.24.0-1 built 2026-09-20); toolchain = GCC 16.2.0 (pkg 1~16.2.0.r229124.be93d9d35bf-1), binutils 2.47 (2.47-2), picolibc 1.8.12 (1.8.12.r26255.40c274b4a-1). Environment variables exactly match wonderful_shell.cmd.

**Impact on the plan.** The v1 "auto-install toolchain" step (research-toolchain.md line 155; plan's install workstream) would, as written, (a) pop a UAC consent dialog or fail when launched from a non-elevated/non-interactive IDE process, and (b) silently end with no BlocksDS installed: first `-Syu wf-tools` only self-updates wf-pacman (exit 0), `wf-config` is then 'command not found', and `wf-pacman -S blocksds-toolchain` fails with 'target not found' because the blocksds repo was never enabled. The installer step needs `/CURRENTUSER` (or an elevated launcher), and the pacman step needs the double `-Syu wf-tools` (or a loop keyed on wf-config's existence) plus exit-code/output checks; the IDE's 'installed' detection should key on C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\ndstool\ndstool.exe, not merely on wf-pacman.exe. Any later bootstrap re-publish that catches up with the repo would make the first `-Syu` succeed in one pass, so the loop form is the durable choice.

**Residual risk.** Only an actual run settles: (1) whether the compiled exe's PrivilegesRequired matches the repo .iss (innoextract 1.9 cannot read Inno 6.4.3 headers; the .iss predates the 2026-01-13 build and is the maintainer's only source); (2) wf-pacman 7.1.0-1 (built Jan 2026) running against the local msys-2.0.dll 3.6.9 and downloading over HTTPS with MSYS2's ca-bundle under a non-TTY `bash -lc` (progress-bar/pipe patches exist but were not exercised); (3) the wf-tools-lua post_install `bin/wf-config migrate` scriptlet and `wf-config repo enable`'s symlink-then-copy fallback behaving on Windows without Developer Mode; (4) total download size/time and whether `CheckSpace` warnings appear; (5) end-to-end `make` of a BlocksDS example with MSYS2's make 4.4.1-2 under the replicated env (not part of this claim but the natural Phase 0 smoke test).

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** 1) Installer command: `wf-bootstrap-windows-x86_64.exe /VERYSILENT /SUPPRESSMSGBOXES /CURRENTUSER /DIR=C:\msys64 /LOG=<scratch>\wf-bootstrap.log` — `/CURRENTUSER` is required for a prompt-free run: the shipped .iss has no PrivilegesRequired line (Inno default = admin), so without it an unelevated Node child_process triggers a UAC consent dialog; `PrivilegesRequiredOverridesAllowed=commandline` permits the override and C:\msys64 grants Modify to Authenticated Users. Document the installer's side effects: it is Inno Setup 6.4.3 (unsigned), copies the bootstrap (wf-pacman 7.1.0-1, runtime-msys2-shell, wonderful_shell.cmd) to C:\msys64\opt\wonderful, writes C:\msys64\unins000.exe/.dat plus an HKCU uninstall key and a per-user Start Menu shortcut, and RUNS `C:\msys64\usr\bin\pacman.exe -Sy --noconfirm make` (refreshes the MSYS2 sync DBs and upgrades make 4.4.1-2 -> 4.4.1-3+; needs network; exit code ignored by Inno). Pre-check that C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe and usr\bin\pacman.exe exist before running it (DirDoesntExist is auto-answered Yes and would create an empty C:\msys64). Alternative that avoids UAC, the uninstaller files and the pacman side effect: extract https://wonderful.asie.pl/bootstrap/wf-bootstrap-windows-x86_64.tar.gz (4.9 MB, same payload) into C:\msys64\opt\wonderful (the official 'Windows (Manual)' route).
2) Package sequence (all via `C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc`, env as in the claim): run `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools` TWICE (loop until `/opt/wonderful/bin/wf-config` exists). The bootstrap ships wf-pacman 7.1.0-1 while the repo has 7.1.0-3; wf-pacman's core-update patch upgrades only itself, prints 'Run wf-pacman again in order to finish the upgrade.' and exits 0 WITHOUT installing wf-tools, so `wf-config repo enable blocksds` right after a single run fails with exit 127. Then: `wf-config repo enable blocksds`, `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm`, `wf-pacman -S --noconfirm blocksds-toolchain blocksds-docs`. Budget ~177 MB download / ~591 MB installed. Detection of a finished install: C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin\wf-config AND ...\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\ndstool\ndstool.exe.
3) Confirmed as stated: `bash.exe -lc` from Node with MSYSTEM=UCRT64, MSYS2_PATH_TYPE=inherit, PATH prefixed with C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin, BLOCKSDS/BLOCKSDSEXT/WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN reproduces the Wonderful shell (PATH order /ucrt64/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/opt/wonderful/bin:..., cwd preserved, exit codes propagate); `-l` is mandatory (wf-pacman.exe needs msys-2.0.dll from /usr/bin and wf-config needs cygpath). Versions confirmed: BlocksDS 1.24.0 (2026-09-21), toolchain GCC 16.2.0 (pkg 1~16.2.0.r229124), binutils 2.47-2, picolibc 1.8.12.r26255 (updated in BlocksDS 1.22.3, 2026-08-08).

**Impact on the plan.** The toolchain-installer/detector workstream must: add `/CURRENTUSER` (or treat the installer as an interactive, UAC-prompting step in the UI), make the wf-tools step idempotent/looped instead of a single linear chain, pre-check for an existing MSYS2 before running the installer, and either accept that the installer mutates the user's MSYS2 (pacman -Sy + make upgrade, unins000.exe in C:\msys64) or switch to extracting the bootstrap tarball. Any `&&`-chained one-shot install script as written would stop at `wf-config: command not found`, leaving the toolchain uninstalled while reporting success for the first step. Timeouts must allow ~180 MB of downloads plus a 590 MB extraction under Defender.

**Residual risk.** Not executed here (installing is out of scope): the actual wf-pacman self-replacement on Windows during the core update, network/mirror behaviour, the MSYS2 `pacman -Sy --noconfirm make` step against a SigLevel=Required keyring, the wf-tools post-install `wf-config migrate` hook under wf-pacman's scriptlet shell, and Defender's effect on extracting ~590 MB. Cheapest Phase-0 spike: extract wf-bootstrap-windows-x86_64.tar.gz into C:\msys64\opt\wonderful (or run the exe with /CURRENTUSER /LOG), then from Node run `bash.exe -lc "wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools"` and capture exit code + stdout of the FIRST run (expect exit 0 and 'Run wf-pacman again...'), run it a second time, check `ls /opt/wonderful/bin/wf-config`, then finish with repo enable / -Syu / -S blocksds-toolchain and verify `arm-none-eabi-gcc --version` reports 16.2.0 and `$BLOCKSDS/tools/ndstool/ndstool.exe` exists.

- **Live https://wonderful.asie.pl/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/wonderful.db parsed with Python (2026-09-24) vs the bootstrap's bundled sync db**: Live: wf-pacman 7.1.0-3 (built 2026-01-24); wf-tools 0.2.0-3 (deps wf-tools-lua, wf-tools-native, wf-pacman); wf-tools-lua 0.1.0.r183.ad72e38-1 (2026-09-19); toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-gcc 1~16.2.0.r229124.be93d9d35bf-1 (2026-08-08, 55.8 MB); toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-binutils 2.47-2 (2026-08-15); toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-picolibc-generic 1.8.12.r26255.40c274b4a-1 (2026-08-02); libstdcxx-picolibc 16.2.0...-1 (73.7 MB); runtime-gcc-libs 0.1.0-14. Bootstrap bundles wf-pacman 7.1.0-1 and its Jan-2026 sync db lists GCC 15.2.1 -> a wf-pacman self-upgrade is guaranteed on the first -Syu.
- **wonderful-packages packages/wf-pacman/pacman-7.0.0-core-update.patch (GitHub raw) + strings in the shipped bin/wf-pacman.exe 7.1.0-1**: Patch: in sync_trans(), when -u is given, core_update() runs first; if wf-pacman itself has an update it sets trans->add = core packages only (discarding the wf-tools target), executes that transaction, prints 'Run wf-pacman again in order to finish the upgrade.' and returns 0 without processing the requested targets. The shipped 7.1.0-1 binary contains 'Starting core system upgrade...' and 'Run wf-pacman again in order to finish the upgrade.', so the behaviour is present in the bootstrap. Consequence: a single `wf-pacman -Syu --noconfirm wf-tools` exits 0 with wf-tools NOT installed, and the next step `wf-config repo enable blocksds` fails with 'command not found' (exit 127).
- **codeberg.org/WonderfulToolchain/wf-tools (main, updated 2026-09-19; GitHub mirror archived 2026-01-12): lua/wf-config.lua, lua/wf/internal/tool/config/repo.lua, lua/wf/internal/path.lua; wonderful-packages packages/wf-tools-lua/PKGBUILD + wf-tools-lua.install**: wf-config is a Lua script (shebang #!/opt/wonderful/bin/wf-lua) installed as /opt/wonderful/bin/wf-config (extension stripped). `repo enable <name>` copies <base>/pacman/config/*-<name>.conf into <base>/etc/pacman.d (tries a symlink, falls back to copy), no prompts. base comes from WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN (default /opt/wonderful) converted with `cygpath -w` on Windows, so it needs an MSYS2 login-shell PATH. post_install hook runs `bin/wf-config migrate` non-interactively.
- **Node 24.16 spawnSync experiment (scratch wfverify/spawntest.js) launching C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc with the replicated wonderful_shell.cmd environment**: Result: MSYSTEM=UCRT64, login shell = yes, stdin not a tty, cwd preserved (no cd to $HOME; also works with cwd 'C:\Program Files'), BLOCKSDS propagated, PATH = /ucrt64/bin:/usr/local/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/opt/wonderful/bin:<inherited Windows PATH> (same ordering the official shell produces), pacman/gcc/make/cygpath resolved, exit code propagated (7). Without -l, /usr/bin is absent and `make` is not found, so -l is mandatory. Setting the PATH key while process.env holds 'Path' worked, but the override should target the existing key name to be safe.
- **https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/ listing + blocksds.db parsed; codeberg blocksds/sdk docs/content/docs/changelog.md**: blocksds-toolchain 1.24.0-1 (BUILDDATE 1789947883 = 2026-09-20 23:44 UTC; depends toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-{binutils,gcc-libs,gcc,picolibc-generic,libstdcxx-picolibc}, runtime-gcc-libs) and blocksds-docs 1.24.0-1. Changelog top: '### Version 1.24.0 (2026-09-21)'; the line 'binutils 2.47, GCC 16.2.0, and picolibc 1.8.12' sits under '### Version 1.22.3 (2026-08-08)'. Full closure for wf-tools + blocksds-toolchain + blocksds-docs = 19 packages, 176.6 MB download, 591.3 MB installed.
- **https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/docs/setup/windows/ and https://wonderful.asie.pl/wiki/doku.php?id=getting_started (fetched 2026-09-24)**: Official sequence matches the claim: wf-pacman -Syu wf-tools; wf-config repo enable blocksds; wf-pacman -Syu; wf-pacman -S blocksds-toolchain [blocksds-docs]. Wonderful page: install MSYS2 first, run the installer, change the directory in the installer if MSYS2 is elsewhere, use only the 'Wonderful Toolchain Shell'.
- **Local MSYS2 state (pacman -Q/-Si/-Qu against the cached sync DBs, read-only)**: make 4.4.1-2 installed; cached msys.db (2026-07-04) already offers make 4.4.1-3 and 89 pending upgrades; MSYS2 SigLevel = Required. The installer's [Run] `pacman.exe -Sy --noconfirm make` will therefore refresh all six MSYS2 sync DBs and upgrade make (a -Sy partial upgrade MSYS2 normally warns against); Inno ignores its exit code.

</details>

## 2. BlocksDS `make` spawned from Node via `bash -lc`

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** `make` with the BlocksDS default `sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile` works when spawned from Node/PowerShell via `bash -lc` outside the Wonderful shell, because `-specs=$(BLOCKSDS)/sys/crts/ds_arm9.specs` resolves crt0 and linker scripts from the BLOCKSDS environment variable alone, and the compiler is at C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\toolchain\gcc-arm-none-eabi\bin\arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe. A runtime Makefile can set NAME, SOURCEDIRS, INCLUDEDIRS, BINDIRS, LIBS := -lmm9 -lnds9 -lc and `include $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile`; files named *.arm.c are compiled with -marm -mlong-calls and ITCM_CODE places a function in ITCM.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** `make` with the BlocksDS 1.24.0 default `sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile` works from Node via `C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc "make -j16"` (cwd = the runtime dir) outside the Wonderful shell ONLY with this spawn env: `CHERE_INVOKING=1` (MSYS2 login shells otherwise `cd $HOME` via /etc/post-install/05-home-dir.post; verified locally), `BLOCKSDS=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/core` and `BLOCKSDSEXT=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/external` (the Makefile's built-in default is `/opt/blocksds/core`, wrong for a Wonderful install), `WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN=/opt/wonderful`, `MSYSTEM=UCRT64`, `MSYS2_PATH_TYPE=inherit`, `PATH=C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin;%PATH%` (runtime-gcc-libs DLLs for the native gcc.exe). `ds_arm9.specs` resolves crt0 and both linker scripts with `%:getenv(BLOCKSDS /sys/crts/...)` (fatal error if BLOCKSDS is unset), and the MSYS2 runtime converts the POSIX `BLOCKSDS` value and `-specs=/opt/...`/`-I`/`-L` arguments to `C:/msys64/opt/...` for the native compiler at `C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\toolchain\gcc-arm-none-eabi\bin\arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe` (Makefile: `$(WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN)/toolchain/gcc-arm-none-eabi/bin/arm-none-eabi-gcc`). The runtime Makefile sets, before the include: `NAME`, `SOURCEDIRS`, `INCLUDEDIRS`, `BINDIRS`, `LIBS := -lmm9 -lnds9`, `LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod` (the default Makefile appends `$(BLOCKSDS)/libs/libnds` and `-lc` itself; without the maxmod LIBDIRS entry `maxmod9.h` and `-lmm9` are not found), then `include $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile`. Files named `*.arm.c` are built by the rule `$(BUILDDIR)/%.arm.c.o : %.arm.c` with `$(CFLAGS) -marm -mlong-calls` (object `build/<NAME>/<path>/vm.arm.c.o`; GNU make's shortest-stem rule picks it over `%.c.o`). `ITCM_CODE` (libnds ndstypes.h, ARM9 only: section `.itcm.text.<file>.<line>` plus `__long_call__`) is collected by `*(.itcm.*)` in ds_arm9.ld into the 32 KB `itcm` region at 0x01000000; it does not select ARM mode by itself, so the VM file must be `vm.arm.c` (or use `ARM_CODE`).

**Impact on the plan.** WS1 `buildRuntime()` (plan line 279: `bash -lc "make -j16"` with that env) silently builds nothing / fails with 'No targets specified and no makefile found' unless `CHERE_INVOKING=1` is added to the spawn env or the command becomes `bash -lc "cd '<posix path>' && make -j16"`; the same applies to any other `bash -lc` invocation that relies on `cwd` (the plan's smoke-build line 369 already uses an explicit `cd`, so it is fine). `scripts/install-toolchain.ps1` and `detectToolchain()` env/paths are otherwise correct and must keep exporting BLOCKSDS/BLOCKSDSEXT/WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN and the `opt\wonderful\bin` PATH prefix. WS3 `runtime/Makefile` (plan line 297) must add `LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod` and can drop `-lc` from LIBS; the `samples/hello` Makefile and the selftest Makefile need the same if they use maxmod. Risk item 1 (line 427) can be narrowed: ds_arm9.specs resolution outside the Wonderful shell is now explained and verified at the MSYS2 layer; the remaining unknown is only the install itself.

**Residual risk.** The Wonderful toolchain and BlocksDS are not installed here, so the end-to-end `make` was not executed. Phase 0 spike: after install, from Node spawn `C:\msys64\usr\bin\bash.exe -lc "make V=1"` in `$BLOCKSDS/examples/maxmod/nitrofs` with the corrected env and confirm (a) the printed gcc command line shows `-specs=C:/msys64/opt/wonderful/...` and the link step finds `ds_arm9_crt0.o`/`.mem`/`.ld` via BLOCKSDS, (b) `arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe` loads its host DLLs from `opt/wonderful/bin` (check with `ldd`), (c) a test `foo.arm.c` produces `build/<NAME>/source/foo.arm.c.o` and `objdump -d` shows ARM (not Thumb) code, (d) an `ITCM_CODE` function lands at 0x01000xxx in the map file, and (e) total ITCM use (libnds's own ITCM code plus the VM) stays under 32 KB. Also confirm the installed GCC version (changelog says 16.2.0 for 1.22.3+; the GitHub PKGBUILD mirror still says 15.2.1) since `-mcpu=arm946e-s+nofp` and `--use-blx` behavior are version-dependent.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/v1.24.0/sys/crts/ds_arm9.specs (downloaded to scratchpad/verify/ds_arm9.specs), lines 17 and 20**: CONFIRMS crt0/linker-script resolution via env var: `*link: %(blocksds_link) -T %:getenv(BLOCKSDS /sys/crts/ds_arm9.mem) -T %:getenv(BLOCKSDS /sys/crts/ds_arm9.ld) --gc-sections ...` and `*startfile: %:getenv(BLOCKSDS /sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0%O)`. libc/libgcc come from the toolchain itself (`%include <picolibc.specs>`, `*lib: %(libgcc)`), libnds from the Makefile's `-L$(BLOCKSDS)/libs/libnds/lib`.
- **GCC manual, Spec Files, https://gcc.gnu.org/onlinedocs/gcc-14.2.0/gcc/Spec-Files.html**: `getenv` spec function: 'If the environment variable is not defined, a fatal error is issued. Otherwise, the return value is the value of the environment variable concatenated with the string.' So BLOCKSDS must be present in gcc's environment at link time; it is not baked into the specs.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/v1.24.0/sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile (byte-identical to master; scratchpad/verify/rom_arm9_Makefile_v1.24.0)**: Line 5: `export BLOCKSDS ?= /opt/blocksds/core` (NOT the Wonderful path, so BLOCKSDS must be exported by the spawner). Lines 8-9: `export WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN ?= /opt/wonderful`, `ARM_NONE_EABI_PATH ?= $(WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN)/toolchain/gcc-arm-none-eabi/bin/`; line 78-79 `PREFIX := $(ARM_NONE_EABI_PATH)arm-none-eabi-`, `CC := $(PREFIX)gcc`. Lines 14/35/36/38/55: NAME, SOURCEDIRS, INCLUDEDIRS, BINDIRS, LIBS are all `?=` (user assignment before `include` works). Line 56: `LIBDIRS += $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/libnds` (only libnds by default). Lines 128-132: `ifeq ($(SOURCES_CPP),) LIBS += -lc else LIBS += -lstdc++ -lc` (-lc is appended automatically). Line 124: `SPECS ?= $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/crts/ds_arm9.specs`, used in CFLAGS/ASFLAGS/LDFLAGS (lines 139-154). Lines 287-290: `$(BUILDDIR)/%.arm.c.o : %.arm.c` -> `$(CC) $(CFLAGS) -MMD -MP -marm -mlong-calls -c -o $@ $<` (and 260-263 for COMPDB=1; %.arm.cpp likewise). Line 120: `DEFINES += -D__NDS__ -D__BLOCKSDS__ -DARM9`. Lines 99/113-115: sources discovered with `$(shell find -L ...)` (needs POSIX find/mkdir/rm from an MSYS PATH).

Without LIBDIRS the build fails on `maxmod9.h` and `cannot find -lmm9`.
- **BlocksDS changelog (docs/content/docs/changelog.md at v1.24.0), Version 0.8 (2023-07-16)**: 'Automatically link with libc and libstdc++ rather than forcing users to do it explicitly.' and 'Support `*.arm.c` and `*.arm.cpp` filenames for compatibility with devkitARM-utilizing projects.' - so `-lc` in LIBS is redundant and the .arm.c rule is a stable, documented feature.
- **GNU make manual, 'How Patterns Match', https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/Pattern-Match.html; local `C:\msys64\usr\bin\make.exe --version` = GNU Make 4.4.1 (x86_64-pc-msys)**: 'make will choose the rule with the shortest stem (that is, the pattern that matches most specifically). If more than one pattern rule has the shortest stem, make will choose the first one found in the makefile.' For build/x/vm.arm.c.o both `%.c.o` (stem vm.arm) and `%.arm.c.o` (stem vm) match; the .arm.c rule wins. Object files are named `<file>.arm.c.o`, not `<file>.arm.o`.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/libnds/7fd8ccbe781ed48a06fe063a2dcbb69035fa97d3/include/nds/ndstypes.h (the libnds commit pinned by SDK tag v1.24.0, from the GitHub tree API) lines 63-66, 119**: CONFIRMS: under `#if defined(ARM9)`: `#define ITCM_CODE __attribute__((__section__(".itcm.text." __FILE__ "." LIBNDS_STRINGIFY(__LINE__)), __long_call__))`; also `ITCM_FUNC(x)` (no long_call) and `#define ARM_CODE __attribute__((__target__("arm")))`. ITCM_CODE requires -DARM9 (supplied by Makefile line 120 and specs `*cpp:` line 8). ITCM_CODE does NOT switch to ARM mode; a `.arm.c` file or ARM_CODE is needed for that.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/master/sys/crts/ds_arm9.ld lines 97-101, 258-271; ds_arm9.mem lines 3-7**: CONFIRMS placement: `.text` excludes `*.itcm*` files; `.itcm : { ... *(.itcm) *(.itcm.*) *.itcm*(.text .stub .text.*) ... } >itcm AT>ewram :itcm`; MEMORY `itcm : ORIGIN = 0x01000000, LENGTH = 32K`, `dtcm : ORIGIN = 0x02ff4000, LENGTH = 16K`. The `.itcm.text.<file>.<line>` sections from ITCM_CODE match `*(.itcm.*)`; crt0 copies them from the EWRAM load address.

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** 1) Spawn spec for buildRuntime(): spawn('C:\\msys64\\usr\\bin\\bash.exe', ['-lc', 'make -j16'], { cwd: <runtime dir>, windowsHide: true, env: { ...process.env, MSYSTEM: 'UCRT64', MSYS2_PATH_TYPE: 'inherit', CHERE_INVOKING: '1', BLOCKSDS: '/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/core', BLOCKSDSEXT: '/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/external', WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN: '/opt/wonderful', [existing PATH key, case-insensitive]: 'C:\\msys64\\opt\\wonderful\\bin;' + <existing PATH> } }). Without CHERE_INVOKING=1 (or an explicit `cd <dir> && make` in the -c string) the login shell cds to $HOME before running make. 2) runtime/Makefile: NAME := dsdude_runtime; SOURCEDIRS := core/src platform/ds/src; INCLUDEDIRS := core/include gen; BINDIRS := data; LIBS := -lmm9 -lnds9 (the default Makefile appends -lc itself; an extra -lc is harmless); LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod (the default Makefile appends $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/libnds via +=); include $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile. Without the LIBDIRS line the link fails with 'cannot find -lmm9' and <maxmod9.h> is not found. 3) Precise mechanism statement: ds_arm9.specs resolves ds_arm9.mem, ds_arm9.ld and ds_arm9_crt0.o through %:getenv(BLOCKSDS ...) (picolibc.specs comes from the toolchain's own arm-none-eabi/lib). The native arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe only sees a usable path because the MSYS2 runtime rewrites BLOCKSDS and every /opt/... argument to C:/msys64/opt/... when make (an msys process) spawns it; so gcc must be launched via bash/make, or, if ever called directly from Node, with BLOCKSDS=C:/msys64/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/core and Windows-form -specs=/-I/-L paths. Compiler path C:\\msys64\\opt\\wonderful\\toolchain\\gcc-arm-none-eabi\\bin\\arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe (GCC 16.2.0) and the *.arm.c -> -marm -mlong-calls rule and ITCM_CODE placement are confirmed as stated.

**Impact on the plan.** WS1 packages/toolchain buildRuntime() and contracts/toolchain-api.md (bash -lc only for make): add CHERE_INVOKING=1 to the replicated wonderful_shell.cmd environment (also in scripts/install-toolchain.ps1 for the smoke builds of examples/graphics_2d/bg_regular_nitrofs and examples/maxmod/nitrofs, and in `dsdude doctor`); otherwise M0/phase0 'samples/hello builds' fails with 'No targets specified and no makefile found' because make runs in /home/zache. WS3 runtime/Makefile deliverable must add LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod or the selftest ROM cannot link maxmod (mmInitDefault/mmEffect). If any workstream ever bypasses bash and calls arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe/ndstool.exe directly (tools-pack, fetch-vendor.ps1, direct compile of user C modules), BLOCKSDS and all SDK paths must be given in Windows form since no MSYS2 conversion happens then. Nothing else in the architecture changes.

**Residual risk.** The Wonderful toolchain is not installed here (C:\\msys64\\opt is empty), so the real arm-none-eabi-gcc.exe 16.2.0 / ld / libnds9 / libmm9 were never executed: untested are (a) that the native GCC finds its runtime DLLs from /opt/wonderful/bin under a Node-spawned bash (depends on MSYS2_PATH_TYPE=inherit surviving), (b) %include <picolibc.specs> lookup from the relocated prefix, (c) the actual link of -lmm9 -lnds9 with --gc-sections, and (d) that the VM's ITCM function fits in the 32 KB ITCM alongside libnds's own ITCM code and lands in ARM mode. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (about 5 minutes after `wf-pacman -S blocksds-toolchain`): from PowerShell/Node with the env above including CHERE_INVOKING=1, spawn `bash -lc \"make VERBOSE=1\"` with cwd=C:\\msys64\\opt\\wonderful\\thirdparty\\blocksds\\core\\examples\\maxmod\\nitrofs (exercises -lmm9 and NitroFS) and check that the printed link line contains -T C:/msys64/opt/.../ds_arm9.ld via `make VERBOSE=1 LDFLAGS=-Wl,--verbose | grep ds_arm9`; then build a 3-file test with vm.arm.c containing an ITCM_CODE function and confirm with `arm-none-eabi-nm build/x.elf | grep vm_run` (address in 0x01000000-0x01007FFF) and `arm-none-eabi-objdump -d --section=.itcm` (32-bit ARM encodings, not Thumb); finally run `bash -lc \"make\"` once WITHOUT CHERE_INVOKING to see the failure mode and document it in `dsdude doctor`.

## 3. ndstool ELF packing, NitroFS offsets and magic

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** BlocksDS's ndstool accepts ELF inputs for -9 and -7 and multiple -d NitroFS directories, e.g. `ndstool -c game.nds -9 arm9.elf -7 <BlocksDS>/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf -b icon.png "Title;Subtitle;Author" -d nitrofs_dir`; with its default header size the FNT and FAT land at offsets >= 0x8000 and the ROM carries the `NitroFS!` magic; libnds `nitroFSInit(NULL)` then succeeds in both melonDS and DeSmuME 0.9.13 without DLDI or argv; `ndstool -i` prints the FNT/FAT offsets so a packer can verify them; libnds returns ENODEV / fails if FNT/FAT are below 0x8000. Re-packing a prebuilt arm9.elf with different NitroFS contents needs no compiler and takes milliseconds.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** BlocksDS's ndstool (v1.24.0; behaviour present since BlocksDS 1.14.2) accepts ELF inputs for -9 and -7 (detected by .elf extension or the ELF magic) and one or more NitroFS root folders via `-d dir1 dir2 ...` or repeated `-d`, all merged at the filesystem root; e.g. `ndstool -c game.nds -9 arm9.elf -7 <BlocksDS>/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf -b icon.png "Title;Subtitle;Author" -d nitrofs_dir`. The header defaults to 0x4000 when both inputs are ELF (0x200 otherwise), but the FNT/FAT position does not depend on it: ndstool always places the ARM7 binary at >= 0x8000 (`arm7_min`), and FNT and FAT follow it, so `ndstool -c` can never put FNT/FAT below 0x8000 (a 1 KB ARM9 with `-h 0x200` still yields FNT 0xA000 / FAT 0xA400). The 8-byte `NitroFS!` magic is written immediately after the FAT only when the filesystem contains at least one file; an empty NitroFS has FAT size 0 and no magic and `nitroFSInit` fails with ENODEV. libnds `nitroFSInit(NULL)` is documented to work on emulators via official card-read commands without DLDI patching or an argv-passing loader (DeSmuME 0.9.13 always injects argv[0]="fat:/<rom>", libnds tries and discards it, then falls back to the card-command path and verifies the magic; melonDS injects argv/DLDI only when its SD emulation is enabled). `nitroFSInit` returns false with errno=ENODEV when the FAT offset is < 0x8000, the FAT size is 0, or the magic cannot be read; an FNT offset < 0x8000 does NOT fail `nitroFSInit` (it returns true), but every later path-based open/opendir/stat/chdir then fails with ENODEV. `ndstool -i` prints tab-separated lines `0x40 File name table offset 0x...`, `0x44 File name table size`, `0x48 FAT offset`, `0x4C FAT size` that a packer can parse; it does not check the magic, so verifyRom() must read the 8 bytes at fat_offset+fat_size itself. Re-packing a prebuilt arm9.elf with different NitroFS contents involves no compiler and takes tens of milliseconds (measured 61-83 ms for a 2.4 MB ROM with 62 files; scales with NitroFS size). The packer's ndstool must be from BlocksDS >= 1.14.2 (same or newer than the libnds used to build arm9.elf) or the magic is absent and NitroFS fails on emulators.

**Impact on the plan.** (1) Risk item 2 and the verifyRom() spec are aimed at a failure ndstool cannot produce: FNT/FAT < 0x8000 is impossible from `ndstool -c`, so the check is only a sanity assertion; the real failure modes to guard are an empty build/nitrofs folder (FAT size 0, no magic), a runtime ELF built against a libnds newer than the tools-pack ndstool (< 1.14.2 writes no magic), and a build/nitrofs folder that is missing files. verifyRom() should assert fat_size > 0, read 8 bytes at fat_offset+fat_size == 'NitroFS!', and pin the ndstool/libnds BlocksDS version together in tools-pack.json. (2) The Definition-of-done item "a deliberately tiny ARM9 triggers E6xx" (plan line 280 / meta line 168) cannot be satisfied by packing a tiny ELF; the test fixture must be a hand-corrupted ROM (hex-patch the FAT offset/size or magic, or pack with an empty -d folder). (3) Plan line 428's "fail silently" is inaccurate: nitroFSInit sets errno; the runtime's DSD|ERR path should print strerror(errno) after a failed nitroFSInit. (4) The `-i` parser must handle the tab-separated `0x%X` format and must not rely on -i to detect the magic. (5) The ~100 ms pack budget (line 48) is realistic for small games but grows linearly with NitroFS size. (6) The tools pack will need libiconv-2.dll (and mingw runtime DLLs) beside ndstool.exe, consistent with existing risk item 8.

**Residual risk.** Actually booting a ROM packed from a real BlocksDS 1.24.0 arm9.elf + arm7_maxmod.elf in melonDS 1.1 and DeSmuME 0.9.13 and observing nitroFSInit(NULL) return true and a file read succeed (no emulator was launched here); confirming that fatInitDefault() on DeSmuME with no DLDI returns quickly rather than hanging; the exact wall-clock of the Wonderful-built ndstool.exe and its DLL dependencies (ldd) on this machine; and that the installed BlocksDS 1.24.0 ndstool/libnds pair both carry the 1.14.2+ magic code (Phase 0 spike: build examples/filesystem/nitrofs, run `ndstool -i`, hexdump the 8 bytes after the FAT, boot in both emulators).

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **blocksds/ndstool source/ndscreate.cpp lines 91-112, 391-393, 602, 681 (GitHub master, fetched 2026-09-24)**: `HasElfExtension()` (case-insensitive .elf) OR `HasElfHeader()` ("\x7fELF" magic) decide `is_arm9_elf`/`is_arm7_elf`; ELF inputs go through `CopyFromElf()` (elf.cpp: requires EM_ARM and contiguous PT_LOAD p_paddr). Verified live: synthetic ARM ELFs packed successfully by a scratch build of ndstool.
- **blocksds/ndstool source/ndstool.cpp lines 128, 296-312**: Usage table: `-d directory1 <directory2> ...` "All directories are combined in the root of the filesystem"; parser loops over arguments until the next `-` option (MAX_FILEROOTDIRS). Live test: both `-d nitro1 nitro2` and `-d nitro1 -d nitro2` merged 62 files into one root (`ndstool -l` lists /big.bin, /hello.txt, /gfx/..., /scripts/...).
- **blocksds/sdk sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile lines 53, 187-192, 208-211; sys/arm7/main_core/Makefile lines 28-57, 103**: `ARM7ELF ?= $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf`; ROM rule `ndstool -c $@ -7 $(ARM7ELF) -9 $(ELF) -b $(GAME_ICON) "$(GAME_FULL_TITLE)" $(NDSTOOL_ARGS)` with `NDSTOOL_ARGS := -d $(NITROFSDIR)`; main_core Makefile builds NAME=maxmod and installs `arm7_*.elf`, confirming the arm7_maxmod.elf file name/path in the claim's command.
- **blocksds/ndstool source/ndscreate.cpp line 401 and lines 19-26, 663**: `headersize = headerfilename_or_size ? strtoul(...) : (is_both_elf ? 0x4000 : 0x200)` — default 0x4000 only when BOTH inputs are ELF. BUT the >= 0x8000 property does not come from the header size: `static const long arm7_min = 0x8000;` and `header.arm7_rom_offset = std::max((position + arm7_align) &~ arm7_align, arm7_min);` force the ARM7 binary to >= 0x8000, and FNT/FAT are laid out after it (lines 736-751). Upstream commit f7568827 (2018-04-24) "Ensure ARM7 ROM offset is always above 0x8000".
- **Live run of scratch-built ndstool (source tarball blocksds/ndstool master, built with MSYS2 ucrt64 g++ 15.2 into the scratchpad; not installed)**: 0x400-byte ARM9 ELF + 0x2000-byte ARM7 ELF, default header: `ndstool -i` prints `0x20 ARM9 ROM offset 0x4000`, `0x30 ARM7 ROM offset 0x8000`, `0x40 File name table offset 0xA000`, `0x48 FAT offset 0xA400`, `0x4C FAT size 0x1F0`, `0x84 ROM header size 0x4000`. Same tiny ARM9 with `-h 0x200`: ARM9 at 0x200 but ARM7 still 0x8000, FNT 0xA000, FAT 0xA400. `ndstool -c` therefore cannot place FNT/FAT below 0x8000 regardless of -h or ARM9 size.
- **blocksds/ndstool source/ndscreate.cpp lines 753-775; hexdump of test ROMs**: Right after the FAT (`fat_end_offset = fat_offset + fat_size`) ndstool writes the 8 bytes 'N','i','t','r','o','F','S','!' — but ONLY `if (file_count > 0)`. Verified: t1.nds bytes at 0xA5F0 == b'NitroFS!'; a ROM built with no -d has FAT size 0 and no magic (bytes 01 00 db 0a ...). Added in commit a83230b5 (2025-09-16) = BlocksDS 1.14.2 (2025-09-17) per docs/content/docs/changelog.md lines 1569-1600, which also says libnds's nitroFSInit now reads that magic.
- **blocksds/ndstool source/header.cpp lines 182, 235-238, 654-666**: `ShowInfo()` (the `-i` action, ndstool.cpp line 549) calls `ShowHeaderInfo()` which prints `printf("0x40\t%-25s\t0x%X\n", "File name table offset", ...)`, `0x44 File name table size`, `0x48 FAT offset`, `0x4C FAT size`. Confirmed live; format is tab-separated `0x40<TAB>File name table offset   <TAB>0xA000`. `-i` does NOT check the magic; a packer must read 8 bytes at fat_offset+fat_size itself.
- **blocksds/libnds source/arm9/libc/nitrofs_device.c lines 815-1028 (nitroFSInit)**: Lines 1001-1016: `if (fatOffset >= 0x8000 && fatSize > 0) {...} else { errno = ENODEV; return false; }`. Lines 1019-1022: `if (filenameOffset >= 0x8000 && filenameSize > 0) fnt_offset = filenameOffset;` with comment "Allow opening files by direct ID even without an FNT" — an FNT below 0x8000 does NOT make nitroFSInit fail; instead nitrofs_open/opendir/chdir/stat/fat_get_attr later return ENODEV when `fnt_offset == 0` (lines 256-259, 544-548, 672-676, 718-722, 756-760). Lines 968-993: when no file could be opened, libnds reads 8 bytes at `__NDSHeader->fatOffset + fatSize` via card commands and sets `errno = ENODEV; return false` if they are not 'NitroFS!'. Failures are not silent: errno is set (the SDK example does `perror("nitroFSInit()")`).
- **blocksds/libnds nitrofs_device.c lines 842-893; TASEmulators/desmume tag release_0_9_13 desmume/src/NDSSystem.cpp lines 2591-2612; desmume/src/addons/slot1comp_rom.cpp lines 67-82**: nitroFSInit(NULL) uses argv[0] if `argvMagic == ARGV_MAGIC`; DeSmuME 0.9.13 ALWAYS writes argv[0] = "fat:/" + RomName at 0x02FFFE70 (magic 0x5f617267). libnds then calls fatInitDefault(); if the file cannot be opened and the path came from argv it deliberately falls through (comment: "we may be running on DeSmuME, which always sets argv[0] but doesn't have automatic FAT support", citing that DeSmuME code) to Slot-2 detection and then the card-command magic check. DeSmuME's slot1 ROM component serves standard reads at any address >= 0x8000 (`if (_address < 0x8000) _address = 0x8000 + (_address & 0x1FF)` then `gameInfo.readROM(_address)`), so the magic at >= 0x8000 is readable. So the ROM needs neither DLDI nor an argv-passing loader on DeSmuME.
- **melonDS-emu/melonDS src/NDSCart/CartHomebrew.cpp lines 47-83; src/NDS.cpp lines 430-486**: melonDS routes ROMs whose header `IsHomebrew()` to CartHomebrew; it injects argv[0] = "fat:/<rom>" and DLDI-patches the ROM only `if (SD)` (DLDI SD emulation enabled), in which case it also injects the ROM into the emulated SD so the argv path works; otherwise no argv and no DLDI, and libnds uses card commands. Either way no DLDI/argv is required from the packer.
- **blocksds/sdk docs/content/docs/guides/filesystem.md lines 98-102, 130-136, 152-156, 297-355; docs/content/docs/guides/faq (rendered)**: "Emulators: They support official Nintendo cartridge read commands. NitroFS can use the same commands to easily access the files the same way as any official game." Example uses `nitroFSInit(NULL)` and notes it calls fatInitDefault() internally if required. Warning: "An empty NitroFS filesystem will cause nitroFSInit() to fail." The 'Running on emulators' section's `make dldipatch` / `--slot1=R4 --slot1-fat-dir` advice for DeSmuME concerns FAT (SD-card) access, not NitroFS. FAQ: "The recommended emulators are melonDS, DeSmuMe and no$gba." Actual emulator boot of a packed ROM was NOT executed in this check (rule: no emulator launches).
- **Live timing on this machine (Python subprocess, scratch-built ndstool -O2, ~2.3 MB NitroFS, 62 files, PNG icon)**: 10 repacks: min 61 ms, median 69 ms, max 83 ms; `ndstool -i`: ~23 ms. No compiler involved (CopyFromElf only reads ELF program headers). Time scales with NitroFS byte size since all file bytes are copied. Note: linking required `-liconv` on MinGW (utf16.cpp), so the Wonderful-built ndstool.exe likely depends on libiconv-2.dll (relevant to the plan's tools-pack DLL risk, not to this claim).

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** BlocksDS ndstool 1.24.0 (`ndstool.exe`, GPL-3, needs libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll and libiconv-2.dll from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin on PATH or beside it; without them it exits 0xC0000135 with no output) accepts ELF or raw binaries for -9/-7 (detected by .elf extension or ELF magic) and up to 32 `-d` directories merged at the root; `-7` must be explicit unless BLOCKSDS is set. ndstool always places the ARM7 at >= 0x8000 (`arm7_min`), so the FNT and FAT are >= 0x8000 for any header size and any ARM9 size (verified: 0x900-byte ARM9 with `-h 0x200` still gives ARM7 0x8000, FNT 0xB200); a tiny ARM9 cannot produce a sub-0x8000 FAT. With two ELF inputs the header is 0x4000, unit code 0x02, the BlocksDS ARM9 (0x800 zero secure stub, entry = RAM+0x800) is padded to 0x4000, and the 8-byte `NitroFS!` magic is written immediately after the FAT only if at least one file is packed; an empty NitroFS directory yields FAT size 0 and no magic (nitroFSInit -> ENODEV), so always pack at least one file. libnds nitroFSInit(NULL) returns false with errno ENODEV only when the magic cannot be read via card commands or when fatOffset < 0x8000 || fatSize == 0; an FNT below 0x8000 or of size 0 does not fail init, it silently disables path lookups. `ndstool -i` prints '0x40 File name table offset', '0x44 File name table size', '0x48 FAT offset', '0x4C FAT size' (tab-separated) but not the magic; verifyRom() must parse those four, require FNT/FAT >= 0x8000 and FAT size > 0, and read the 8 bytes at fatOffset+fatSize itself; it must key on ndstool's exit code because a failed run leaves a truncated .nds. Icon: `-b icon.png` must be exactly 32x32 with <= 15 opaque colours plus a transparent one (ndstool does not quantise); the IDE's asset step must quantise the icon first. NitroFS names must be ASCII 0x20-0x7E and <= 127 chars (ndstool does not validate; longer names corrupt the FNT) and full build paths must stay < 260 chars. Re-packing a prebuilt arm9.elf needs no compiler: ~40 ms for a ~160 KB ROM (Node spawn ~80 ms), ~250 ms for a 4 MB / 200-file NitroFS, with multi-second cold-cache outliers. Emulator behaviour (nitroFSInit via card commands, no DLDI/argv needed, in melonDS 1.1 and DeSmuME 0.9.13) is supported by libnds/DeSmuME/melonDS source and BlocksDS release notes but was not executed on this machine and remains a Phase-0 check.

**Impact on the plan.** (1) Replace the DoD item 'a deliberately tiny ARM9 triggers E6xx' with 'an empty NitroFS directory (or a ROM with the magic zeroed by the test) triggers E6xx', and drop 'the runtime exceeds 16 KB anyway' as the reason FNT/FAT are >= 0x8000. (2) verifyRom() must read the magic bytes and FAT size itself (ndstool -i does not report the magic) and must check ndstool's exit code and delete partial output. (3) packRom() must guarantee at least one file in build/nitrofs (game.dsdb always present) and reject/rename names that are non-ASCII or > 127 chars, and warn when build paths approach 260 chars (OneDrive project folder). (4) The Play-time spawn of ndstool needs PATH (or the tools pack directory) to contain libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll and libiconv-2.dll; otherwise Play fails with exit 3221225781 and an empty log; the tools-pack/ldd step moves from 'packaging' to a Phase-0 requirement. (5) The asset pipeline must quantise the project icon to 32x32, <= 15 opaque colours + transparency before ndstool sees it (or ship a fixed default icon), otherwise packing fails. (6) Play-time budget: keep '~100 ms' only for small projects; allow ~300 ms for multi-MB asset sets and one cold-cache outlier after a fresh asset write.

**Residual risk.** Actual boot and nitroFSInit success in DeSmuME 0.9.13 and melonDS 1.1 was not executed (no libnds-built ARM9 is available before the BlocksDS install, and emulators are GUI). Source reading of libnds, DeSmuME and melonDS says it works, and BlocksDS maintainers test DeSmuME, but a DeSmuME-specific regression (e.g. slot-1 'Retail Auto' behaviour with unit code 0x02 DSi-enhanced headers, or the argv 'fat:/' path when the user has --slot1 R4 with a fat dir configured so open(argv[0]) partially succeeds) can only be excluded by running it. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (about 15 minutes after the BlocksDS install): build $BLOCKSDS/examples/filesystem/nitrofs (or samples/hello with nitroFSInit + fopen('nitro:/hello.txt') printing DSD|LOG), run `ndstool -i` and the magic read on the output, then launch it in melonDS and DeSmuME (default settings, and DeSmuME once more with --slot1 R4 --slot1-fat-dir set) and confirm the file contents print; also re-pack the same arm9.elf with a different nitrofs dir from a Node spawn with PATH set and confirm the new file contents appear without recompiling.

(is_both_elf ? 0x4000 : 0x200)`, lines 609-613 (pad ARM9 to 0x4000 only when header>0x200 && entry-ram==0x800 && size<0x4000), lines 753-775 (magic written only `if (file_count > 0)`); local case C**: The >=0x8000 property does not come from the default header size: ndstool always places the ARM7 at >= 0x8000, so FNT/FAT are always >= 0x8000 + ARM7 size regardless of header size or ARM9 size. The FNT check `filenameOffset >= 0x8000 && filenameSize > 0` only decides whether fnt_offset is set; otherwise nitroFSInit still returns true and only ID-based opens work. With the DLL dir on PATH it works. The plan's tools-pack/ldd mitigation (risk 8) is necessary at Play time too, not only for packaging.

It does not quantise: a 17-colour PNG fails ('too many colors 17 > 16'), a 16-opaque-colour PNG fails ('Could not adjust image palette for transparency: too many colors'). On any such failure rc=1 and a truncated .nds (66584 bytes) is left on disk, so the packer must key on the exit code, not file existence.

My crafted ELF reproduced ndstool's own classification '(OK, multiboot)'.

## 4. VM speed: >= 35K simple ops per frame

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** A computed-goto, ARM-mode, ITCM-resident bytecode dispatch loop on the ARM946E-S at 67 MHz can achieve >= 35,000 simple VM ops per 60 Hz frame (1,120,380 cycles/frame), i.e. <= ~16 cycles/op average when 50% of the frame is budgeted to scripts, or at least the plan's estimate of 19-27 cycles per simple op (~20-29K ops/frame at 50% budget) is realistic; comparable data points: Lua on DS, GB Studio's GBVM, other interpreters on ARM9/ARMv5TE without FPU. ITCM is 32 KB and DTCM 16 KB on the DS ARM9.

### Primary-source check: partially true (medium confidence)

**Correction.** Replace the plan's section 2.3 rationale sentence and the M1 gate wording with: "The ARM946E-S at 67.03 MHz has 1,120,380 cycles per 59.83 Hz frame (355 x 263 dots x 6 bus cycles x 2; GBATEK). A switch-dispatch VM over UNTYPED Q20.12 registers was hand-priced from an mwccarm disassembly with the ARM9E-S cycle table (DDI 0165B Table 8-2: B/BX 3, LDR->PC 5, data-op->PC 3, load-use interlock 1) at ~27 cycles per simple op; computed-goto ARM-mode dispatch in ITCM removes two pipeline refills (~19). Neither figure includes the C2 tagged-cell type check (~4-5 cycles) or the per-op watchdog decrement (~2), so plan on ~23-27 cycles per typed simple op: ~41-48K ops per full frame, ~21-24K ops in a 50% script budget (about 250-500 instances at 30-80 ops per Step, or 100-150 with headroom). These are estimates, not measurements." M1 gate: "microbenchmark must show <= 32 cycles per typed simple op averaged over ADD/CMPJ/GETSLOT/MOV (equivalently >= 35K such ops per FULL 1,120,380-cycle frame, i.e. >= 17.5K in the 50% budget), cycle-counted with the hardware timer. melonDS does not model load-use interlocks, ITCM contention or the caches accurately (melonDS issue #2408), so require <= 27 cycles/op on melonDS as the gate and re-measure on hardware or no$gba before M4." Also delete the phrase "measured ~27 cycles" (it was priced from a disassembly, not measured) and drop "Lua on DS / GBVM" as comparable data points (no quantitative numbers exist; GBVM's constants 16 contexts / 64-word stacks / 768-word heap / quantum 16 are confirmed but say nothing about ARM9 cycles/op).

**Impact on the plan.** If the gate is read as 35K ops within the 50% budget (16 cycles/op), it is stricter than the plan's own best estimate and will fail at M1 even with a correct implementation, triggering the 'spend a week on dispatch' fallback for no reason; if read as 35K per full frame (32 cycles/op) it passes even with switch dispatch and does not test the computed-goto/ITCM design at all. The instance-count promise (250-700 scripted instances) should be lowered to ~250-500 (100-150 with headroom) because typed cells and the watchdog cost ~6-7 cycles per arithmetic op that the estimate omitted. The melonDS-only measurement can under-read hardware by ~10-20%, so a hardware (or no$gba) cross-check needs to be added to the M1/M4 checklist, and WS3's DS timer harness should report cycles per op class, not one aggregate number. C2 could also be amended to let the compiler emit statically typed ADDI/ADDF variants when the checker knows both operand types, recovering most of the tag-check cost.

**Residual risk.** Only the M1 microbenchmark can settle: (1) what GCC 14/15 at -O2 -marm actually emits for the computed-goto handlers (whether it keeps one ldr-pc/bx per handler or tail-merges into a shared dispatch, adding a 3-cycle b per op; register pressure with 8-byte cells and A/B/C decode on ARMv5TE, which lacks UBFX); (2) the real interlock and D-cache-miss cost of fetching bytecode from main RAM (GBATEK: 46 ARM9 cycles per 32-byte line miss) versus the ITCM-resident loop; (3) the melonDS-vs-hardware delta for this loop; (4) whether the VM plus the hottest builtins fit in the ITCM left after libnds's vectors, and the 4 KB VM register stack fits DTCM alongside the SVC/IRQ/user stacks. No ARM cross-compiler is present on this machine (mwccarm has no labels-as-values; the mwccarm\\devkitARM folder has no bin), so none of this could be checked without the BlocksDS install.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **GBATEK, DS Video Stuff (problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-video-stuff.htm)**: "256 dots visible, 99 dots blanking, 355 dots total"; "192 lines visible, 71 lines blanking, 263 lines total"; dot clock "5.585664 MHz (=33.513982 MHz / 6)"; "59.8261 Hz". 355 x 263 x 6 = 560,190 bus cycles/frame; x2 = 1,120,380 ARM9 cycles/frame. Plan's frame budget CONFIRMED.
- **GBATEK, DS Technical Data + DS Memory Timings (gbatek-ds-technical-data.htm, gbatek-ds-memory-timings.htm, downloaded to scratchpad/fc/gbatek-timings.htm)**: "60KB TCM/Cache (TCM: 16K Data, 32K Code) (Cache: 4K Data, 8K Code)"; "NDS9 clock = 66MHz (internally twice bus clock; for cache/tcm)"; NDS9/CODE and NDS9/DATA rows "0.5 0.5 0.5 0.5 32 TCM, Cache_Hit" (units are 33 MHz cycles, i.e. 1 ARM9 cycle); "23 23 23 - 16 Cache_Miss (Main RAM)"; NDS9/DATA Main RAM "10 2 9 1"; "data access to ITCM can't [take NULL cycles]". ITCM 32 KB / DTCM 16 KB CONFIRMED.
- **BlocksDS sdk sys/crts/ds_arm9.mem (raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/master/sys/crts/ds_arm9.mem) and ds_arm9.ld**: MEMORY { ewram : ORIGIN = 0x02000000, LENGTH = 4M - 512k; dtcm : ORIGIN = 0x02ff4000, LENGTH = 16K; itcm : ORIGIN = 0x01000000, LENGTH = 32K }. .itcm section also holds KEEP(*(.vectors)); DTCM also hosts IRQ flags/vector and SVC/IRQ/user stacks (__sp_svc, __sp_irq, __usr_stack), so the VM does not get all 32K/16K.
- **BlocksDS libnds include/nds/ndstypes.h and sdk sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile**: #define ITCM_CODE __attribute__((__section__(".itcm.text." __FILE__ "." LIBNDS_STRINGIFY(__LINE__)), __long_call__)); rule "$(BUILDDIR)/%.arm.c.o : %.arm.c ... $(CC) $(CFLAGS) -MMD -MP -marm -mlong-calls -c"; default ARCH := -mthumb -mcpu=arm946e-s+nofp. The plan's ARM-mode/ITCM mechanism is CONFIRMED.
- **ARM9E-S TRM, ARM DDI 0165B (Microchip mirror ww1.microchip.com/downloads/en/DeviceDoc/doc6178.pdf, saved as scratchpad/fc/arm9es-trm.pdf), Table 8-2 and sections 8.3, 8.5, 8.12**: Table 8-2: Data Op 1 cycle (normal), 2 with register-controlled shift, 3 with PC destination; LDR 1 normal, 2 if next instruction uses the loaded word ("1 cycle load-use interlock"), 3 for byte/halfword/unaligned (2-cycle interlock), 5 "PC is destination register"; STR 1; SMULL/UMULL 3 (4 if RdHi used next); B/BL/BX/BLX 3 (2S+1N). 8.3: "Any ARM or Thumb branch ... takes three cycles" (pipeline refill; no branch prediction exists - the word 'prediction' does not appear in the TRM). 8.12: "A load to the PC occupies the Execute stage for five cycles." No extra cycle is listed for an immediate-shifted register offset on LDR/STR, so the research's '+1 for a shifted offset' overcounts by ~3 cycles per op.
- **scratchpad/wf1/research-hardware.md section 2 (origin of the plan's numbers)**: "I compiled a 10-opcode register VM (switch dispatch, 20.12 registers) with mwccarm -proc arm946e -O4,s ... Pricing the OP_ADD path with the ARM9E-S cycle table ... dispatch 16 cycles + body 11 cycles = ~27 cycles per simple op"; computed goto "roughly 11 cycles of dispatch, ~19 per ADD". This is a hand-priced disassembly of an UNTYPED fixed-point register VM, not a timer measurement, and not on GCC/ITCM/DS. The plan's wording "A switch-dispatch VM measured ~27 cycles" is therefore wrong.
- **scratchpad/wf1/unified-plan.md lines 50, 78, 148, 248 (contract C2)**: C2 specifies "8-byte cells {u32 tag; s32 payload} with tags UNDEF/INT/REAL/BOOL/...", a single ADD opcode with "Lua-5.3-style promotion (int op int stays int; anything fractional becomes fixed)" and "a per-frame instruction watchdog". A typed ADD therefore needs two tag loads + orr/cmp/bne (~4-5 cycles) and a subs/beq watchdog (~2 cycles) that the 19/27 estimates do not include. Re-priced with Table 8-2: computed-goto dispatch (ldr opcode 1, and 1(+1 interlock), ldr pc 5) ~7-8 cycles; typed ADD body (A/B/C decode 3-4, 4 loads 4-6, tag check 3, add+2 stores 3, watchdog 2) ~16-18 cycles; total ~23-27 cycles per typed simple op => ~41-48K ops per full frame, ~21-24K ops at a 50% budget.
- **Arithmetic check against the plan's own figures**: 35,000 ops in 560,190 cycles (50% budget) = 16.0 cycles/op, below the plan's best-case 19; 35,000 ops in 1,120,380 cycles (100%) = 32.0 cycles/op, which even the 27-cycle switch estimate passes (41.5K). 1,120,380/19 = 58,967 (29,484 at 50%); 1,120,380/27 = 41,495 (20,748 at 50%) - the '20-29K at 50%' sentence is arithmetically correct, but the '>= 35K per frame' gate does not state which budget it uses and is inconsistent with the 50% sentence next to it.
- **melonDS issue #2408 "Known Timing Bugs" (github.com/melonDS-emu/melonDS/issues/2408, opened 2025-08-17, open) and issue #776; melonDS src/CP15.cpp on master; 1.0 and 1.0rc release notes**: #2408 lists for the ARM9: "ICache is not modeled accurately", "DCache is not modeled accurately", "Cache streaming is unimplemented", "Interlocks are unimplemented", "Write buffer is unimplemented", "ITCM data reads ... should cause contention with single cycle instruction accesses". CP15.cpp does contain ICacheLookup with kCodeCacheTiming = 3 and DataCycles = 1 for ITCM/DTCM, but no DCache lookup. 1.0 / 1.0rc release notes mention no ARM9 cache or timing work. So a hardware-timer measurement on melonDS will omit the load-use interlocks that an interpreter loop hits on nearly every op (~2-4 of ~25 cycles, i.e. it under-reads hardware by roughly 10-20%).
- **GBVM include/vm.h (raw.githubusercontent.com/chrismaltby/gbvm/master/include/vm.h)**: "#define VM_MAX_CONTEXTS 16", "#define VM_CONTEXT_STACK_SIZE 64", "#define VM_HEAP_SIZE 768", "#define INSTRUCTIONS_PER_QUANT 0x10". Constants in the research are CONFIRMED, but GBVM publishes no cycles-per-op figure; it is a fairness quantum on an SM83, not a data point for ARM9 dispatch cost.
- **MicroLua DS wiki/SourceForge/GitHub (asiekierka/micro-lua-ds), BPCore-Engine README (github.com/evanbowman/BPCore-Engine)**: No ops/s or cycles/op numbers are published for MicroLua or DSLua (web search of official pages found none; the plan itself says "Lua/Python on DS have no performance numbers"). BPCore (Lua 5.3 on the 16.78 MHz GBA) says only "because Lua is resource-intensive for the GBA, this library is only suitable for making relatively small minigames" and caps entities at 128. The 'comparable data points' part of the claim is therefore NOT CONFIRMED: no quantitative comparable exists.

</details>

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Replace the estimate and the gate with: "A GCC-compiled computed-goto handler for a tag-checked 3-address op on 8-byte cells costs ~30 ARM9 cycles with code in ITCM and data in TCM (22-24 instructions by Table B3.9), ~36 when the bytecode is straight-line code streamed from main RAM (+46-cycle line fill per 8 words); a type-specialized untagged op costs ~21; a hand-written ldrd/strd handler ~24 (tagged) / ~18 (untagged). Expect 31-37K tag-checked simple ops per frame at 100% CPU, i.e. ~16-19K at the 50% script budget (not 20-29K); typed opcodes raise that to ~27K at 50%. M1 gate: >= 35,000 ops/frame at 100% CPU, measured with the cascaded hardware timer (ticks x2 = ARM9 cycles) on melonDS in interpreter mode (JIT off), on a defined op mix (e.g. 40% tag-checked ADD/SUB/MUL, 30% MOV/LOADI/ADDI, 20% CMPJ/JMP, 10% GETSLOT/SETSLOT) with the bytecode in main RAM as a >= 4 KB straight-line block, not a cache-resident loop; because melonDS master does not model caches, interlocks, or scaled-offset penalties (it reports ~24-26 cycles for a handler that costs 30-36 on hardware), apply a 1.25x derating (i.e. require >= 44K on melonDS) or confirm once on real hardware via flashcart. vm.arm.c must be compiled with -fno-gcse -fno-crossjumping (BlocksDS's default .arm.c rule only adds -marm -mlong-calls) and its dispatch table must be DTCM_DATA_VAR, not .rodata."

**Impact on the plan.** The product conclusion (hundreds of scripted instances at 60 fps) survives: at 30-36 cycles/op the 50% script budget still yields ~16-19K ops/frame, i.e. roughly 200-600 instances with 30-80-op Step events instead of 250-700. What changes: (1) the M1 gate needs an explicit CPU share, op mix, bytecode placement and emulator mode, otherwise a tight LOADI/JMP loop on melonDS passes at 2x the real number; (2) '19 cycles per simple op' must be reassigned to type-specialized opcodes, which should move from optional to a WS2 deliverable (typed ADD/SUB/MUL/CMPJ variants emitted when the compiler proves int/fixed operands; contract C2 gains those opcodes), otherwise the 35K/100% gate is marginal (~31K) with a straight-line bytecode stream; (3) WS3's Makefile needs per-file flags for vm.arm.c (-fno-gcse -fno-crossjumping) and the dispatch table in DTCM; (4) the risk register entry 'VM too slow' should say the melonDS measurement is optimistic by ~20-30% and name a hardware run (or a 1.25x derating) as the real gate; (5) the plan's '20-29K ops/frame at 50%' text in section 2.3 and research-hardware.md lines 58, 72, 92 should be revised to 16-27K. Nothing in the architecture (bytecode VM vs transpile) flips.

**Residual risk.** Absolute hardware cycle counts are static estimates from Table B3.9: real ARM946E-S behaviour of the write buffer, ITCM data-read contention, D-cache set conflicts between the dispatch table/instance blocks/bytecode, and GCC's register allocation in the full 70-opcode vm_run (GCC 15 already used ip/lr and reloaded the table address in the small typed variant) can move the per-op cost by +-3-5 cycles either way; melonDS cannot settle this because it models none of those effects, and no arm-none-eabi-gcc is installed here, so I could not build a .nds. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (half a day once BlocksDS is installed): build scratchpad\vmgoto\vm.c into the hello ROM with vm_run in ITCM_FUNC and regs/table in DTCM, run 1M ops of the defined mix with cpuStartTiming/cpuEndTiming in three configurations (bytecode loop resident in DTCM; 4 KB straight-line block in main RAM; typed vs tagged opcodes) on melonDS interpreter mode and on DeSmuME, compare against the predicted 30 / 36 / 21 cycles, and if any DS/DSi with a flashcart is available run the same ROM once on hardware to calibrate the melonDS derating factor.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **Compiler Explorer API, ARM GCC 14.2.0 and 15.2.0 (unknown-eabi), '-O2 -marm -mcpu=arm946e-s -mtune=arm946e-s [-fno-gcse -fno-crossjumping]' on scratchpad\vmgoto\vm.c (a 10-opcode register VM in the plan's exact shape: 32-bit op:8 A:8 B:8 C:8 words, 8-byte {u32 tag; s32 payload} cells, int fast path, out-of-line fixed-point fallback). Outputs saved as C:\Users\zache\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\C--Users-zache-OneDrive-Desktop-Projects-DSDude\957dcbd6-e6b3-4e38-9787-f65a383d1ec3\scratchpad\vmgoto\gcc14_O2.s, gcc14_O2_nogcse.s, gcc15_O2_nogcse.s, gcc15_typed.s**: Tag-checked 3-address ADD handler = 24 instructions (GCC 14) / 22 instructions (GCC 15): 2 tag loads + 2 payload loads, orr/cmp/bne, tag store + payload store, then 'ldr r4,[r5],#4; and r3,r4,#255; ldr r3,[r7,r3,lsl #2]; mov pc,r3'. With plain -O2 GCC merges the dispatch tails (6 indirect jumps for 9 handlers, 4 handlers end in 'b .L32'), costing an extra 3-cycle branch per op; with -fno-gcse every handler carries its own dispatch (13 indirect jumps). A type-specialized untagged ADD (vm_typed.c, 4-byte int register file) is 14 instructions.
- **Sloss/Symes/Wright, ARM System Developer's Guide, Appendix B3 Table B3.9 'ARM9Erev2 (ARMv5TE) instruction cycle timings' (PDF fetched from gab.wallawalla.edu and text-extracted with pypdf to scratchpad\vmgoto\B3.txt)**: Verbatim: 'ALU Rd not pc 1 (+1 if a register-specified shift)'; 'ALU Rd is pc 3 (+1 if the operation is logical or any shift is used)'; 'B, BL, BX, BLX 3'; 'LDR Rd not pc 1, Rd is not available for one cycle, +1 if the load offset is shifted'; 'LDR Rd is pc 5, +1 if the load offset is shifted'; 'LDRD 2'; 'STR 1, +1 if a shifted offset is used'; 'STRD 2'; 'xMULL 3'. Pricing the GCC tag-checked ADD with this table (4 scaled-offset ldr/str at 2 each, one load-use interlock before the opcode 'and', one before 'mov pc', mov pc = 3) gives 30 cycles with code in ITCM and all data in TCM/cache. The GCC typed ADD prices at 21 cycles.
- **Local hand-written handlers assembled with C:\devkitPro\devkitARM\bin\arm-none-eabi-as.exe -mcpu=arm946e-s (scratchpad\vmgoto\hand.s, disassembled with the local objdump; all encodings valid incl. 'ldr pc,[r7,r3,lsl #2]', 'ldrd/strd', 'add r2,r6,r2,lsr #13')**: Best-case hand-scheduled tagged ADD using ldrd/strd and a hoisted next-instruction fetch = 16 instructions = 24 cycles by Table B3.9. Best-case untagged 4-byte-register ADD = 10 instructions = 18 cycles. So 16 cycles/op (the 50%-budget reading of 35K/frame) is below the floor even for hand-written assembly; 19 cycles/op is reachable only for untagged/typed ops, not for the plan's 8-byte tagged cells with Lua-5.3-style int/fixed promotion.
- **Arithmetic on the plan's own figures (research-hardware.md line 19: 1,120,380 ARM9 cycles/frame = 263 x 355 x 6 x 2, recomputed locally)**: 35,000 ops/frame = 32.0 cycles/op at 100% CPU, 16.0 cycles/op at 50%. unified-plan.md line 50 states both '50% of the frame gives 20-29K ops per frame' and 'benchmark >= 35K simple ops per frame is a hard gate' without saying the gate is at 100% CPU; lines 289/386/418 repeat '>= 35K ops/frame' with no CPU-share or op-mix definition. The gate is therefore ambiguous; at 100% CPU (32 cycles/op) it is marginally achievable, at 50% it is not.
- **GBATEK DS memory timings as recorded in research-hardware.md lines 9-13 and 106 (high confidence): TCM/cache hit 1 ARM9 cycle; main-RAM cache-line fill 46 ARM9 cycles per 32-byte line; non-sequential 32-bit main-RAM data 20 cycles; ARM946E-S D-cache 4 KB, read-allocate, no prefetch**: Bytecode lives in main RAM (DSDB loaded from NitroFS). Straight-line Step-event code (30-80 ops, typical) misses once per 8 words: +5.75 cycles/op amortized, so the realistic hardware cost of a tag-checked simple op is ~36 cycles (~31K ops/frame at 100% CPU), ~30 only when the loop's bytecode stays cache-resident (which is what a tight microbenchmark loop would measure). The dispatch table (tbl.0, ~280 B for 70 opcodes) is emitted in .rodata (main RAM) unless explicitly placed in DTCM.
- **melonDS master sources downloaded to scratchpad\vmgoto: src/CP15.cpp lines 38-39, 307, 402-403, 782, 802-805; src/ARM.h (saved as ARMv5.h) lines 266-305; src/ARM.cpp ARMv5::JumpTo lines 294-352; GitHub issue melonDS-emu/melonDS#2408 'Known Timing Bugs' (opened 2025-08-17); issue #776 (2020-10-01, ARM9 caches/PU request, no linked PR)**: CP15.cpp: 'const int kDataCacheTiming = 3;//2; const int kCodeCacheTiming = 3;//5;', 'CodeCycles = kCodeCacheTiming;//ICacheLookup(addr);', '// TODO: later on, handle PU, and maybe caches'. AddCycles_CD/CDI = 'Cycles += std::max(numC + numD - 6, std::max(numC, numD))' with DataCycles = 1 for ITCM/DTCM, so every LDR/STR from TCM costs 1 cycle, with no scaled-offset penalty and no load-use interlock; 'mov pc' costs 1 + 2 refill fetches = 3; cacheable main-RAM data reads cost a flat 3 (never 46 for a miss, never 1 for a hit). Issue #2408 lists 'ICache is not modeled accurately', 'DCache is not modeled accurately', 'Cache streaming is unimplemented', 'Write buffer is unimplemented', 'Interlocks are unimplemented', 'ITCM data reads ... should cause contention with single cycle instruction accesses'. Net: melonDS would report ~24-26 cycles for the handler Table B3.9 prices at 30-36, i.e. it under-counts this loop by ~20-30%, so a bare pass of 35K on melonDS can be ~27-29K on hardware. melonDS releases 0.9.5 (Nov 2022) through 1.1 (Nov 2025) list no cache/timing work.
- **BlocksDS default ARM9 makefile, https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/master/sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile**: 'ARCH := -mthumb -mcpu=arm946e-s+nofp', 'CFLAGS := ... $(ARCH) -O2', rule '$(BUILDDIR)/%.arm.c.o : %.arm.c' compiles with '-marm -mlong-calls'. No -fno-gcse/-fno-crossjumping, so vm.arm.c compiled with the default rule gets the merged-dispatch codegen (extra 3-cycle branch per op) unless the runtime Makefile or a '#pragma GCC optimize' / __attribute__((optimize)) adds them.
- **BlocksDS docs: https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/docs/internal/memory_map/ and https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/tutorial/intermediate/tcm_and_cache/**: ITCM 32 KB (accessed at 0x01000000), DTCM 16 KB at 0x02FF4000-0x02FF8000; 'DTCM is used for the stack of the ARM9', 'The stack starts from the end of DTCM and it grows downwards', 'Developers may use DTCM for their own variables, but that will reduce the amount of stack available'; libnds keeps exception handlers in ITCM; 'The libraries included in BlocksDS don't place any variables in DTCM'; placement macros ITCM_FUNC(x), DTCM_DATA_VAR(x), DTCM_BSS_VAR(x). The plan's 4 KB register stack + dispatch table in DTCM is feasible but leaves ~11 KB for the C stack; no free-space figure is documented.
- **Comparable data points: IJES 2009 vol.4 no.1 'Embedded Java benchmark analysis on the ARM processor' (abstract via search: 'interpretation cost of a single bytecode is nearly 21 native instructions and yields average CPI rates of 2.67 on a typical ARM platform'); research-priorart.md lines 46-50, 132 (no published perf numbers for MicroLua/DSLua/ndsChaury on DS); research-hardware.md line 62 (GBVM on a 4.19 MHz SM83)**: ~21 native instructions per bytecode matches the 22-24 measured here; at CPI 2.67 (cache-missing workload) that is ~56 cycles/bytecode, bracketing the 30-36 estimated for a TCM-resident purpose-built VM. Lua-on-DS offers no numbers and GBVM runs on a machine 16x slower with a different pipeline, so neither supports or refutes the specific figure; the only usable comparables are instruction counts, which agree with the GCC measurement.

</details>

## 5. grit -ftr GRF assets and the VRAM bank plan

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** In BlocksDS 1.24 libnds, `grfLoadPath()` (nds/arm9/grf.h) loads GRF files produced by grit with `-ftr`; sprite sheets from `grit sheet.png -gB8 -gt -gTFF00FF -m! -ftr -o out` have frames stacked vertically with stride (w/8)*(h/8)*64 bytes for 8bpp 1D-mapped tiles; 8bpp text backgrounds from `grit bg.png -gB8 -gt -m -mLs -mRtf -gTFF00FF -ftr -o out` load into BG VRAM with a screen-block map (`-mRtf` = tile reduction with flips; `-mRtpf` would additionally reduce by palette which is a 4bpp concept); 4bpp sprites use `-gB4 -pn16`. The VRAM bank plan A=main OBJ, B=main BG, C=sub BG, D=sub OBJ, E=extra main BG, F+G=main extended palettes (BG and OBJ), H=sub BG extended palette, I=sub OBJ extended palette is valid per GBATEK bank sizes and allowed mappings, and gives 128 KB OBJ VRAM per screen.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Keep the grit command lines and grfLoadPath() as written (all flags verified; -mRtf is also grit's built-in default for 8bpp). Amend the two under-specified statements: (1) Sprite stride: frames are stacked vertically in a PNG whose width equals the frame width w, giving (w/8)*(h/8)*64 bytes per frame at 8bpp and (w/8)*(h/8)*32 at 4bpp; because oamInit(..., SpriteMapping_1D_128, ...) addresses OBJ VRAM in 128-byte units (oamGfxPtrToOffset = (addr & 0xFFFFF) >> 7), the runtime must place every frame on a 128-byte boundary, i.e. VRAM stride = max(frameBytes, 128) (affects 8x8 8bpp = 64 B, and 8x8/16x8/8x16 4bpp = 32/64 B). (2) VRAM bank plan: vramSetBankA(VRAM_A_MAIN_SPRITE) [0x06400000, 128 KB]; vramSetBankB(VRAM_B_MAIN_BG) [= VRAM_B_MAIN_BG_0x06020000, 128 KB]; vramSetBankC(VRAM_C_SUB_BG) [0x06200000]; vramSetBankD(VRAM_D_SUB_SPRITE) [0x06600000, 128 KB]; vramSetBankE(VRAM_E_MAIN_BG) [fixed at 0x06000000, 64 KB; E and B are NOT contiguous - E must hold the screen-block maps because bgInit's mapBase (2 KB x 0-31) only reaches the first 64 KB unless DISPLAY_SCREEN_BASE(n) is used, and B must not be set to VRAM_B_MAIN_BG_0x06000000 or it overlaps E]; vramSetBankF(VRAM_F_BG_EXT_PALETTE_SLOT01) [main BG extended palettes for BG0 and BG1 only; BG2/BG3 get none - if all four main layers ever need extended palettes, use vramSetBankE(VRAM_E_BG_EXT_PALETTE) (slots 0-3) and drop E as extra BG]; vramSetBankG(VRAM_G_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE); vramSetBankH(VRAM_H_SUB_BG_EXT_PALETTE) [slots 0-3]; vramSetBankI(VRAM_I_SUB_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE). OBJ VRAM is 128 KB per screen (A main, D sub), fully addressable with 1D_128 mapping. Also note: GRF files must come from the grit shipped in the same BlocksDS install (the 'GRF ' chunk lost its BlocksDS-specific size field in 1.16.0, 2025-12-25, on both the grit and libnds side).

**Impact on the plan.** No command line, flag, function name or number in the claim is wrong, so WS5's asset pipeline contract (C3) and the grfLoadPath wrappers in WS3 stand. Three things must be added to the plan text/contracts so implementers do not trip: (a) contract C3 / the per-screen OBJ VRAM allocator must align each uploaded frame to 128 bytes (or forbid 8x8 8bpp sprites / pad them), and state the 4bpp stride (x32); (b) the bank plan in section 'VRAM' (unified-plan.md line 143) and the WS3 selftest must spell out the libnds enumerators above, in particular VRAM_B_MAIN_BG at 0x06020000 with E at 0x06000000 holding the map screen blocks, and that only BG0/BG1 on the main screen have extended palettes (sufficient for the planned BG0 text layer + BG1 room background; BG2/BG3 do not); (c) the toolchain contract should pin grit and libnds to the same BlocksDS release because the GRF container changed in 1.16.0.

**Residual risk.** Only an installed toolchain can confirm end to end: (1) that the shipped Windows grit.exe from blocksds-toolchain 1.24.0 is built from the same grit master I read (GRF without the GRF-chunk size field, mapAttr=2 for -mLs 8bpp) and that grfLoadPath returns GRF_NO_ERROR on it; (2) that a 512x512 8bpp text BG with maps in bank E and tiles in bank B (tileBase >= 8) renders correctly on melonDS/DeSmuME and hardware with the E/B split, versus simply putting B at 0x06000000 and not using E for BG; (3) that -pn16 combined with the in-house 15-colour quantizer never triggers grit's palette-overflow error; (4) actual behaviour of 128-byte-aligned frame placement with oamSetGfx for sub-128-byte frames. These belong in the Phase 0 spike: build the SDK's bg_regular_nitrofs and sprites_animated examples, then run the plan's exact grit lines on a 32x384 strip and a 512x256 background and load both with grfLoadPath.

For an 8bpp BG with >16 colours -mRtpf therefore merges tiles that differ in the high nibble and corrupts colours; the plan's '-mRtf, not -mRtpf' is correct and material.

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Replace the claim with: "BlocksDS 1.24.0 grit (grit v1.24.0) with -ftr writes RIFF/'GRF ' files with an HDRX (v2, 24-byte) header that libnds 1.24 grfLoadPath() loads into malloc'd RAM (pass pointer-to-NULL; copy to VRAM with dmaCopy/memcpy16, do not pass VRAM pointers for uncompressed chunks); GRFs must come from BlocksDS grit, not devkitPro grit (which writes the unsupported 'HDR ' chunk). Sprites: `grit sheet.png -gB8 -gt -gTFF00FF -m! -ftr -fh! -W1 -o <out>` (add -fh! or a stray <out>.h lands in build/nitrofs): frames stacked vertically are contiguous with file stride (w/8)*(h/8)*64 bytes (8bpp) or *32 (4bpp) in row-major 8x8 tiles; the OBJ VRAM uploader must additionally align each frame to 128 bytes because SpriteMapping_1D_128 addresses VRAM in 128-byte units (pads 8x8 8bpp and 8x8/16x8/8x16 4bpp frames). The pipeline must emit indexed PNGs with at most 255 (or 15) colours and magenta at index 0; if magenta is absent grit swaps an unused index into slot 0 and nothing is transparent. Backgrounds: `grit bg.png -gB8 -gt -m -mLs -mRtf -gTFF00FF -ftr -fh! -W1 -o <out>` gives GRF_BGFMT_SBB_8BPP with 2 KB screen blocks (4 for 512x512); -mRtf equals grit's default (GRIT_RDX_REG8) and is kept for clarity; -mRtpf must not be used on 8bpp (it merges tiles that differ in the upper nibble and writes bogus palette-bank bits). 4bpp sprites: `-gB4 -pn16` (palette chunk 32 bytes). grit.exe needs libstdc++-6.dll and libgcc_s_seh-1.dll: runGrit must put C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin on PATH or the tools pack must ship those two DLLs, otherwise the process dies with exit code 3221225781 (0xC0000135). VRAM: vramSetBankA(VRAM_A_MAIN_SPRITE), vramSetBankB(VRAM_B_MAIN_BG_0x06020000), vramSetBankC(VRAM_C_SUB_BG), vramSetBankD(VRAM_D_SUB_SPRITE), vramSetBankE(VRAM_E_MAIN_BG) [fixed at 0x06000000, so B goes to 0x06020000 and 0x06010000-0x0601FFFF is unmapped: keep maps in E (mapBase 0-31) and tiles in B (tileBase 8-15), or drop E and use B alone], vramSetBankF(VRAM_F_BG_EXT_PALETTE_SLOT01) [covers BG0/BG1 only; BG2/BG3 get no extended palette on the main screen], vramSetBankG(VRAM_G_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE) [first 8 KB used], vramSetBankH(VRAM_H_SUB_BG_EXT_PALETTE) [all 4 slots], vramSetBankI(VRAM_I_SUB_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE); this yields 128 KB OBJ VRAM per screen (main max 256 KB, sub max 128 KB)."

**Impact on the plan.** Section 2.9 / WS5 command lines: add `-fh!` (and `-W1`) to both grit invocations, otherwise `.h` files are written into build/nitrofs/gfx and bg and packed into the ROM; make the 255/15-colour-plus-magenta-at-index-0 quantizer output a hard requirement of contract C3. WS1 `runGrit()` and `tools/fetch-vendor.ps1`: grit.exe must be spawned with C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin on PATH or shipped with libstdc++-6.dll and libgcc_s_seh-1.dll (risk #8 is confirmed, exit 0xC0000135, empty stderr, so the doctor/diagnostic must special-case that status); plan B (devkitPro) cannot be a silent swap for GRF generation. WS3 bank plan: B must be mapped at 0x06020000 if E is used as main BG (libnds VRAM_B_MAIN_BG default already does this), BG maps must live in E's 64 KB (mapBase 0-31) and tiles in B (tileBase 8-15), or drop E; only two main BG layers get extended palettes via F, so keep the room BG and the draw_text layer on BG0/BG1 (or use E for BG ext palettes and B alone for BG data); the per-screen OBJ VRAM allocator must round every frame slot up to 128 bytes, so contract C13's objVramBytesPerScreen meter must count padded sizes (8x8 8bpp frames cost 128 B, not 64 B). Runtime GRF wrappers: load to RAM then dmaCopy, never hand grfLoadPath a VRAM pointer for uncompressed data.

**Residual risk.** What only a real ROM can settle: (1) that grfLoadPath under NitroFS on melonDS/DeSmuME returns these exact chunk sizes and that the loaded 8bpp text BG + extended palettes (F slot 0-1) and 1D_128 sprites display correctly, including the E-at-0x06000000 / B-at-0x06020000 split with bgInit map/tile bases; (2) 128-byte OBJ alignment behaviour for 8x8 8bpp frames; (3) whether Wonderful's grit.exe finds its DLLs on a clean Windows 11 profile with no MSYS2 or Git for Windows. Cheapest Phase-0 experiment: after the WS1 install, build $BLOCKSDS/examples/graphics_2d/bg_regular_nitrofs, drop the already-generated scratchpad\gritlab\out\bg_tf.grf and sheet.grf into its nitrofs folder with a 20-line main.c that applies the nine vramSetBank calls, bgInit(1, BgType_Text8bpp, BgSize_T_512x256, 0, 8), oamInit(&oamMain, SpriteMapping_1D_128, true), and shows frame 3 of the sheet; run in melonDS and compare against the PNGs. For the DLL question, run scratchpad\gritlab\spawn_test.js on a clean VM (or with PATH=System32 plus the two DLLs copied beside grit.exe).
