- **Local run of the real BlocksDS 1.24.0 Windows grit.exe (extracted from https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/blocksds-toolchain-1.24.0-1-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz, 3.0 MB, builddate 2026-09-20, into scratchpad\gritlab\pkg\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\grit\grit.exe); `grit -V` prints `grit v1.24.0-dirty`**: Sprite command on a 32x128 indexed PNG (4 frames of 32x32): output sheet.grf = RIFF/'GRF ' with chunks HDRX (24 B, version=2, gfxAttr=8, mapAttr=0=GRF_BGFMT_NO_DATA, palAttr=256, tile 8x8, gfx 32x128), GFX 4096 B (4-byte compression header type 0x00 + payload), PAL 512 B with pal[0]=0x7C1F (magenta). GFX payload is byte-identical to a row-major 8x8 tiling of the PNG and frame k occupies bytes k*1024..k*1024+1023 = PNG rows 32k..32k+31, i.e. stride (w/8)*(h/8)*64 = 1024 confirmed.
- **Same local grit.exe, background command on a synthetic 512x256 8bpp PNG containing tile A, tile B = A + 0x20 (same low nibble), hflip(A), and a blank tile (scratchpad\gritlab\parsegrf.py output)**: `-m -mLs` (no -mR) and `-m -mLs -mRtf` produce byte-identical files (cmp): 3 tiles, MAP = 4096 B = 2 screen blocks x 1024 entries, 512 entries carry the hflip bit 0x0400, mapAttr=2=GRF_BGFMT_SBB_8BPP. `-mRtpf` on the same 8bpp image logs 'palswap' reduction and emits only 2 tiles with palette-bank values {0,2} in map bits 12-15, i.e. it merges tiles that differ in the upper nibble and mis-labels them with a 4bpp palette bank: actively wrong for 8bpp, which is stronger than the plan's 'is a 4bpp concept'.
- **blocksds/grit libgrit/grit_core.cpp grit_init() and libgrit/grit_core.h; srcgrit/grit_main.cpp help text**: Default mapRedux = GRIT_RDX_REG8 = 0x05 = TILE|FLIP, so -mRtf is grit's default for -m made explicit. Help text: '-mR[48a] Common tile reduction combos: reg 4bpp (-mRtpf), reg 8bpp (-mRtf), affine (-mRt)'. grit_prep_map() sets TMAP_PBANK whenever GRIT_RDX_PBANK is set with no bpp guard, and cldib/cldib_tmap.cpp then compares 8bpp tiles with mask 0x0F and stores pixel>>4 as the palette bank, which explains the corrupted 8bpp output above.
- **Local run: `grit in/spr16.png -gB4 -gt -gTFF00FF -m! -pn16 -ftr -o out/spr16 -W3` on a 16x32 PNG with 16 colours**: spr16.grf: gfxAttr=4, GFX payload 256 B (2 frames x 16x16/2), PAL 32 B (16 entries); grit logs 'Guessed number of palette colors: 16' and 'Palette transparency: pal[0]'. -pn16 sets palEnd = palStart + 16 in grit_parse_pal(); confirmed.
- **Local run with -W3 on a PNG with no magenta and index 0 used as a real colour**: grit warns 'Transparent color not found, adding it to the palette. Setting unused color index 157 to transparent color' and swaps it into index 0; image data is remapped consistently but nothing becomes transparent. If all 256 entries are in use there is no unused slot, so the pipeline's 'quantize to 255/15 colours and put magenta at index 0' step is a requirement, not an option.
- **Local run, files listing in scratchpad\gritlab\out and 'out dir'**: With -ftr grit also writes `<out>.h` (655-768 B) next to every .grf unless -fh! is given (verified: the Node run with -fh! produced only 'node sheet.grf'). Input and -o paths containing spaces ('in dir/sheet two.png', 'out dir/sheet two') work.
- **Local Node 24.16 child_process.spawnSync test (scratchpad\gritlab\spawn_test.js, shell:false, windowsHide:true); `objdump -p grit.exe` import table**: grit.exe imports libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll and api-ms-win-crt-* (UCRT). With PATH=C:\Windows\System32 only the spawn exits with status 3221225781 (0xC0000135 STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND) and empty stderr; with C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin on PATH (or the inherited PATH, where Git's /mingw64/bin supplies libstdc++-6.dll) it exits 0. The package itself ships no DLLs (only tools/grit/grit.exe and COPYING).
- **blocksds/libnds source/arm9/grf.c and include/nds/arm9/grf.h (master, 2026-09)**: grfLoadPath(path, header, gfxDst, gfxSize, mapDst, mapSize, palDst, palSize): the mandatory chunk is 'HDRX' (old 'HDR ' no longer supported); it checks chunk size == sizeof(GRFHeader) (24 B: version u16, gfxAttr/mapAttr/mmapAttr/palAttr u16, tileWidth/tileHeight/metaWidth/metaHeight u8, flags u16, gfxWidth/gfxHeight u32); no version-number check; a NULL dst argument skips the chunk, a pointer to NULL makes it malloc(), a pointer to an address is used directly. Uncompressed chunks (type 0x00, what -ftr emits) are copied with a raw fread()/memcpy into *dst, so VRAM destinations are only safe via RAM + dmaCopy/memcpy16; LZ77/RLE use the VRAM-safe decompressors, Huffman is not VRAM-safe. GRF_BGFMT_SBB_8BPP = 2 matches the mapAttr observed locally.
- **devkitPro/grit master libgrit/grit_xp.cpp**: devkitPro's grit writes the GRF header as chunk 'HDR ' with an 8-bit attrs struct and no version field. BlocksDS 1.24 grf.c ignores unknown chunks and requires HDRX, so GRFs made with devkitPro grit 0.10 load with an unfilled header: the tools pack / plan-B path must always use BlocksDS grit for GRF.
- **GBATEK gbatek-ds-memory-control-vram.htm and blocksds/libnds include/nds/arm9/video.h**: Bank sizes A-D 128K, E 64K, F/G 16K, H 32K, I 16K (656K total). A,B: main BG (OFS 0-3) / main OBJ (OFS 0-1); C: main BG, ARM7, sub BG; D: main BG, ARM7, sub OBJ; E: main BG fixed at 6000000h with no OFS, main OBJ, tex pal, BG ext pal slots 0-3; F,G: main BG/OBJ, tex pal, BG ext pal 'Slot 0-1 (OFS=0), Slot 2-3 (OFS=1)', OBJ ext pal; H: sub BG, sub BG ext pal slots 0-3; I: sub BG, sub OBJ, sub OBJ ext pal. Max mappable main BG 512K, main OBJ 256K, sub BG 128K, sub OBJ 128K. libnds: VRAM_E_MAIN_BG has only the 0x06000000 variant; VRAM_B_MAIN_BG defaults to 1|VRAM_OFFSET(1) = 0x06020000; VRAM_F_BG_EXT_PALETTE_SLOT01 / _SLOT23; VRAM_F_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE, VRAM_H_SUB_BG_EXT_PALETTE, VRAM_I_SUB_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE all exist. So every mapping in the plan is legal and A (128K) + D (128K) give 128 KB OBJ VRAM per screen.
- **GBATEK gbatek-ds-video-extended-palettes.htm**: BG ext palette = 4 slots x 8K (32K) per engine, 'normally BG0..3 are using Slot 0..3' (BG0/BG1 can switch to 2/3 via BGxCNT); OBJ ext palette = 8K per engine, 'only the initial 8K' of a 16K bank is used; 16-colour BGs/OBJs keep using the standard palette. Consequently F as main BG ext palette covers only two layers (slots 0-1 or 2-3), H covers all four sub layers.
- **blocksds/libnds include/nds/arm9/sprite.h and background.h**: oamInit(OamState*, SpriteMapping, bool extPalette) exists; SpriteMapping_1D_128 = '128 byte boundary between offsets' (needed to address 128 KB with 10-bit tile indices), so OAM gfx offsets are 128-byte granular: 8x8 8bpp frames (64 B) and 4bpp frames below 128 B (8x8, 16x8, 8x16) must be padded/aligned to 128 B in VRAM even though the GRF stride is (w/8)*(h/8)*64 (8bpp) or *32 (4bpp). bgInit takes mapBase 0-31 (2 KB units) and tileBase 0-15 (16 KB units) and libnds sets no DISPCNT 64 KB char/screen-base offsets ('you must do that before using any background-related function'), so BG maps must sit in the first 64 KB of main BG VRAM.

</details>

## 6. melonDS 1.1 download, stdout logging, config

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** melonDS 1.1 (Windows x86_64 release zip https://github.com/melonDS-emu/melonDS/releases/download/1.1/melonDS-1.1-windows-x86_64.zip, SHA-256 9F3F8A244103BE20B5B657AF5B0ED1B2A66BB20A7181476A6D294C9A53D4F8C8, 19,484,283 bytes) is the current stable release; it boots DS-mode homebrew without BIOS/firmware dumps; launched as `melonDS.exe <rom>` it forwards nocash-style debug messages written by the ROM to stdout (BlocksDS libnds `nocashMessage`/`nocashWrite` use the 0x04FFFA18 register protocol and/or the legacy `mov r12,r12` + 0x6464 signature) so Node `spawn(exe,[rom],{stdio:'pipe'})` receives them; it ships with no default key bindings so `melonDS.toml` must be written before launch with keys like `[Instance0.Keyboard]`, `[Instance0.Window0]`, `[3D] Renderer`, `[Screen] UseGL`, `[Instance0.Gdb] Enabled` (GDB stub ports 3333/3334); DeSmuME 0.9.13 prints only the legacy-signature messages and ignores the 0x04FFFAxx registers, has no GDB stub, and keeps desmume.ini next to the exe.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** melonDS 1.1 (Windows x86_64 release zip https://github.com/melonDS-emu/melonDS/releases/download/1.1/melonDS-1.1-windows-x86_64.zip, SHA-256 9F3F8A244103BE20B5B657AF5B0ED1B2A66BB20A7181476A6D294C9A53D4F8C8, 19,484,283 bytes, containing only melonDS.exe) is the current stable release (2025-11-18); it boots DS-mode homebrew without BIOS/firmware dumps (FreeBIOS + DirectBoot defaults). Launched as `melonDS.exe <rom>` it prints nocash-style debug messages to stdout (Platform::Log -> vprintf, unfiltered): on the ARM9 the no$gba registers 0x04FFFA10 (raw), 0x04FFFA14 (with %params%, NO newline), 0x04FFFA18 (with %params% + newline) and 0x04FFFA1C (char), and on both CPUs, in the interpreter (JIT off is the default), the legacy `mov r12,r12; b; .hword 0x6464` signature in ARM and Thumb form. BlocksDS libnds 1.15.0+ (including 1.24.0) `nocashMessage`/`nocashWrite`/stderr on the ARM9 write REG_NOCASH_STR_PARAM = 0x04FFFA14 (no linefeed: messages must embed '\n' or the runtime must write 0x04FFFA18 itself), while the ARM7 versions use the legacy Thumb signature; libnds never uses 0x04FFFA18. Message strings must live in main RAM (melonDS reads them through NDS::ARM9Read8, which does not map DTCM/ITCM). Node `spawn(exe,[rom],{stdio:'pipe'})` receives this output, but melonDS.exe is a GUI-subsystem binary with no setvbuf/fflush, so on a pipe stdout is fully buffered: lines arrive in ~4 KB chunks or at clean exit (WM_CLOSE via `taskkill /PID`), and `child.kill()`/TerminateProcess discards unflushed output. It ships with no default key bindings (Config.cpp defaults `Instance*.Keyboard` and `Instance*.Joystick` = -1), so `melonDS.toml` (portable build: next to melonDS.exe, read once at startup) must be written before launch with `[Instance0.Keyboard]`, `[Instance0.Window0]`, `[3D] Renderer = 0`, `[Screen] UseGL = false`, `[Instance0.Gdb] Enabled = true`, `[Instance0.Gdb.ARM9] Port = 3333`, `[Instance0.Gdb.ARM7] Port = 3334` (3333/3334 are the defaults; ENABLE_GDBSTUB is on in release builds). DeSmuME 0.9.13 prints only the legacy-signature messages (ARM and Thumb branch hooks, both CPUs) through a bare `printf` with no fflush and no appended newline (so its pipe output is block-buffered too, not immediate), ignores the 0x04FFFAxx registers entirely, has no GDB stub in the official Release build (GDB_STUB is defined only in the 'Dev+' MSBuild configuration; the shipped exe reports '0.9.13 x64 SSE2' without 'dev+' and contains no gdbstub strings; `--arm9gdb` makes it exit 1), and keeps desmume.ini next to the exe unless the exe is under %TEMP% (then %LOCALAPPDATA%\DeSmuME\desmume.ini).

**Impact on the plan.** 1) Contract C8 / WS3 and unified-plan.md line 68: replace '0x04FFFA18 prints' and 'nocashMessage + legacy signature' with an explicit runtime print routine: write the string pointer to 0x04FFFA18 directly (or append '\n' if libnds nocashMessage()/0x04FFFA14 is used, otherwise consecutive DSD| lines run together on melonDS), keep the formatted log buffer static in main RAM (never a DTCM stack buffer), and emit the legacy signature (Thumb is fine) for DeSmuME. 2) unified-plan.md line 70 ('immediate flushes from DeSmuME') and risk #7 are wrong in the DeSmuME half: both emulators block-buffer stdout on a pipe, so the batched-pipe design applies to both. 3) phase0/M0/CP-B gates that say 'DSD|LOG|hello captured' must define capture as 'after a graceful Stop (taskkill /PID -> WM_CLOSE)' or after >= 4 KB of output; the research snippet's `current.kill()` would lose the line. Keep taskkill-without-/F first (already in section 3.2). 4) The research doc's 'melonDS does not implement the legacy signature' is false; both protocols remain reasonable for JIT-on/no$gba coverage, but the melonDS-only path could be the signature alone. 5) The node-pty rationale (AttachConsole in main.cpp) describes post-1.1 master, not the 1.1 binary; the v1.1 experiment must be measured, not assumed. 6) Nothing changes for the download URL, hash, size, toml keys, GDB ports, no-BIOS boot, DeSmuME no-GDB or desmume.ini location.

**Residual risk.** Only a run can settle: (a) that a real BlocksDS 1.24.0 ROM's DSD| lines reach Node through the melonDS pipe and when (the prior test used a hand-built ROM and only the emulator's own startup lines; expect delivery at ~4 KB or at graceful close); (b) whether node-pty/ConPTY makes melonDS 1.1 stdout unbuffered (1.1 has no console-attach code, so this depends purely on the CRT treating the ConPTY handle as a char device); (c) DeSmuME's actual pipe flush timing (source shows no fflush; a timed pipe test is needed before promising live logs from the DeSmuME profile); (d) that the Wonderful `blocksds-toolchain` package installs libnds 1.24.0 (assumed from the changelog); (e) GDB attach/stepping from gdb-multiarch 17.1 to the 1.1 stub with JIT off.

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** melonDS 1.1 (melonDS-1.1-windows-x86_64.zip, 19,484,283 bytes, SHA-256 9F3F8A244103BE20B5B657AF5B0ED1B2A66BB20A7181476A6D294C9A53D4F8C8, single melonDS.exe, GUI subsystem, portable build) is the newest release (2025-11-18) and boots DS-mode homebrew with FreeBIOS + DirectBoot and no BIOS/firmware files. It implements the no$gba debug registers on the ARM9 ONLY (0x04FFFA10 raw string ≤1023 chars no LF; 0x04FFFA14 / 0x04FFFA18 string with %param% substitution, TRUNCATED AT 120 CHARS, 0x18 appends LF; 0x04FFFA1C char out) and does NOT implement the legacy `mov r12,r12` + 0x6464 signature nor any ARM7 debug register. BlocksDS libnds (1.15.0+, incl. 1.24.0) ARM9 nocashMessage()/nocashWrite() write the pointer to 0x04FFFA14 (NO linefeed; nocashWrite truncates to 119 chars), and the ARM7 versions use the legacy Thumb signature with a 120-byte buffer. So the DSD runtime must (a) write 0x04FFFA10 itself with '\n' included in the string (or 0x04FFFA18 for lines ≤120 chars without '%') rather than rely on nocashMessage, and (b) emit the legacy signature in inline asm (ARM: E1A0C00C, B, 6464h, flags, NUL-terminated text incl. '\n'; or Thumb 46E4h) for DeSmuME. Output reaches Node `spawn(exe,[rom],{stdio:'pipe'})` but stdout is fully buffered by UCRT (~4 KB) and melonDS never flushes: lines appear only every ~4 KB or at clean exit (WM_CLOSE / taskkill without /F); child.kill()/taskkill /F loses the buffered tail. For live output use ConPTY (@lydell/node-pty 1.2.0-beta.15 has a win32-x64 prebuild). Do NOT pass windowsHide:true: on this machine it sets STARTUPINFO wShowWindow=SW_HIDE, which Windows applies to the child's first ShowWindow(SW_SHOWNORMAL) and hides the emulator window (verified with a Win32 probe under Node 24.16). melonDS ships with every Instance0.Keyboard/Joystick key = -1, so melonDS.toml must be written beside melonDS.exe before launch with [Instance0.Keyboard] (Qt key codes: A=88 B=90 X=83 Y=65 L=81 R=87 Start=16777220 Select=16777248 Up=16777235 Down=16777237 Left=16777234 Right=16777236), [Instance0.Window0] IntegerScaling/ShowOSD, [3D] Renderer=0, [Screen] UseGL=false, [Emu] DirectBoot=true, and for Debug [Instance0.Gdb] Enabled=true + [Instance0.Gdb.ARM9] Port=3333 / [Instance0.Gdb.ARM7] Port=3334 (sockets open only once a ROM runs). DeSmuME 0.9.13 prints only the legacy signature (ARM and Thumb forms, interpreter core, unlimited length, no LF appended, printf+fflush so it is live) and SWI 0xFC; it ignores 0x04FFFAxx, has no GDB stub (no arm9gdb/gdbstub strings in the exe), and writes desmume.ini next to the exe unless the exe lives under %TEMP% (then %LOCALAPPDATA%\DeSmuME).

**Impact on the plan.** 1) Section 3.2 step 7 and research section 4 spawn with `windowsHide:true`; keep that and melonDS/DeSmuME start invisible -- remove the flag (no console window is created for GUI-subsystem exes anyway). 2) Contract C8 says lines are printed through 0x04FFFA18; a ≤120-char cap and %-substitution apply there, and DSD|ERR lines with file paths/messages will exceed 120 chars -- switch to 0x04FFFA10 with an explicit '\n' (1023-char cap, no % mangling) and never route DSD| lines through BlocksDS nocashMessage()/nocashWrite() (0x04FFFA14, no LF, 119-char truncation), or the Output parser sees concatenated/truncated lines. 3) User-journey step 6 ('launching melonDS... then DSD|READY') and the phase0/M0 acceptance 'DSD|LOG|hello captured' will not happen live with plain pipes: melonDS output arrives in 4 KB blocks or at clean exit, and a forced kill discards it. Either accept that the line shows only after a graceful Stop (taskkill without /F first, wait for exit before writing the next melonDS.toml), pad the runtime's startup output, or promote ConPTY (@lydell/node-pty prebuild) from v1.1 experiment to Phase 0. 4) melonDS has no ARM7 debug output and no legacy-signature support; DeSmuME requires the interpreter (default CPUmode=0) for the signature check -- keep JIT off in both emulators' generated configs. 5) Legacy-signature output needs a writable text buffer inside code (as BlocksDS's ARM7 asm does) or a per-message asm block; message must carry its own '\n'.

**Residual risk.** Not testable here without launching an emulator (forbidden) or a BlocksDS toolchain (not installed): (a) that melonDS's Qt main window is the first ShowWindow call in the process (the probe proves the OS mechanism and Qt uses SW_SHOWNORMAL, but a hidden helper window shown earlier would change the outcome) -- and, if the plan keeps windowsHide:true, whether the window is hidden; (b) that a real BlocksDS ROM's 0x04FFFA10/18 writes reach the Node pipe with the expected text (the research run only captured melonDS's own log lines from a `b .` stub ROM); (c) that ConPTY (node-pty) really yields unbuffered output (UCRT treats console handles as unbuffered, but unverified with melonDS); (d) NitroFS booting on both emulators. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (≈30 min): build samples/hello with BlocksDS printing one line via 0x04FFFA10 (with '\n') and one via an ARM-mode legacy signature block, then from Node run three launches -- spawn without windowsHide (assert window visible), spawn with stdio:'pipe' and graceful WM_CLOSE after 5 s (assert the line arrives only at exit), and spawn under @lydell/node-pty (assert the line arrives within ~200 ms) -- and the same ROM in DeSmuME 0.9.13 with `--start-paused` asserting the legacy line appears immediately on the pipe.

No handler for the legacy mov r12,r12/0x6464 signature exists in ARM.cpp, ARMInterpreter*.cpp or ARMJIT.cpp. CP15.cpp emulates only the ICache (no data cache), so a freshly written string buffer is visible to NocashPrint.

The stdio path (nocash_putc_buffered) flushes on '\n' or at 120 chars, keeping the '\n' in the string. ARM7 nocashMessage/nocashWrite are Thumb asm using the legacy signature (mov r12,r12; b 2f; .hword 0x6464; .hword 0; 120-byte inline buffer) with a comment 'melonDS doesn't support the debug registers on the ARM7'. v1.24.0 adds is_nocashgba() but nocashMessage does not gate on the emulator ID.

A ROM emitting fewer than ~4 KB after the startup chatter delivers nothing until the process exits cleanly; TerminateProcess (child.kill(), taskkill /F) discards the buffered tail.
- **Local experiment scratchpad\verify-emu\probe (probe.c compiled with C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\gcc.exe -mwindows; run.js under Node v24.16.0)**: GUI-subsystem child doing ShowWindow(hwnd, SW_SHOWNORMAL) -- what Qt's QWindowsWindow::show_sys() (qtbase 6.8) and DeSmuME's MainWindow->Show(SW_NORMAL) do -- reports with windowsHide:true: dwFlags=0x101 wShowWindow=0 IsWindowVisible=0 (window hidden); with windowsHide:false or default: wShowWindow=10 IsWindowVisible=1. stdoutFileType=3 (pipe) in all cases, so piped stdout from a GUI app works. libuv src/win/process.c confirms: windowsHide sets wShowWindow=SW_HIDE with STARTF_USESHOWWINDOW always set.

MMU.cpp has no 0x04FFFAxx handling. Exe contains 0 occurrences of 'arm9gdb'/'gdbstub' (flags are #ifdef GDB_STUB only). GetINIPath writes <exe dir>\desmume.ini unless the exe path starts with GetTempPath, then %LOCALAPPDATA%\DeSmuME. [Console] Show defaults to false in PUBLIC_RELEASE; console.cpp keeps an inherited pipe (redirects only when FILE_TYPE_UNKNOWN). The user's Downloads copy has desmume.ini beside the exe.

## 7. Tools pack (ndstool/grit/mmutil) without MSYS2

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** The Windows binaries `ndstool.exe`, `grit.exe` and `mmutil.exe` installed by the BlocksDS package under C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\ are native PE executables that run on a clean Windows 11 profile once the DLLs reported by `C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe` (ucrt64/msys runtime DLLs) are copied beside them, so an IDE can ship a small 'tools pack' without MSYS2; ndstool is GPL-3, grit GPL-2, mmutil ISC/permissive; running GPL tools as separate processes does not impose GPL on the IDE.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** ndstool.exe, grit.exe and mmutil.exe (blocksds-toolchain 1.24.0-1, installed at C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\<tool>\<tool>.exe) are native PE32+ x86-64 console executables built by the MSYS2 UCRT64 MinGW-w64 GCC (16.1.0). They do NOT depend on msys-2.0.dll. Their only non-Windows dependencies are the MinGW-w64 runtime DLLs that Wonderful's `runtime-gcc-libs` package installs into C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin (found via PATH inside the Wonderful shell, not beside the tools): ndstool.exe needs libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll and libiconv-2.dll; grit.exe needs libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll; mmutil.exe needs none. ucrtbase.dll and the api-ms-win-crt-*.dll API sets are part of Windows 10/11 and must not be shipped. Verified: with a PATH of only C:\Windows\System32;C:\Windows and those four DLLs copied beside the exes (taken from runtime-gcc-libs-0.1.0-14), `ndstool -c`/`-i`, `grit -ftc`/`-ftr` and `mmutil -d` all succeed; without the DLLs ndstool exits 0xC0000135. tools/fetch-vendor.ps1 must therefore: (1) copy the DLLs from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin (same install as the exes), never from C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin; (2) if it uses C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe, run it with C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin on PATH (ldd executes the binary under the Windows loader and only sees DLLs that resolve), keep only entries whose resolved path is under /opt/wonderful/bin (convert with cygpath -w), and drop everything under /c/WINDOWS plus any 'api-ms-win-crt-* => not found' lines; or simply use `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\objdump.exe -p <exe> | findstr "DLL Name"` recursively (libstdc++-6.dll itself imports libgcc_s_seh-1.dll and libwinpthread-1.dll); (3) quote arguments like "-osoundbank.bin" when spawning from Windows PowerShell 5.1. Licenses: ndstool GPL-3.0 (ship both COPYING.gpl3 and COPYING.mit, the latter for Forest Belton's MIT component); grit GPL-2.0 (COPYING is the plain GPLv2 text, no 'or later' grant found: treat as GPL-2.0-only; the repo also carries licence-mit.txt (J Vijn 2007) and bundled libplum); mmutil ISC (Mukunda Johnson 2008, notice required). The redistributed DLLs are GPL-3.0-or-later with the GCC Runtime Library Exception (libstdc++/libgcc/libwinpthread) and libiconv-2.dll is GNU libiconv (LGPL; not separately verified in this pass) - each needs its notice in the license bundle. Spawning the tools as child processes with command-line arguments and files is the FSF FAQ's 'separate programs' case, so the IDE's own code is not GPL-bound; but redistributing the binaries triggers GPL-3 §6 / GPL-2 §3: publish the exact Corresponding Source (blocksds/ndstool and blocksds/grit at the v1.24.0 tag plus the blocksds/packages PKGBUILD and Wonderful build scripts) as archives in the same release/download location as the tools pack, with clear directions next to the pack, not merely 'source links' to upstream (GPL-2 §3 requires equivalent access 'from the same place').

**Impact on the plan.** WS1 deliverable tools/fetch-vendor.ps1 (plan line 279) and risk #8 (line 434) must change from 'copy the DLLs listed by C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe' to the explicit 4-DLL list sourced from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin, with ldd run inside the Wonderful environment (or replaced by objdump -p) and the /c/WINDOWS and api-ms-win-crt-* lines filtered out; otherwise the pack either fails to build (ldd reports 'not found' outside the shell) or ships nothing useful. The 'static rebuild' fallback in risk #8 is unnecessary. The license bundle (lines 90, 351-352, 435) must add: ndstool's COPYING.mit, grit's licence-mit.txt/libplum notice, the GCC Runtime Library Exception notice for libstdc++-6.dll/libgcc_s_seh-1.dll/libwinpthread-1.dll, a notice for libiconv-2.dll, and the exact Corresponding Source archives hosted in the same release as the tools pack (GPL-2 'same place' rule for grit) rather than only 'source links'. The M6 'clean Windows 11 VM without MSYS2' exit criterion remains the right end-to-end confirmation, but it is now a check rather than an open question.

**Residual risk.** Only a genuinely fresh Windows 11 VM (no MSYS2 ever installed) settles the last mile; the test here scrubbed PATH and confirmed System32 lacks the MinGW DLLs and that the control run fails, but it still ran on a machine where ucrtbase/api-ms-win-crt API sets are inbox Windows components (true for all Windows 10/11, but not exercised on a different image). Wonderful's runtime-gcc-libs DLL versions change per release (0.1.0-14 today, GCC 16.1.0), so fetch-vendor.ps1 must always pair the DLLs with the exes from the same install rather than a cached set; and the BlocksDS Windows package is rebuilt monthly, so the import list (e.g. ndstool's libiconv-2.dll) should be re-derived at pack-build time. The libiconv license and the exact scope of grit's MIT/libplum components were not verified against primary text in this pass and belong in the license-bundle review.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **Published package https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/blocksds-toolchain-1.24.0-1-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz (sha256 23f78983ac9706b2da4e91ec30d8c412cbc387d7ded42097e98a8daead6d3af7), downloaded to scratch and listed with tar**: Contains thirdparty/blocksds/core/tools/ndstool/{ndstool.exe,COPYING.gpl3,COPYING.mit}, tools/grit/{grit.exe,COPYING}, tools/mmutil/{mmutil.exe,COPYING}. .PKGINFO: pkgver 1.24.0-1, depend = runtime-gcc-libs (plus toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-*). Paths are relative to WF_DESTDIR=/opt/wonderful, i.e. C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\thirdparty\blocksds\core\tools\<tool>\<tool>.exe (one subfolder per tool, not flat).
- **objdump -p (C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\objdump.exe) and `file` on the three extracted binaries**: All three: 'PE32+ executable for MS Windows 5.02 (console), x86-64', file format pei-x86-64, Subsystem 3 (Windows CUI). Imports: ndstool.exe -> libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, KERNEL32.dll, api-ms-win-crt-*; grit.exe -> libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, KERNEL32.dll, api-ms-win-crt-*; mmutil.exe -> KERNEL32.dll + api-ms-win-crt-* only. No msys-2.0.dll, no libpng/zlib/FreeImage DLL in any of them.
- **C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe (ldd (cygwin) 3.6.9) run on the three exes with PATH containing the ucrt64 DLL dir**: ndstool: ntdll, KERNEL32, KERNELBASE, ucrtbase (all /c/WINDOWS/System32) + libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll; grit: same minus libiconv-2.dll; mmutil: only the four Windows system DLLs. With PATH lacking the MinGW dir, ldd prints 'libgcc_s_seh-1.dll => not found', 'libstdc++-6.dll => not found', 'libiconv-2.dll => not found' AND 'api-ms-win-crt-*.dll => not found' for all 11 API-set stubs (which Windows provides via ucrtbase) - so a naive 'copy what ldd lists' script would either miss the DLLs or chase non-existent api-ms files, and must not copy the /c/WINDOWS entries.
- **https://cygwin.com/cygwin-ug-net/ldd.html**: 'ldd invokes the Windows loader on the file specified, then uses the Windows debugging interface to report DLLs loaded, and (for executables) to attempt to stop execution before the entrypoint. Thus, you should never use ldd on an untrusted file.' ldd therefore executes the tool and only sees what resolves in the current PATH; -r/-u/-v are 'currently unimplemented' (ldd --help).
- **Scratch-folder execution test via powershell.exe -NoProfile with $env:PATH = 'C:\Windows\System32;C:\Windows;C:\Windows\System32\Wbem' (Test-Path confirmed System32 has no libstdc++-6.dll / libiconv-2.dll)**: CONTROL: ndstool.exe alone (no DLLs beside it) exits -1073741515 = 0xC0000135 STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND with no output. WITH libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll beside the exes: `ndstool.exe -c t.nds -9 a9.bin -7 a7.bin` exit 0, `ndstool.exe -i t.nds` prints header (Header CRC OK), `grit.exe test.png -gB8 -gt -m! -ftc -o testout` exit 0 (testout.c 3328 B / testout.h) and with -ftr produced testout.grf (836 B), `mmutil.exe -d test.wav "-osoundbank.bin" "-hsoundbank.h"` exit 0 (soundbank.bin 844 B, soundbank.h with SFX_TEST). Repeated with the DLLs taken from Wonderful's own runtime-gcc-libs-0.1.0-14 package: identical success. (The first mmutil attempt failed only because Windows PowerShell 5.1 splits the unquoted token -osoundbank.bin into '-osoundbank' and '.bin'; quote such args when spawning from PowerShell.)
- **https://wonderful.asie.pl/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/runtime-gcc-libs-0.1.0-14-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz (downloaded, listed) and https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WonderfulToolchain/wonderful-packages/HEAD/packages/runtime-gcc-libs/windows/PKGBUILD**: Package installs bin/libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libgomp-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll, libxxhash.dll, libzstd.dll, zlib1.dll (PKGBUILD: cp -d from /ucrt64/bin into $pkgdir/bin then wf_relocate_path_to_destdir -> /opt/wonderful/bin); .PKGINFO pkgdesc 'MSYS/UCRT64 GCC runtime libraries', license = GPL-3.0-or-later. The DLLs carry 'GCC: (Rev7, Built by MSYS2 project) 16.1.0' and differ byte-for-byte from this machine's ucrt64 15.2.0 copies (libstdc++-6.dll 2665473 vs 2463194 bytes). So the DLLs live in C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin, NOT beside the tools and NOT in C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WonderfulToolchain/wonderful-packages/HEAD/packages/runtime-msys2-shell/windows/wonderful_shell.cmd and misc/windows/bootstrap-installer-x86_64.iss**: wonderful_shell.cmd sets MSYSTEM=UCRT64, MSYS2_PATH_TYPE=inherit, PATH=%WD%../../opt/wonderful/bin;%PATH%, WONDERFUL_TOOLCHAIN=/opt/wonderful, BLOCKSDS=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/core, BLOCKSDSEXT=/opt/wonderful/thirdparty/blocksds/external. Installer: DefaultDirName=C:\msys64, files to {app}\opt\wonderful. Hence the tools find their DLLs only through PATH inside the Wonderful shell; outside it they fail with 0xC0000135 (as reproduced).
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/WonderfulToolchain/wonderful-packages/HEAD/containers/windows/x86_64/Containerfile and .../containers/windows/x86_64/msys2**: Windows packages are built in an MSYS2 install run under Wine on debian:bookworm with mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-toolchain, wrapper: MSYSTEM="${MSYSTEM:-UCRT64}" ... wine bash.exe -l. So host tools are compiled by the UCRT64 MinGW-w64 GCC (native PE, UCRT CRT), not as MSYS (Cygwin-like) programs.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/packages/master/blocksds-toolchain/PKGBUILD (repo github.com/blocksds/packages, mirror of codeberg.org/blocksds/packages)**: depends=(runtime-gcc-libs runtime-musl toolchain-gcc-arm-none-eabi-...); Windows-specific line `sed -i -e 's/$(LIBS)/$(LIBS) -liconv/' tools/ndstool/Makefile` (explains ndstool's libiconv-2.dll import); build `BLOCKSDS=`pwd` make -j`nproc``; install `INSTALLDIR="$pkgdir$WF_DESTDIR"/thirdparty/blocksds/core make -j1 install`. Tool Makefiles (blocksds/ndstool, blocksds/grit, blocksds/mmutil master) use HOSTCC ?= gcc / HOSTCXX ?= g++, no -static, LIBS empty (grit), -lm (mmutil), -liconv only on Darwin (ndstool) -> dynamic MinGW runtime as observed.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/grit/master/changelog.md**: BlocksDS v0.9.0: 'Replaced FreeImage by libplum. This lets us distribute binaries without the need for DLLs.' - consistent with grit.exe importing no image-library DLL.
- **GitHub API license detection: https://api.github.com/repos/blocksds/{ndstool,grit,mmutil} and .../license; in-package COPYING files**: ndstool: GPL-3.0, COPYING.gpl3 ('GNU GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE Version 3, 29 June 2007') plus COPYING.mit ('Copyright (C) 2009 Forest Belton', MIT) both shipped in tools/ndstool/. grit: GPL-2.0, COPYING = 'GNU GENERAL PUBLIC LICENSE Version 2, June 1991' (repo also has licence-mit.txt 'Copyright (c) 2007, J Vijn', license-fi.txt, and bundled libplum; no 'or (at your option) any later version' grant found in grit_main.cpp / grit_core.cpp / cldib_core.cpp, whose headers only carry 'SPDX-FileNotice: Modified from the original version by the BlocksDS project, starting from 2023'). mmutil: ISC, COPYING 'ISC License ... Copyright (c) 2008, Mukunda Johnson'. https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/docs/guides/licenses/ does not list host tools at all (only libraries + picolibc/libstdc++/libgcc), so the licenses page is not a source for the tool licenses.
- **https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-faq.en.html (#MereAggregation, #GPLInProprietarySystem, #CanIUseGPLToolsForNF)**: 'pipes, sockets and command-line arguments are communication mechanisms normally used between two separate programs. So when they are used for communication, the modules normally are separate programs.' but 'if the semantics of the communication are intimate enough, exchanging complex internal data structures, that too could be a basis to consider the two parts as combined into a larger program.' Also: 'if the two programs remain well separated, like the compiler and the kernel, or like an editor and a shell, then you can treat them as two separate programs' and users must 'clearly understand the free status of the GPL-covered software in the collection.' This is the FSF's interpretation, not a court ruling; spawning ndstool/grit/mmutil with argv + files is the textbook 'separate programs' case.
- **GPL-3 §1/§6 (https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/ndstool/master/COPYING.gpl3) and GPL-2 §3 (https://www.gnu.org/licenses/old-licenses/gpl-2.0.txt)**: GPL-3 §6(d): may convey object code from a designated place and 'offer equivalent access to the Corresponding Source in the same way through the same place'; the source 'may be on a different server (operated by you or a third party) ... provided you maintain clear directions next to the object code saying where to find the Corresponding Source. Regardless of what server hosts the Corresponding Source, you remain obligated to ensure that it is available for as long as needed'. Corresponding Source = 'all the source code needed to generate, install, and (for an executable work) run the object code and to modify the work, including scripts to control those activities'. GPL-2 §3: 3(a) accompany with source, 3(b) 3-year written offer, and 'offering equivalent access to copy the source code from the same place counts as distribution' - GPL-2 has no third-party-server clause, so for grit a bare link to codeberg is weaker than hosting the exact source archive next to the tools pack.
- **Local machine state (read-only ls)**: C:\msys64\opt\wonderful does not exist (BlocksDS/Wonderful never installed here); C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe exists; C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin has libstdc++-6.dll/libgcc_s_seh-1.dll/libwinpthread-1.dll (mingw-w64-ucrt-x86_64-gcc-libs 15.2.0-11, libiconv 1.18-1). The plan's `detectToolchain()` paths (tools\ndstool\ndstool.exe etc.) match the package layout.

</details>

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** ndstool.exe, grit.exe and mmutil.exe from blocksds-toolchain 1.24.0 are native PE32+ x86-64 console executables (no msys-2.0.dll). They run on a clean Windows 11 profile (PATH=C:\Windows\System32 only, spawned from Node with windowsHide, paths with spaces) when these four DLLs from the Wonderful `runtime-gcc-libs` package (installed at C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin) sit beside them: libstdc++-6.dll, libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll (mmutil needs none; grit needs no libpng DLL). Do NOT derive the list from `ldd.exe` at build time: ldd resolves by the caller's PATH and on this machine (Git Bash / Git's mingw64\bin ahead of ucrt64) it returns C:\Program Files\Git\mingw64\bin\*.dll, msvcrt-flavour builds that load and work silently but mismatch the UCRT exes; it also lists ntdll/KERNEL32/KERNELBASE/ucrtbase/msvcrt and prints POSIX paths. tools/fetch-vendor.ps1 should instead (a) read imports with `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\objdump.exe -p` recursively, ignoring KERNEL32/api-ms-win-*, and copy from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin only (or unpack runtime-gcc-libs-*.pkg.tar.xz directly), (b) assert each copied DLL imports api-ms-win-crt-*, not msvcrt.dll, and (c) run `ndstool -V`, `grit -V`, `mmutil` with env PATH=C:\Windows\System32 expecting exit 0, and without the DLLs expecting exit 3221225781 (0xC0000135). Licences: ndstool GPL-3.0, grit GPL-2.0, mmutil ISC, runtime DLLs GPL-3 with runtime exception; spawning them as separate processes with argv/files does not put the IDE under the GPL (FSF FAQ MereAggregation/GPLPlugins), but redistributing the binaries requires shipping the GPL texts (not in the package; take COPYING.gpl3/COPYING from the blocksds repos) and a Corresponding Source offer for the exact build (host the v1.24.0 source tarballs of tools/ndstool and tools/grit with the release rather than only linking upstream).

**Impact on the plan.** Risk item 8 and the WS1 tools/fetch-vendor.ps1 deliverable change: replace 'DLLs listed by C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe' with an objdump-based import walk sourced from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin (or the runtime-gcc-libs package), plus a CRT-flavour assertion and a clean-PATH run test, otherwise the pack can silently ship Git-for-Windows msvcrt DLLs on this machine. Drop the 'msys runtime DLLs' wording (nothing msys is needed; msys-2.0.dll must not be copied). 'Static rebuild as a fallback' is unnecessary. The licence bundle needs the GPL texts fetched from the repos and a hosted source tarball for ndstool/grit v1.24.0, not just links. Tools pack size ~5 MB plus arm7_maxmod.elf, so 'small' holds.

**Residual risk.** (1) The post-install location C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin for the four DLLs is inferred from the package layout (bin/ under WF_PATH=/opt/wonderful, WF_DESTDIR=/), not observed, since BlocksDS is not installed on this machine. (2) Only argument-level runs were tested; a long grit/mmutil session on real assets under the clean environment was not, though the import table leaves no other external dependency. (3) Unsigned MinGW-built exes may trip antivirus heuristics on some clean machines; SmartScreen does not apply to CreateProcess spawns. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (about 5 minutes after the install): run `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\objdump.exe -p` on the three exes, copy the four DLLs from C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin next to them in a temp folder with spaces, then from PowerShell `$env:PATH='C:\Windows\System32'; .\ndstool.exe -V; .\grit.exe -V; .\mmutil.exe` expecting exit 0, delete the DLLs and confirm ndstool/grit exit 0xC0000135; keep that as the tools-pack CI test.

Imports: ndstool.exe (235,520 B) -> libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, KERNEL32, api-ms-win-crt-*; grit.exe (497,664 B) -> libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, KERNEL32, api-ms-win-crt-*; mmutil.exe (257,024 B) -> KERNEL32 + api-ms-win-crt-* only. No msys-2.0.dll anywhere (only wf-pacman.exe imports msys-2.0.dll), no libpng/zlib DLL (grit links them statically). libstdc++-6.dll additionally pulls libwinpthread-1.dll. That package contains exactly bin/libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libgomp-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll, libzstd.dll, installed to /opt/wonderful/bin (= C:\msys64\opt\wonderful\bin). The four needed DLLs total ~3.8 MB; exes ~1 MB; tools pack ~5 MB plus arm7_maxmod.elf.

Negative test without DLLs: ndstool and grit exit 3221225781 (0xC0000135 STATUS_DLL_NOT_FOUND), mmutil still runs. Note: ndstool -c without -7 fails 'FATAL: No arm7 specified and BLOCKSDS missing from environment!' (plan already passes -7 explicitly) and -b icons must be 32x32.
- **Local experiment: C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe on the real ndstool.exe and on a freshly built ucrt64 g++ test exe, from the Bash tool (Git Bash, PATH has /mingw64/bin = C:\Program Files\Git\mingw64\bin) vs with /c/msys64/ucrt64/bin prepended**: With Git's PATH ordering ldd reports libgcc_s_seh-1.dll, libiconv-2.dll, libstdc++-6.dll, libwinpthread-1.dll => /c/Program Files/Git/mingw64/bin/*.dll (msvcrt-flavour: that libstdc++-6.dll imports msvcrt.dll, not UCRT). With ucrt64 first it reports /ucrt64/bin/*.dll (POSIX paths, need cygpath). ldd also lists ntdll, KERNEL32, KERNELBASE, ucrtbase, msvcrt which must be filtered out. ldd loads the target under the debugger (marker file not created, so main() does not run). SHA-256 of the same DLL names differ across runtime-gcc-libs, C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin and Git mingw64 (three distinct builds).
- **Local experiment run3.js: the UCRT exes with Git's msvcrt-flavour DLLs beside them, clean PATH**: ndstool -i / -c and grit run to completion and the GRF is byte-identical to the correct-DLL run. So a wrong-CRT tools pack produced by an unguarded 'copy what ldd lists' script passes a smoke test silently; the mismatch is latent, not caught by the M6 clean-VM check.
- **GitHub API https://api.github.com/repos/blocksds/{ndstool,grit,mmutil}/license**: blocksds/ndstool: GPL-3.0 (COPYING.gpl3). blocksds/grit: GPL-2.0 (COPYING). blocksds/mmutil: ISC (COPYING, Copyright 2008 Mukunda Johnson). Matches the claim. The installed package ships only cc0-1.0.txt, mit.txt, zlib.txt under core/licenses; no GPL text, so the tools pack must source licence texts from the repos. Package URL is https://codeberg.org/blocksds/sdk (Codeberg blocks scraping).
- **https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-faq.en.html (MereAggregation, GPLPlugins)**: 'Pipes, sockets and command-line arguments are communication mechanisms normally used between two separate programs. So when they are used for communication, the modules normally are separate programs.' 'A main program that uses simple fork and exec to invoke plug-ins and does not establish intimate communication between them results in the plug-ins being a separate program.' The IDE spawns the tools with argv and files on disk, so no GPL obligation on the IDE; distributing the GPL binaries themselves still triggers GPLv2 s3 / GPLv3 s6 source-offer duties for those binaries.
- **Local machine state**: C:\msys64\opt\wonderful does not exist (BlocksDS not installed); C:\msys64\usr\bin\ldd.exe exists; C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin has objdump.exe and the four DLL names but no libpng; C:\devkitPro has only 4 binutils. Side note: ucrt64 gcc.exe invoked by absolute path without ucrt64\bin on PATH exits 1 silently (cc1plus DLLs), so 'spawn native .exe by absolute path' only works when every DLL sits beside the exe or PATH is set.

## 8. npm version pins and compatibility

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** These npm package versions exist on the registry today (2026-09-24) and are mutually compatible as pinned: electron 44.4.5, electron-vite 5.0.0, vite 7.3.6, react 19.3.0, typescript 7.0.2, monaco-editor 0.57.0, pixi.js 8.21.0, dockview 8.3.1, zustand 5.0.15, immer 11.1.18, zod 4.6.5, tailwindcss 4.3.3, radix-ui 1.6.7, pngjs 7.0.0, wavefile 11.0.0, @audio/decode 3.15.0, chokidar 5.0.0, tree-kill 1.2.2, vitest 5.0.1, @vitest/browser-playwright 5.0.1, @playwright/test 1.63.0, @biomejs/biome 2.5.14, electron-builder 26.15.3, electron-updater 6.8.9; none of them requires native compilation (node-gyp) on Windows; Monaco 0.57 works in an Electron renderer with sandbox + contextIsolation using `?worker` Vite imports; Playwright `_electron` can drive an electron-vite app; TypeScript 7 (the Go-based compiler) is usable for `tsc -b --noEmit`.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** All 24 pinned versions exist on npm (checked 2026-09-24) and their peer/engine ranges are mutually satisfied on Node 24.16 (electron-vite 5.0.0 peer vite ^5||^6||^7; vitest 5.0.1 peer vite ^6.4||^7||^8, node ^22.12||^24; @vitest/browser-playwright 5.0.1 peer vitest 5.0.1 exactly plus a non-optional `playwright` peer that @playwright/test 1.63.0 supplies; radix-ui 1.6.7 peer react ^19; zustand 5.0.15 peer immer >=9.0.6). Add react-dom 19.3.0, @types/react 19.3.0, @types/react-dom 19.3.0 and @tailwindcss/vite 4.3.3. Replace `dockview 8.3.1` with `dockview-react 8.3.1` (since dockview 7.x the `dockview` package is a pure re-export of dockview-core with no React components and no react peer; dockview-react 8.3.1 has peer react/react-dom ^16.8||^17||^18||^19, depends on dockview ^8.3.1; CSS stays `dockview/dist/styles/dockview.css`). Nothing in the set compiles native code with node-gyp on install: TypeScript 7 ships a Go executable (@typescript/typescript-win32-x64), Biome/esbuild/rollup/@tailwindcss/oxide ship prebuilt win32-x64 binaries via optionalDependencies, and Electron (since 42) has no postinstall and downloads its binary on the first `electron` run (`npx install-electron` to prefetch; ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD is no longer honoured); set electron-builder `npmRebuild: false` so @electron/rebuild/node-gyp are never invoked. Monaco 0.57.0: since 0.56.0 the package has an `exports` map (`"./*": "./esm/vs/*.js"`), so the plan's `monaco-editor/esm/vs/editor/editor.api` and `monaco-editor/esm/vs/editor/editor.worker?worker` imports fail to resolve in Vite ('Failed to resolve import', monaco issue #5480); use `import * as monaco from 'monaco-editor/editor'` plus `import 'monaco-editor/features/register.all'` (or individual `monaco-editor/features/<suggest|hover|parameterHints|find|folding|documentSymbols|gotoSymbol|referenceSearch|rename|format|codeAction|semanticTokens|...>/register` — the editor entry alone has no completion/hover/find widgets) and `import EditorWorker from 'monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker'; self.MonacoEnvironment = { getWorker: () => new EditorWorker() }`. Electron sandbox + contextIsolation do not restrict renderer Web Workers; keep the renderer CSP at `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` (Vite injects <style> tags in dev) and set `sandbox: true` explicitly because electron-vite's template ships `sandbox: false`. Playwright 1.63.0 `_electron` (experimental, Electron >=12.2, nodeCliInspect fuse must stay enabled) drives the app only after `electron-vite build`, via `electron.launch({ args: ['.'], cwd: 'apps/ide' })` (package.json main -> ./out/main/index.js); electron-vite has no official e2e guidance. TypeScript 7.0.2's native `tsc` accepts `-b --noEmit`, but any project that is referenced by another must emit (TS6310 'Referenced project may not disable emit'), so for the packages/* reference graph use `composite: true` + `emitDeclarationOnly: true` in every package and run plain `tsc -b` (emits only .d.ts + .tsbuildinfo), or drop `references` and run `tsc -p <pkg> --noEmit` per package (or one solution tsconfig with `paths` and no cross-references); TS 7.0 has no programmatic API (7.1), so any TS-API tool needs the `typescript@npm:@typescript/typescript6` alias. Replace `@audio/decode 3.15.0` (its hard dependencies include GPL-2.0 @audio/decode-aac and GPL-2.0-or-later -ac3/-dts/-wma, LGPL -eac3) with direct dependencies on `@audio/decode-wav 1.5.0` (MIT, pure JS) and `@audio/decode-mp3 1.3.1` (MIT, WASM), adding `@audio/decode-vorbis 1.3.2` / `-flac 1.3.3` / `-opus 1.3.1` only if needed (MIT wrappers that pull LGPL-3.0 codec-parser).

**Impact on the plan.** WS6 IDE skeleton: pin `dockview-react` 8.3.1 (not `dockview`) or the React panels/popouts do not exist; rewrite the Monaco host to the `monaco-editor/editor` + `monaco-editor/features/register.all` + `monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker` entry points (the research doc's snippet and Monaco's own Vite sample use pre-0.56 paths that break the Vite build); explicitly set `sandbox: true` and keep `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` in the CSP. Phase 0 'Biome + Vitest + tsc -b wiring': `tsc -b --noEmit` cannot type-check the planned cross-referenced packages; switch to composite + emitDeclarationOnly + `tsc -b` (declaration outputs become part of the per-package build artefacts/gitignore) or per-package `tsc --noEmit`. Asset pipeline (WS for audio): the 'MIT' @audio/decode umbrella would put GPL-2.0 code into node_modules and the packaged installer, contradicting the plan's rejection of ffmpeg-static/libimagequant on licence grounds; depend on the individual MIT codec packages. Packaging/CI: add `npmRebuild: false`, and account for Electron's first-run binary download (run `npx install-electron` in setup/CI; no ELECTRON_SKIP_BINARY_DOWNLOAD). E2E: Playwright smoke must run `electron-vite build` first and launch `out/main/index.js` (dev mode depends on the ELECTRON_RENDERER_URL env var that only `electron-vite dev` sets). The plan's 'zero native Node modules' wording should read 'no node-gyp compilation; only prebuilt dev-tool binaries (TS 7, Biome, esbuild, rollup, @tailwindcss/oxide)'.

**Residual risk.** Only an actual `npm install` + `electron-vite build` in the workspace settles: (1) whether Vite 7.3.6's resolver and the `?worker` transform accept `monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker` end-to-end and the IIFE worker chunk loads under the planned production `app://` custom scheme (the scheme must be registered `standard`+`secure` for workers/storage; a `file://` fallback should be kept for the spike); (2) Monaco 0.57 rendering under the strict CSP with a sandboxed CJS preload in Electron 44 (style injection, Trusted Types); (3) Playwright 1.63.0's experimental `_electron.launch` actually attaching to the electron-vite build on this Windows 11 machine (fuse and first-window timing); (4) `tsc -b` with `composite`+`emitDeclarationOnly`+`paths`+`moduleResolution: bundler` across the real 7-package graph, including watch/incremental behaviour of the Go compiler; (5) dockview-react 8.3.1 popout windows under `setWindowOpenHandler` + sandbox; (6) `@vitest/browser-playwright` needing `npx playwright install chromium` (a download, not a compile) and running WebGL (PixiJS) headless on this GPU. None of these can be resolved by documentation alone; they are Phase-0 spike items.

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Keep all 24 pins (they exist and resolve together with no peer conflicts, and nothing needs node-gyp/a C++ toolchain), but change three statements/commands in the plan:

1. TypeScript: replace "TypeScript 7.0.2 (`tsc -b --noEmit` only)" with "TypeScript 7.0.2 for type-checking via `tsc -b` (build mode; `--noEmit` cannot be combined with composite project references - TS6310 on both TS 6.0.2 and 7.0.2). Each package's tsconfig sets `composite: true, declaration: true, emitDeclarationOnly: true, outDir: './dist-types', tsBuildInfoFile: './dist-types/tsconfig.tsbuildinfo'` (gitignored); root `npm run check` = `tsc -b` (optionally `tsc -b --builders 4`). `--build`/`-b` must be the first argument. No TS programmatic API in 7.0 (7.1 will ship one); use `@typescript/typescript6` only if a tool needs the API."

2. Monaco: replace every `monaco-editor/esm/vs/...` import in research-idestack.md section 3 and the unified plan with the 0.56+ entry points:
   `import * as monaco from 'monaco-editor/editor';`
   `import 'monaco-editor/features/register.all';` (or selected `monaco-editor/features/<feature>/register`)
   `import EditorWorker from 'monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker';`
   `self.MonacoEnvironment = { getWorker: () => new EditorWorker() };`
   Do NOT copy `samples/browser-esm-vite-react/src/userWorker.ts` from the repo (stale at v0.57.0, still uses `monaco-editor/esm/vs/...`, which fails under the 0.56+ exports map). Keep CSP `worker-src 'self' blob:` and `base: './'`. Consider pinning monaco-editor 0.56.0 as the fallback if 0.57.0 (published hours ago) shows regressions.

3. Electron binary: add to the root package.json `"scripts": { "postinstall": "install-electron" }` (Electron 42+ has no postinstall; the binary is fetched lazily only by `require('electron')`, and electron-vite 5.0.0's `getElectronPath()` throws `Error('Electron uninstall')` when `node_modules/electron/path.txt` is missing). This makes "`npm install && npm run check && npm test` green on a clean clone" true and stops Playwright's `require('electron/index.js')` from doing a 158 MB synchronous download inside its launch timeout. Document `ELECTRON_OVERRIDE_DIST_PATH`/`electron_config_cache` for offline setups.

Playwright statement stays as written, but add: "Playwright's Electron driver is officially experimental; it launches with `--inspect=0 --remote-debugging-port=0`, so the `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` fuse must stay enabled on test builds (already noted)."

**Impact on the plan.** (a) Every package tsconfig and the root `check` script must use `tsc -b` with an emitted (gitignored) declaration output instead of `tsc -b --noEmit`; the CI byte-identical/regeneration checks and the pre-commit hook that run type-checks need the new command; per-package `dist-types/` must be added to .gitignore and Biome ignore lists. (b) WS6 (IDE shell / Monaco) integration code and the `@dsdude/language-service` Monaco adapter must use `monaco-editor/editor` + `features/*/register` + `monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker`; the bundle-size estimate (~4-5 MB importing `editor.api` only) should be re-measured with `monaco-editor/editor` + selected features, which is now the supported tree-shaking path. (c) WS0's bootstrap (Phase 0 deliverables, 'Definition of done' line 271: `npm install && npm run check && npm test` on a clean clone) and the kickoff prompts for every worktree need the `postinstall: install-electron` step, otherwise `electron-vite dev` and the first Playwright run fail on a fresh checkout/worktree with 'Electron uninstall' or a launch timeout. None of the version pins themselves need to change.

**Residual risk.** Not settled here (needs the real app running): (1) Monaco 0.57.0 inside an Electron 44 renderer with sandbox:true + contextIsolation + CSP `worker-src 'self' blob:` actually spawning the `?worker` editor worker both under `electron-vite dev` (module worker over http://localhost) and in the packaged build (iife worker over file:// or the custom app:// scheme registered with standard/secure/supportFetchAPI) - no primary source contradicts it, but 0.57.0 is hours old and untested by anyone. (2) Playwright 1.63.0 `_electron.launch` against Electron 44's Chromium 152 - Playwright's Electron path is experimental and has broken on a major bump before (Electron 36). (3) TS 7.0.2 strict-by-default type-checking of @types/react 19.3 / pixi.js 8.21 / monaco.d.ts 0.57 / zod 4.6 may surface new diagnostics or unsupported tsconfig options. (4) Electron's first-run download (158 MB, GitHub Releases) behind any proxy. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (about 1 hour): scaffold apps/ide with electron-vite 5.0.0 + the pinned versions in C:\dev\dsdude, add `postinstall: install-electron`, run `npm install`; create packages/a and packages/b with a reference and confirm `tsc -b` (not --noEmit) type-checks and reports an injected error; mount one Monaco editor via `monaco-editor/editor` + `features/register.all` + `editor.worker?worker` in a sandboxed BrowserWindow with the CSP meta and verify in DevTools that a dedicated worker appears and no 'MonacoEnvironment.getWorker' fallback warning is logged, in both `npm run dev` and `electron-vite build` + `electron out/main/index.js`; then run one `@playwright/test` spec doing `_electron.launch({ args: ['.'] })` + `firstWindow()` + a title assertion.
