Custom bundles can now import `monaco-editor/editor` and opt into all editor features with `monaco-editor/features/register.all`, or individual features with `monaco-editor/features/<feature>/register`...'; also removes IMirrorModel/IWorkerContext worker types and renames stackOridinal->stackOrdinal. 0.57.0 has no breaking changes (VS Code core update, DOMPurify 3.4.15, new editor options). The repo's own Vite sample at v0.57.0 STILL uses `import editorWorker from 'monaco-editor/esm/vs/editor/editor.worker?worker'` and pins monaco ^0.32 in its package.json, i.e. it is stale and must not be copied verbatim. PR #5480 (open as of 2026-09-16, tested with Vite 7.3.6) fixes the docs to `monaco-editor/editor/editor.worker?worker` and `monaco-editor/languages/features/<lang>/<lang>.worker?worker`, stating the old `monaco-editor/esm/vs/...` paths 'no longer resolved due to changes in the 0.56.0 exports map'. README at v0.57.0: AMD build deprecated; monaco.d.ts is the only versioned API, 'everything else is considered private'.
- **electron@44.4.5 tarball (npm pack into scratchpad/electron-pack): package.json, install.js, index.js, cli.js; electron@39.0.0 scripts for comparison; electronjs.org/docs/latest/breaking-changes**: electron@44.4.5 package.json has NO `scripts` field (electron@39.0.0 had `postinstall: node install.js`). bin = {electron: cli.js, install-electron: install.js}. index.js: on `require('electron')` it reads path.txt and, if dist/ is missing, runs `spawnSync(process.execPath, ['install.js'])` ('Downloading Electron binary...'); on failure it says 'Please delete node_modules/electron and run "npx install-electron --no" manually'. Breaking-changes doc (v42.0): 'Previously, the electron npm package would download the Electron binary ... in the package's postinstall script. Electron will now download itself dynamically the first time that its main bin script is run (e.g. via npx electron)... you can now call the install-electron script.' v44.0: Windows x86 (ia32) prebuilts removed (irrelevant, plan is x64 NSIS); `clipboard` module no longer exposed to renderers. Electron 44 abi_version 149; checksums.json lists electron-v44.4.5-win32-x64.zip.
- **electron-vite@5.0.0 tarball (npm pack into scratchpad/ev-pack): dist/chunks/lib-q6ns0vZr.js lines 141-160 and 218-240; CHANGELOG v5.0.0; npm dist-tags; web search for 'Electron uninstall'**: electron-vite 5.0.0 `getElectronPath()` = `path.dirname(require.resolve('electron'))` + read `path.txt`; if the file is absent it `throw new Error('Electron uninstall')`. It never `require('electron')`s, so Electron 44's lazy download is never triggered by `electron-vite dev`/`preview`. Consequence: on a clean clone, `npm install` (no postinstall in electron 44) followed by `npm run dev` fails with 'Electron uninstall' until `npx install-electron` (or any `require('electron')`) has run. Community reports match (marktext#5475, oxwen11/pie#211 with electron ~42.1 + electron-vite 5.0.0). electron-vite 5.0.0 is still `latest` (2025-12-07); only 6.0.0-beta.1 (2026-04-12) is newer, so no stable fix exists. Also: its Electron->Node/Chrome build-target tables end at Electron 39; for 44 it falls back to `node22.20` / `chrome142` (conservative, safe). Its `--noSandbox` CLI flag only affects the Chromium sandbox flag; `@swc/core` peer is optional.
- **playwright-core@1.63.0 src/server/electron/electron.ts (raw from GitHub tag v1.63.0), playwright.dev/docs/api/class-electron, microsoft/playwright#30755, electron/electron#47419**: Launch args are `['--inspect=0', '--remote-debugging-port=0', ...options.args]`; default executable comes from `require('electron/index.js')` ('resolves to the actual Electron App') - with Electron 44 that require itself triggers the synchronous 158 MB download if dist/ is missing, inside Playwright's launch timeout window; for non-packaged apps it prepends `-r <playwright>/loader.js`. On win32 it always uses `shell: true` and joins command+args into one double-quoted string (spaces-safe; the 1.44 regression #30755 was fixed via PR #30820). Docs: 'Playwright has experimental support for Electron automation', minimum Electron 12.2/13.4/14+, `chromiumSandbox` default false, and the `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` fuse must not be false or launch times out (plan already notes this). electron/electron#47419 (Electron 36 broke electron.launch on Linux CI) was resolved by Electron 37 - shows major bumps can break the experimental path, but nothing Electron-44-specific was found. Driving an electron-vite app is just `_electron.launch({ args: ['.'] })` after `electron-vite build` (package.json main -> out/main/index.js).
- **electron-builder 26.15.3 / electron-updater 6.8.9 metadata and lockfile**: electron-builder pulls @electron/rebuild 4.2.0 (with node-gyp 12.4.0) and app-builder-lib 26.15.3 with its own @electron/get 3.1.0 (downloads its own Electron zip, independent of node_modules/electron/dist). electron-updater 6.8.9 deps are pure JS (builder-util-runtime 9.7.0, fs-extra, js-yaml, semver...). Electron 44's ia32 removal only matters for ia32 NSIS targets; the plan targets x64 only.

</details>

## 9. maxmod soundbank via mmutil

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** BlocksDS 1.24 maxmod with the prebuilt `sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf` plays WAV effects converted by BlocksDS's mmutil (`mmutil -d <files> -o soundbank.bin -h soundbank.h`) from 22050 Hz mono 16-bit WAV files (with an optional `smpl` loop chunk), loaded at runtime via `mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin")` + `mmLoadEffect(id)` + `mmEffect(id)`, and mmutil assigns ids in the order files are given on the command line, matching the generated soundbank.h defines; XM/MOD/IT/S3M modules play via `mmLoad`/`mmStart`.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** BlocksDS 1.24.0 (wf-pacman package blocksds-toolchain 1.24.0-1) ships `$(BLOCKSDS)/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf` (the rom_arm9 Makefile default ARM7ELF) and the ARM9 links with `LIBS := -lnds9 -lmm9` plus `LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod` (not added by default). Build the soundbank with the BlocksDS mmutil using ATTACHED option values: `mmutil -d <files> -osoundbank.bin -hsoundbank.h` (a space after -o/-h makes the path empty and turns `soundbank.bin`/`soundbank.h` into input files). mmutil reads PCM (format 1) WAVs of 8 or 16 bits at any sample rate; stereo is averaged to mono; 16-bit data stays 16-bit on NDS; the first `smpl` loop (forward or bidi) is honoured, loops shorter than 16 samples are dropped, audio after the loop end is discarded and loop points are aligned to 2 samples. IDs are one counter in command-line order, but samples embedded in XM/MOD/IT/S3M modules consume SFX ids too (BlocksDS tutorial: `SFX_FIRE_EXPLOSION 20` after two XMs), so read ids from the generated `soundbank.h` (`#define SFX_<BASENAME_UPPER>    N\r\n`, name cut at the first '.', non-alphanumerics -> '_', basename < 64 chars) or pass all WAVs before any module. At runtime: `nitroFSInit(NULL)` -> `soundEnable()` -> `mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin")` (it fopen()s the path; path <= 64 chars) -> `mmLoadEffect(SFX_x)` (0 = ok) -> `mmEffect(SFX_x)`; modules: `mmLoad(MOD_x)` then `mmStart(MOD_x, MM_PLAY_LOOP|MM_PLAY_ONCE)`.

**Impact on the plan.** (1) The mmutil invocation in the claim would silently produce a broken build; the plan's own section 2.9 already uses the attached form (`-obuild/nitrofs/soundbank.bin -hbuild/soundbank.h`) so make sure `runMmutil()` / ToolPaths wrapper never inserts a space and runs with cwd set to a writable build dir (mkstemp temp files land in cwd). (2) Contract C3 'soundbank.bin (ids in sorted-name order)' is only true if every WAV precedes every module on the command line; since WS5 already parses soundbank.h, state that the header (CRLF lines) is the source of truth and/or order WAVs first, and enforce asset basenames < 64 chars with no '.' inside. (3) WS3's Makefile snippet (`LIBS := -lmm9 -lnds9 -lc`) must add `LIBDIRS += $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod` or the link fails. (4) The selftest/platform layer must call nitroFSInit before mmInitDefault and check mmLoadEffect's return (1 = bad id, 2 = load failed). (5) mmutil exits 0 and prints 'Unknown file' for unrecognised extensions, so the pipeline should validate expected SFX_/MOD_ defines exist rather than trust the exit code. (6) The soundbank must be produced by BlocksDS's mmutil (MAS 0x19), never devkitPro's.

**Residual risk.** Actual audible playback of a 22050 Hz 16-bit looped WAV through arm7_maxmod.elf in DeSmuME 0.9.13 and melonDS with the 1.24.0 binaries can only be confirmed by building examples/maxmod/nitrofs plus a WAV-with-smpl fixture and running it (upstream states Maxmod examples are not covered by its automated tests, and 1.24.0 changed mmutil's instrument resampling code). Also unverifiable without install: that the wf-pacman package really places arm7_maxmod.elf and tools/mmutil/mmutil.exe at the expected Windows paths, and mmutil.exe's DLL dependencies.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **blocksds/mmutil source/main.c lines 55-56, 172-206 (raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/mmutil/master/source/main.c)**: Usage table is `-o<output>` / `-h<header>`; the parser reads the value as `argv[a] + 2` (attached, no space). Any argv not starting with '-' is counted as an input file. So `-o soundbank.bin -h soundbank.h` (with spaces) sets an empty output/header path and treats `soundbank.bin` and `soundbank.h` as input files. The readme and the SDK Makefile (rom_arm9/Makefile line 323-324: `mmutil $^ -d -o$(SOUNDBANKDIR)/soundbank.bin -h$(SOUNDBANKINFODIR)/soundbank.h`) both use the attached form.
- **blocksds/mmutil source/msl.c lines 61-81, 168-188, 254-292, 332-337, 400-410**: MSL_Create walks argv in order and loads every non-dash argument; MSL_AddSample returns `MSL_NSAMPS - 1` (single incrementing counter). A WAV input gets `#define SFX_<NAME>    <id>` with that counter, so soundbank.h always matches. BUT MSL_AddModule also adds every sample embedded in a module (deduplicated via MSL_AddSampleC) to the same counter, so a WAV listed after a module does not get id = its index among WAVs. Define name = basename, uppercased, cut at first '.', non-alphanumerics -> '_', 64-byte buffer; lines end with \r\n; header also gets MSL_NSONGS/MSL_NSAMPS/MSL_BANKSIZE.
- **BlocksDS tutorial docs/content/tutorial/basic/audio/index.md lines 117-125 (sdk repo)**: With audio files fire_explosion.wav, lasse_haen_pyykit.xm, parallax_80599.xm the generated header is `MOD_PARALLAX_80599 0`, `MOD_LASSE_HAEN_PYYKIT 1`, `SFX_FIRE_EXPLOSION 20` — official confirmation that module-embedded samples consume SFX ids ahead of standalone WAVs.
- **blocksds/mmutil source/wav.c lines 58-104, 106-153, 155-222**: WAV loader accepts only fmt compression code 1 (PCM), bit depth 8 or 16 (else LOADWAV_UNSUPPORTED_BD), any channel count (channels averaged to mono), any sample rate (read from fmt). `smpl` chunk: only the first loop is used; type 0=forward, 1=bidi (values >=2 ignored); loop_end clipped to sample length; loops with (end-start) < 16 samples or start > length are discarded. 22050 Hz mono 16-bit is therefore supported.
- **blocksds/mmutil source/samplefix.c lines 319-408 and source/mas.c lines 253-306**: NDS path keeps 16-bit data as 16-bit (Sample_8bit is only called in FixSample_GBA); data after loop_end is deleted; bidi loops unrolled; loop start/length aligned to 2 samples (16-bit) by unrolling (<=1024 extra samples) or resampling; samples converted to signed. Header stores rate as (freq*1024+16384)/32768 -> 689 for 22050 Hz (~22048 Hz). %c/%o flags are taken from the sample *name*, which Load_WAV zeroes, so they never apply to standalone WAVs.
- **blocksds/maxmod include/maxmod9.h lines 50, 149, 174, 308, 523; include/mm_types.h lines 47, 170-175**: `bool mmInitDefault(const char* soundbank_file)`, `mm_word mmLoad(mm_word module_ID)`, `mm_word mmLoadEffect(mm_word sample_ID)` (doc: 'If the sound bank is in the filesystem, this function will load the required data from the sound bank and save it in RAM'), `void mmStart(mm_word module_ID, mm_pmode mode)` with MM_PLAY_LOOP/MM_PLAY_ONCE, `mm_sfxhand mmEffect(mm_word sample_ID)` ('For DS, make sure the sample is loaded (mmLoadEffect)'; returns MM_SFXHAND_INVALID on error).
- **blocksds/maxmod source/ds/arm9/main_ds9.c lines 119-141; source/ds/arm9/soundbank.c lines 17, 58-119, 157-170; memory.c lines 94-117**: mmInitDefault does `fopen(soundbank_file, "rb")`, reads the first word (sample/module counts), calloc's the bank table and registers the file handler; the path is copied into a 64-char buffer (MM_FILENAME_SIZE). mmLoadEffect returns 1 if id >= sample count, 2 if the file load fails; each load re-opens the soundbank file and malloc's the sample. Hence a filesystem (nitroFSInit) must be up before mmInitDefault.
- **blocksds/sdk examples/maxmod/nitrofs/source/main.c lines 50-73, 75-83, 113, and its Makefile**: Official order: `nitroFSInit(NULL)` -> `soundEnable()` -> `mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin")` -> `mmLoad(MOD_...)` -> `mmStart(MOD_..., MM_PLAY_LOOP)`. Makefile: `AUDIODIRS := audio`, `NITROFSDIR := nitrofs`, `LIBS := -lnds9 -lmm9`, `LIBDIRS := $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/maxmod`.
- **blocksds/sdk sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile lines 52-56, 107, 187-191, 319-324**: Default `ARM7ELF ?= $(BLOCKSDS)/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf`; default `LIBS ?= -lnds9` and `LIBDIRS += $(BLOCKSDS)/libs/libnds` only (maxmod's LIBDIR must be added by the project); AUDIODIRS globs .it/.mod/.s3m/.wav/.xm; with NITROFSDIR set the soundbank is written into NitroFS and ndstool gets `-d`.
- **blocksds/sdk sys/arm7/main_core/Makefile lines 26-58, 95-103; Makefile.generic lines 44-50, 87-90; source/main.c lines 22-23, 99-101**: Targets minimal/dswifi/maxmod/libxm7/dswifi_maxmod/dswifi_libxm7 (+ _debug) build `arm7_$(NAME).elf`; `install` copies `arm7_*.elf` to `<INSTALLDIR>/sys/arm7/main_core/`. USE_MAXMOD=1 links -lmm7 and the ARM7 main() calls `mmInstall(FIFO_MAXMOD)`. The .elf files are build outputs, not in the git tree (codeberg contents API lists only .gitignore, COPYING, Makefile, Makefile.generic, source/).
- **https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/blocksds/sdk/releases (v1.24.0, published 2026-09-21) and https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/blocksds.db**: BlocksDS v1.24.0 exists; wf-pacman repo carries `blocksds-toolchain-1.24.0-1-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz` (sha256 23f78983...). 1.24.0 notes: mmutil 'Improve code that resamples instruments', '%o/%c must be in the instrument name, NDS hardware mode only', XM pattern fix; and 'Currently there is no way to test audio-related examples, so the examples about libnds audio, Maxmod and LibXM7 aren't automated yet.'
- **blocksds/mmutil source/version.h; source/msl.c lines 46-47, 347-377, 412**: BlocksDS mmutil emits MAS_VERSION 0x19 (devkitPro-compat build 0x18) — soundbank must come from BlocksDS's mmutil. mmutil creates mkstemp temp files `mm_samp_tmp.XXXXXXX`/`mm_song_tmp.XXXXXXX` in the current working directory; in soundbank mode the output is overwritten without the interactive y/n prompt (prompt exists only for -m/-b).
- **npm view wavefile (11.0.0) and rochars/wavefile README ('Sample loops' section, line 184)**: wavefile 11.0.0 exposes `wav.smpl.loops[]` with dwType/dwStart/dwEnd and writes 16-bit PCM as format 1 (WAVE_FORMAT_EXTENSIBLE only for non-standard resolutions), so the plan's WAV writer output is in the subset mmutil reads.

</details>

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Command line must use ATTACHED option values, exactly as BlocksDS's own Makefile does: `mmutil <wav files sorted by name> <module files> -d -o<dir>\soundbank.bin -h<dir>\soundbank.h` (e.g. `mmutil -d jump.wav explode.wav music.xm -obuild\nitrofs\soundbank.bin -hbuild\soundbank.h`). `-o soundbank.bin` with a space makes `soundbank.bin` an input file and produces nothing while still exiting 0. Id rule: SFX_ ids are assigned in command-line order but share one counter with the samples embedded in modules, so they are contiguous 0..n-1 only if every WAV precedes every module on the command line; MOD_ ids are always 0..m-1 in module order. Always read ids from soundbank.h rather than assuming them. Runtime sequence: nitroFSInit(NULL); soundEnable(); mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin") (check bool); mmLoadEffect(SFX_x) (check == 0); mmEffect(SFX_x); mmLoad(MOD_x); mmStart(MOD_x, MM_PLAY_LOOP). Wrapper rules: run mmutil with cwd set to a writable build dir (it mkstemp's mm_samp_tmp.*/mm_song_tmp.* there); delete or temp-name the outputs before running and treat a missing/unchanged soundbank.bin or soundbank.h as failure regardless of exit code (mmutil exits 0 when it cannot open the header file, and leaves a partial header when it exits 1 on a bad input); WAVs must be PCM tag 1, 8/16-bit, with only fmt/smpl/data chunks (mmutil is not RIFF pad-byte aware, so odd-length LIST/INFO chunks make it reject the file); loop length >= 16 samples; stems ASCII, <= 63 chars, no '.' inside the stem (name is cut at the first dot; 64-byte buffer); parse soundbank.h with CRLF tolerance. Use the mmutil.exe shipped in the same BlocksDS release as libmm9 (1.24.0 writes MAS 0x18; master already writes 0x19) and rebuild soundbanks on toolchain upgrades.

**Impact on the plan.** Section 2.9 of the unified plan already uses the attached form (`-obuild/nitrofs/soundbank.bin -hbuild/soundbank.h`), so only the claim wording is wrong there. Contract C3 ("soundbank.bin (ids in sorted-name order)") must be qualified: the asset pipeline must pass all WAVs (sorted) before all modules, and assets.manifest.json must take ids from the parsed soundbank.h, never from list position, otherwise any project with a tracker module listed before a WAV gets wrong effect ids at runtime with no build error. WS5's `runMmutil` wrapper needs: writable cwd, pre-deleted/temp-named outputs, output-existence check independent of exit code, stdout parsing for "Can't open"/"Unknown file", WAV writer restricted to fmt+smpl+data (wavefile 11 fromScratch does this), stem sanitisation (<=63 chars, no dots, ASCII), CRLF-tolerant header parser, and a check that the vendored mmutil.exe's `-V` matches the BlocksDS release of the runtime's libmm9 (tools-pack.json should record it). WS3's platform layer must call soundEnable() before mmInitDefault and check the return values of mmInitDefault/mmLoadEffect (1 = bad id, 2 = load failed) so a bad soundbank surfaces as a DSD| diagnostic instead of silence. Good news for WS1: the shipped mmutil.exe is statically linked (no ucrt64 DLLs to copy) and works from Node spawn with a minimal env and paths containing spaces.

**Residual risk.** Everything tool-side was exercised with the real 1.24.0 Windows binaries, but actual playback (ARM7 hardware-mode mixing of a 16-bit looped 22050 Hz sample, loop points honoured, pitch correct, mmInitDefault's fopen of nitro:/soundbank.bin from an ndstool-packed two-ELF ROM) can only be settled by running a ROM. Cheapest Phase-0 spike (after the planned toolchain install): build $BLOCKSDS/examples/maxmod/nitrofs and examples/maxmod/sound_effects unchanged, run in melonDS; then copy sound_effects, switch it to NITROFSDIR + mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin"), drop in the wavefile-generated 22050 Hz looped fixture (scratchpad\mm\wv\wf_loop.wav: 440 Hz tone, loop 1000..5000) plus one XM, press a key to mmLoadEffect+mmEffect, and confirm in melonDS that the tone sustains (loop works) at 440 Hz and that mmLoadEffect returns 0; also confirm `mmutil -V` of the installed toolchain reports v1.24.0 and that the header ids match the manifest for a WAV+XM mix. Also unverified here: behaviour of maxmod when many effects are loaded (RAM is malloc'ed per sample; the plan's 1 MB soundbank budget must fit the 0.5-1 MB maxmod allowance).

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **BlocksDS package DB https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/packages/rolling/windows/x86_64/blocksds.db + .files, and the actual package blocksds-toolchain-1.24.0-1-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz (sha256 23f78983ac9706b2da4e91ec30d8c412cbc387d7ded42097e98a8daead6d3af7) downloaded to scratchpad\mm\wf\**: 1.24.0-1 Windows package ships thirdparty/blocksds/core/tools/mmutil/mmutil.exe (257 KB, `mmutil -V` -> `mmutil v1.24.0-dirty`, ldd shows only system DLLs), sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf (+_debug), libs/maxmod/lib/libmm9.a, include/maxmod9.h. arm-none-eabi-nm on arm7_maxmod.elf shows mmInit7/mmEffect/mmEffectEx/... at 0x037fxxxx; sys/arm7/main_core/source/main.c calls enableSound(); fifoInit(); installSoundFIFO(); mmInstall(FIFO_MAXMOD).
- **mmutil source (codeberg blocksds/mmutil master, source/main.c lines 172-206, 506-511)**: Options are parsed as `argv[a][1]` with the value attached: `-o` -> `str_output = argv[a]+2`, `-h` -> `str_header = argv[a]+2`. A separate token after `-o` is treated as an INPUT file. main() returns 0 regardless of MSL_Create's return value.
- **Experiment with the SHIPPED 1.24.0 mmutil.exe in scratchpad\mm\fx (Git Bash and Node child_process)**: `mmutil -d jump.wav loopy.wav -o sb.bin -h sb.h` (claim syntax, spaces) -> prints `Can't open output header file: ` , creates NO files, exit status 0. With `-osb.bin -hsb.h` (attached) -> soundbank.bin + soundbank.h created, exit 0. Same result with my master build of mmutil compiled from source with C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin\gcc.exe.
- **BlocksDS sys/default_makefiles/rom_arm9/Makefile lines 319-324 (codeberg raw, master)**: The SDK itself invokes `$(BLOCKSDS)/tools/mmutil/mmutil $^ -d -o$(SOUNDBANKDIR)/soundbank.bin -h$(SOUNDBANKINFODIR)/soundbank.h` (attached flags). Default ARM7ELF is `$(BLOCKSDS)/sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf`; with NITROFSDIR set the soundbank goes to build/maxmod_nitrofs and ndstool gets an extra `-d`.
- **mmutil source/msl.c (MSL_LoadFile, MSL_AddSample, MSL_AddSampleC, MSL_AddModule, MSL_PrintDefinition) + experiment with shipped binary**: Standalone WAVs get `MSL_AddSample` -> id = MSL_NSAMPS++ ; modules first add ALL their embedded samples through `MSL_AddSampleC` into the SAME counter, then get MOD_ id = MSL_NSONGS++. Files are processed in argv order. Test: `-d lasse_haen_pyykit.xm jump.wav parallax_80599.xm loopy.wav` -> `SFX_JUMP 5`, `SFX_LOOPY 21`, MOD_ 0/1; `-d jump.wav loopy.wav <xms>` -> `SFX_JUMP 0`, `SFX_LOOPY 1`. Header always matches the bank. Define name = stem uppercased, cut at the FIRST '.', non-alnum -> '_' (`dotted.v2.wav` -> SFX_DOTTED, `1up.wav` -> SFX_1UP), 64-byte stack buffer, CRLF line endings, trailer MSL_NSONGS/MSL_NSAMPS/MSL_BANKSIZE.
- **mmutil source/wav.c + source/samplefix.c (FixSample_NDS) + source/mas.c Write_SampleData; verified by parsing the produced soundbank.bin with a Python script**: WAV loader: PCM (tag 1) only, 8 or 16-bit, N channels averaged to mono, sample rate stored as u16 `(rate*1024+16384)/32768` (22050 -> 689 = ~22048 Hz; the examples' 44100 and 11025 also pass), `smpl` chunk parsed after the data chunk (works even when smpl follows data): loop types 0/1 accepted, loop_end clipped, loops < 16 samples dropped, data after loop_end discarded, loop aligned/unrolled to 2-sample words for 16-bit. NDS keeps 16-bit (dsformat=1, reptype=1 loop / 2 one-shot). Odd-sized RIFF chunks are NOT pad-byte aware: a WAV with a 17-byte LIST chunk is rejected as corrupt (exit 1).
- **wavefile 11.0.0 (npm pack into scratchpad\mm\wv, dist/wavefile.js) round trip through the shipped mmutil.exe**: fromScratch(1, 22050, '16', Int16Array) + smpl loop 1000..5000 writes chunks `fmt :16 data:12000 smpl:60`, fmt tag 1; mmutil -> `SFX_WF_LOOP 0`, sample: dsformat=1 (16-bit), loopstart_words=500 (=1000 samples), len_words=2000 (=4000 samples), dfreq=689, MAS version 0x18. wavefile pads odd byte arrays (lib/wavefile-parser.js:509).
- **Node 24.16 spawnSync test (scratchpad\mm\fx\spawn.mjs)**: spawnSync(mmutil.exe, ['-d', <abs paths with spaces>, '-o<abs path with spaces>', '-h<...>'], {cwd: 'with space', windowsHide: true, env: {SystemRoot}}) -> status 0, both files created, header `SFX_JUMP 0 / SFX_BIG_BANG 1 / SFX_LOOPY 2`. Missing input file -> exit 1 (`Can't open file for reading:`), corrupt WAV -> exit 1 but a PARTIAL soundbank.h is left behind (23 bytes). Missing output directory for -h -> exit 0 with `Can't open output header file: nope\x.h`. Temp files mm_samp_tmp.XXXXXXX / mm_song_tmp.XXXXXXX are mkstemp'ed in the process cwd and removed at exit.
- **maxmod source (codeberg blocksds/maxmod master): include/maxmod9.h, source/ds/arm9/main_ds9.c, source/ds/arm9/memory.c, source/ds/arm9/soundbank.c; documentation/ds_programming_guide.md**: `bool mmInitDefault(const char *soundbank_file)` fopen()s the file, reads first word (nsamps | nsongs<<16), calloc's the bank table, mmInit(FIFO_MAXMOD), registers the file-based callback; `mm_word mmLoadEffect(mm_word)` returns 1 if id >= sample count, 2 if the load fails, otherwise fopen/fseek/fread + malloc of that sample; `mm_sfxhand mmEffect(mm_word)`; `mm_word mmLoad(mm_word)`; `void mmStart(mm_word, mm_pmode)`. Default mode is MM_MODE_A (full hardware mixing). No MAS-version check at runtime.
- **BlocksDS examples/maxmod/nitrofs/source/main.c and examples/maxmod/sound_effects/source/main.c (+ their audio/ dirs)**: nitrofs example: nitroFSInit(NULL); soundEnable(); mmInitDefault("nitro:/soundbank.bin"); mmLoad(MOD_..); mmStart(MOD_.., MM_PLAY_LOOP); mmUnload. sound_effects example: soundEnable(); mmInitDefaultMem; mmLoad; mmLoadEffect(SFX_..); mmEffect(SFX_..). Example WAVs are 16-bit mono at 44100 Hz (fire_explosion.wav) and 11025 Hz (nature.wav), fmt+data only; both convert fine with the shipped mmutil. Note the claim omits soundEnable().
- **blocksds/mmutil commit f0b49489 (2026-09-21, 'mas: Expand size of some fields in MAS file header') and blocksds/maxmod commit 69973c44; diff of shipped 1.24.0 mm_mas.h vs master mm_mas.h; BlocksDS changelog 1.24.0 section**: Shipped 1.24.0 mmutil writes MAS version 0x18 and the shipped mm_mas.h has the matching byte-sized mm_mas_head; master already writes 0x19 with hword fields (module header layout change). 1.24.0 changelog for mmutil: improved instrument resampling, %o/%c flag docs, XM pattern fix, demo-ROM deletion — nothing that changes WAV handling. Consequence: mmutil and libmm9 must always come from the same BlocksDS release.

</details>

## 10. py-desmume headless screenshots

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** py-desmume (PyPI, current version around 0.0.9) provides a win_amd64 wheel for CPython 3.13 that can open a .nds ROM headlessly (no window), advance N frames, inject input and return a screenshot as a PIL image, suitable for CI screenshot regression of a BlocksDS NitroFS ROM.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** py-desmume 0.0.9 (latest, released 2025-12-12; `pip install py-desmume==0.0.9`; wheel `py_desmume-0.0.9-cp313-cp313-win_amd64.whl`, 5,522,695 bytes, bundles libdesmume.dll + SDL2.dll 2.0.14, requires the Microsoft VC++ 2015-2022 x64 runtime and Pillow>=6.1.0) runs headless: `emu = DeSmuME(); emu.open(rom_path); emu.input.keypad_add_key(keymask(Keys.KEY_A)); for _ in range(300): emu.cycle(with_joystick=False); emu.screenshot().save('out.png')` (no window unless `create_sdl_window()` is called; `screenshot()` returns a PIL 'RGB' 256x384 image, top screen above bottom, native resolution, SoftRasterizer, RGB555 expanded with <<3). In CI set `SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy` and `SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy` because `desmume_init` calls `SDL_Init(SDL_INIT_VIDEO|SDL_INIT_TIMER)` (a Linux container without a display otherwise fails with 'Failed to init DeSmuME'; audio failure is tolerated). Its emulator core is NOT DeSmuME 0.9.13 but the SkyTemple/desmume `interface-build` fork (upstream git as of ~April 2022, version string 0.9.12, plus 13 fork commits); Slot-1 defaults to Retail auto -> MC+ROM with the B7 card-read command implemented, which is what BlocksDS NitroFS uses on emulators, but this has not been exercised with a BlocksDS ROM.

**Impact on the plan.** Small but concrete: (1) tests/e2e and CI must export SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy / SDL_AUDIODRIVER=dummy before constructing DeSmuME(), and call cycle(with_joystick=False); (2) the plan should stop describing the py-desmume core as '0.9.13' and must generate golden PNGs with py-desmume itself (not with the DeSmuME 0.9.13 GUI's Ctrl+F12), since the core and the screenshot path (native-res RGB555->RGB888) differ; (3) pin py-desmume==0.0.9 and Pillow, and note the VC++ x64 runtime requirement for any machine running the e2e suite; (4) if the e2e runs inside the BlocksDS Linux docker image, the manylinux_2_28 wheel (glibc >= 2.28) is used, which is fine for Ubuntu 22.04+/Debian 12+ images; otherwise run it on the Windows runner. If NitroFS turned out not to work in this April-2022 core, the screenshot regression would have to fall back to a ROM that embeds its assets or to DeSmuME 0.9.13 GUI + --play-movie + Ctrl+F12 automation.

**Residual risk.** Only a run can settle: (a) that a BlocksDS 1.24 two-ELF NitroFS ROM boots under py-desmume's April-2022 core and nitroFSInit() succeeds via the B7 card protocol (the plan's Phase 0 spike must do exactly this and save the 300-frame screenshot); (b) that SDL_Init succeeds on the actual GitHub Actions Windows/Linux runner with the dummy drivers; (c) that the Microsoft Store Python 3.13 on this machine loads libdesmume.dll cleanly (os.add_dll_directory path) and the VC++ runtime is present on the clean-VM test; (d) frame-to-frame determinism of the screenshot (RTC/firmware-dependent content) for golden comparison.

### Engineering check: confirmed (high confidence)

**Correction.** py-desmume 0.0.9 (PyPI; Windows wheels cp38-cp314 incl. cp313 win_amd64, uploaded 2025-12-12; Requires-Dist Pillow>=6.1.0; GPL-3.0) bundles its own libdesmume.dll built from the SkyTemple/desmume fork (commit 42ee6ea, 2025-12-02; reports itself as 'DeSmuME 0.9.12 git#0') - it is NOT the DeSmuME 0.9.13 desktop binary and must not be described as such. Verified on this machine: `emu = DeSmuME(); emu.open(rom); emu.input.keypad_add_key(keymask(Keys.KEY_A)); for _ in range(300): emu.cycle(with_joystick=False); emu.screenshot().save('out.png')` runs with no window (desmume_init only calls SDL_Init(VIDEO|TIMER)), from Node child_process.spawnSync('python', ..., {stdio:'pipe', windowsHide:true}), with ROM paths containing spaces/non-ASCII, and yields a 256x384 RGB PIL image (top+bottom screens stacked) that is byte-identical across runs. Six BlocksDS-built ROMs (2024-08 to 2026-07, NitroFS with 8-914 files) boot and render NitroFS-loaded graphics; devkitPro libnds-2.x ROMs (hbmenu 0.11.0, ftpd) hang white at the ARM9/ARM7 IPC handshake in this core, so e2e fixture ROMs must be built with BlocksDS. CI prerequisites: MSVC 2015-2022 x64 runtime (msvcp140/vcruntime140/vcruntime140_1), Pillow, and set SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy for headless runners (verified to work). Budget 1-9 s per 300 frames (35-390 fps, interpreter mode). The core tries to write a .dsv beside the ROM and silently falls back to RAM; is_running() does not detect a hung ROM, so assert on screenshot content (or ARM9 PC via emu.memory.register_arm9.pc) rather than on is_running().

**Impact on the plan.** None of WS8's deliverables change. Wording/robustness fixes only: (1) in unified-plan.md / unified-meta.json refer to 'py-desmume's bundled DeSmuME fork core', not 'DeSmuME 0.9.13', when describing the headless CI path (0.9.13-specific statements about the desktop exe - CLI flags, ini, Lua, .dsv paths - do not transfer to the DLL); (2) the CI job must install the VC++ 2015-2022 x64 runtime on any self-hosted/clean runner (GitHub-hosted windows images already have it) and should export SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy; (3) e2e fixture ROMs (samples/hello|minimal) must be produced by the BlocksDS pipeline - a devkitARM/libnds-2 ROM will white-screen in this core and make the golden-PNG test fail for the wrong reason; (4) the screenshot test should compare pixels/colour count and not rely on emu.is_running() to detect a hung ROM; (5) pin py-desmume==0.0.9 because golden PNGs depend on the bundled core's rasteriser.

**Residual risk.** Not exercised here: (a) an actual `pip install py-desmume` (I only downloaded and extracted the wheel and imported it from scratch - the install itself is trivial); (b) a GitHub-hosted windows-latest runner (SDL video init under the runner session, VC runtime present on the image) - cheapest Phase-0 spike: a 10-line workflow step `pip install py-desmume` + the 20-line script above with SDL_VIDEODRIVER=dummy on samples/minimal.nds, asserting >1 colour and comparing to a checked-in PNG; (c) the project's own runtime ROM built with BlocksDS 1.24.0 (2026-09-21) - newer than every ROM tested (latest ~2026-07) - so run the first real BlocksDS hello/NitroFS sample through the same script as the first act of WS8 (the toolchain-research doc already recommends this); (d) if the plan's ARM7 or crt0 is customised, re-check the ARM9/ARM7 handshake in this core, since that is exactly where libnds-2 ROMs hang.

Input injection reaches the emulated hardware and affects rendering.
- **Local observation of stdout and file system after runs**: The core prints 'BackupDevice: ... Failed to get read/write access to the save file! Will operate in RAM instead.' (it tries to create a .dsv beside the ROM and silently falls back); no files were created in cwd. is_running() only reflects pause state - an idle/hung ROM (argvTest spinning in a wait loop, 300 frames in 0.2 s) still reports running=True, so a regression test cannot rely on it to detect a crashed ROM.

## 11. Host build as the deterministic oracle

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** MSYS2 UCRT64 gcc 15.2 on this machine builds a C11 program with `-fwrapv -fsanitize=undefined -fsanitize-trap=all` (trap mode links; the runtime-library mode fails with `cannot find -lubsan`), and pure-integer C using 64-bit intermediates, libnds's vendored trig lookup tables (sinLerp/cosLerp tables, Zlib-licensed) and truncating division produces bit-identical results on x86-64 and on the ARM946E-S build using libnds `divf32`/`sqrtf32` (hardware divider with truncation toward zero), so a host build can serve as the semantic oracle for the DS runtime.

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** MSYS2 UCRT64 gcc 15.2.0 (package 15.2.0-11) builds the C11 host core with `-std=c11 -fwrapv -fsanitize=undefined -fsanitize-trap=all`; the runtime-library mode fails at link with `ld.exe: cannot find -lubsan: No such file or directory` because GCC's libsanitizer has no mingw target (verified 2026-09-24). Two build caveats: (1) gcc.exe invoked by absolute path from a plain Windows process fails silently (exit 1, no message; cc1.exe cannot load libmpfr-6.dll) unless `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin` is on PATH, so Makefile.host and the IDE's process spawner must prepend it; (2) with `-fwrapv`, GCC does not instrument +, -, * overflow at all (TYPE_OVERFLOW_SANITIZED requires !TYPE_OVERFLOW_WRAPS), so the UBSan trap build catches shifts, x/0, INT_MIN/-1, bounds and null but never arithmetic overflow; the plan's own 64-bit-intermediate overflow trap is the only overflow check. Bit-identity between the host and the ARM946E-S build holds for int32/int64 arithmetic with GCC-defined arithmetic `>>` (libnds `mulf32` is `(int32_t)(((int64_t)a*(int64_t)b) >> 12)`), for the vendored BlocksDS libnds trig code (`source/arm9/trig.c`: one quarter-wave `const u16 SIN_LUT[129]` in .15 fixed plus integer lerp, `cosLerp` = `sinLerp(angle + quarter)`, SPDX Zlib, no floats), and for division ONLY on the guarded domain den != 0 and not (INT_MIN, -1): there, hardware `divf32` (DIV_64_32 of `(int64)num << 12`, low 32 bits of the quotient), libgcc `__aeabi_idiv`, and C11 `/` all truncate toward zero. Outside that domain they diverge: DS hardware returns +/-1 (sign opposite to the numerator) for x/0 and -MAX for INT_MIN/-1 (GBATEK), libgcc `__aeabi_idiv` returns a saturated 0x7fffffff/0x80000000/0 for x/0, while the host crashes (x86 #DE) or hits `ud2` in the trap build, so the VM must test den == 0 and (INT_MIN, -1) before every divide on both targets. `sqrtf32` is not the divider: it is the separate SQRT unit, takes an UNSIGNED 32-bit argument (`uint32_t sqrtf32(uint32_t)`), and returns floor(sqrt(a << 12)) of an unsigned 64-bit parameter, so the host oracle needs a pure-integer isqrt64 and a negative-input guard. Also use fixed-width `int8_t`/`uint8_t` (or pass `-fsigned-char` on both builds): plain `char` is signed on x86-64 MinGW and unsigned on ARM (gcc arm.h DEFAULT_SIGNED_CHAR 0).

**Impact on the plan.** WS2's Makefile.host and the IDE's build/oracle spawner must set PATH to include C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin (or run under msys2_shell.cmd -ucrt64), otherwise `dsdude-host.exe` never builds and the failure is a silent exit 1. The division-equivalence test in section 2.4 / WS2 must enumerate den == 0, INT_MIN / -1, negative operands, and quotients whose 64-bit result exceeds 32 bits (divf32 returns the low 32 bits), and the VM's div/mod/`/` builtins must branch on den == 0 and (INT_MIN, -1) before calling divf32/div32 or emitting a C `/` on either target; without that guard the host crashes where the DS silently returns +/-1, saturated values or INT_MIN. Add a sqrt-equivalence test against an integer isqrt64 with the argument treated as unsigned, and never feed a negative Q20.12 to sqrtf32. Add `-fsigned-char` (or use only int8_t/uint8_t) to both the host and BlocksDS builds. Do not describe the UBSan trap build as an overflow detector while `-fwrapv` is on; if a test variant is meant to catch overflow, build it without `-fwrapv` (or keep the explicit 64-bit overflow trap as the only mechanism). Vendor `source/arm9/trig.c` (SIN_LUT, sinLerp/cosLerp, tanLutLookup) with its Zlib SPDX header, not a file named trig_lut.c. The 'truncation toward zero' wording applies to the divider only; sqrt is floor of an unsigned value.

**Residual risk.** Real ARM946E-S divider and sqrt behaviour, including the DIV0 / -MAX/-1 special cases and any rounding subtlety, is documented by GBATEK and modelled by melonDS but can only be settled by running the division/sqrt equivalence fixture on melonDS and, ideally, on hardware in Phase 0. Whether BlocksDS's libgcc multilib returns the saturated value or 0 for x/0 depends on the ARM-vs-Thumb variant actually linked; the explicit guard makes this moot but only a linked test ROM proves the guard covers every division site (including compiler-generated ones from `/` and `%`). The trap-mode host build was verified with a 15-line probe, not the full runtime; whether -O2 plus UBSan trap instrumentation changes any observable integer result in the real core (it should not) is confirmed only by running the golden traces on both the plain and the trap variants.

No libubsan/libasan runtime exists.
- **Local compile probe (scratchpad\ubsan-probe\probe.c, PATH prefixed with C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin)**: `gcc -std=c11 -O2 -fwrapv -fsanitize=undefined -fsanitize-trap=all probe.c -o probe_trap.exe` -> exit 0, 139,259-byte exe, runs. `gcc -std=c11 -O2 -fwrapv -fsanitize=undefined probe.c` -> `ld.exe: cannot find -lubsan: No such file or directory` / `collect2.exe: error: ld returned 1 exit status`. Bare `-fsanitize-trap` (no `=all`) also links.
- **Local compile probe, gcc invoked by absolute path WITHOUT C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin on PATH (both Bash and PowerShell tools)**: Every gcc invocation (even `gcc -E`, `-S`, `-c` of `int x;`) returned exit 1 with NO diagnostic. Direct cc1 run shows why: `cc1.exe: error while loading shared libraries: libmpfr-6.dll: cannot open shared object file`. gcc.exe adds only its libexec dir to PATH; cc1/ld need libmpfr-6.dll, libgmp-10.dll, libmpc-3.dll from ucrt64\bin. Works once PATH contains C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin.
- **GCC 15.2 docs, Instrumentation-Options.html (-fsanitize-trap[=opts])**: "report ... undefined behavior using __builtin_trap rather than a libubsan library routine ... The advantage of this is that the libubsan library is not needed and is not linked in, so this is usable even in freestanding environments. Currently this feature works with -fsanitize=undefined (and its suboptions except for -fsanitize=vptr) ... -fsanitize-trap=all can be also specified".
- **GCC 15 libsanitizer/configure.tgt (releases/gcc-15)**: Target case list contains only *-linux*, *-freebsd*, *-darwin*, *-solaris2.11*; no mingw/cygwin/w64 entry, default branch `UNSUPPORTED=1` (line 89). This is why MSYS2's mingw GCC ships no libubsan.
- **GCC 15.2 docs, Code-Gen-Options.html (-fwrapv) and gcc/tree.h lines 956-960, 982-985; local asm probe ops.c**: -fwrapv: "signed arithmetic overflow of addition, subtraction and multiplication wraps around" (division is NOT covered). tree.h: TYPE_OVERFLOW_WRAPS includes `|| flag_wrapv`; TYPE_OVERFLOW_SANITIZED requires `!TYPE_OVERFLOW_WRAPS`. Verified: with -fsanitize=undefined -fsanitize-trap=all, `-S` of add/mul/div/shift emits 4 ud2 without -fwrapv but only 2 with -fwrapv (add and mul checks vanish; div and shift checks remain).
- **GCC 15 gcc/c-family/c-ubsan.cc lines 36-80 and docs -fsanitize=signed-integer-overflow**: ubsan_instrument_division instruments `op1 == 0` (SANITIZE_DIVIDE) and `op0 == INT_MIN && op1 == -1` (SANITIZE_SI_OVERFLOW) without consulting flag_wrapv; docs: "This also detects INT_MIN / -1 signed division". Verified: the -fwrapv trap build's dv() has `cmpl $-2147483648` / `cmpl $-1` / `testl` -> ud2, and running probe_trap.exe on INT_MIN/-1 died with `Illegal instruction` (exit 132); the plain -fwrapv build crashed too (x86 #DE), i.e. host INT_MIN/-1 and x/0 never return a value.
- **GBATEK DS Maths (problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-maths.htm)**: DIVCNT modes 0: 32/32 (18 clks), 1: 64/32 (34 clks), 2: 64/64 (34 clks); NUMER/DENOM/RESULT signed. "Division Overflows: DIV0 --> REMAIN=NUMER, RESULT=+/-1 (with sign opposite of NUMER); -MAX/-1 --> RESULT=-MAX (instead +MAX)". SQRTCNT (0x40002B0): "Unsigned 64bit parameter, and unsigned 32bit result", 13 clks. GBATEK never states a rounding rule for the quotient; it only writes RESULT "=Numer/Denom" and DIVREM "=Numer MOD Denom".
- **melonDS src/NDS.cpp NDS::DivDone (lines 1791-1840) and NDS::SqrtDone (1877+)**: Emulator models the divider as C++ `num / den`, `num % den` on s32/s64 (truncation toward zero), with den==0 -> quotient +/-1 and remainder=num, and INT_MIN/-1 -> 0x80000000 (wrap). Sqrt is an integer bit-by-bit loop (floor of unsigned 64-bit input). This is the checkpoint emulator the plan uses, so truncation semantics are consistent across GBATEK, melonDS and C11 6.5.5.
- **BlocksDS libnds include/nds/arm9/math.h (raw master), lines 60-110, 121-125, 127-171, 175-217**: divf32_asynch: `REG_DIV_NUMER = (int64_t)((uint64_t)(int64_t)num << 12); REG_DIV_DENOM_L = den;` mode DIV_64_32; divf32_result returns `REG_DIV_RESULT_L` (low 32 bits). divf32 with both operands constant folds to `((int64_t)((uint64_t)(int64_t)num << 12)) / den` at compile time. mulf32 = `(int32_t)(((int64_t)a * (int64_t)b) >> 12)`. sqrtf32 is `uint32_t sqrtf32(uint32_t a)` (UNSIGNED), uses the separate SQRT unit (`REG_SQRT_PARAM = ((uint64_t)a) << 12`, SQRT_64) and, for constants, `(uint32_t)__builtin_sqrt((double)((uint64_t)a << 12))`. div32 with constant den compiles to plain `num / den` (libgcc software divide on ARM9).
- **BlocksDS libnds source/arm9/trig.c (not trig_lut.c; Codeberg tree + raw master) and include/nds/arm9/trig_lut.h**: Both files start `// SPDX-License-Identifier: Zlib`. trig.c: one quarter-wave table `const u16 SIN_LUT[LUT_SIZE + 1]` (129 entries, .15 fixed), pure integer (no `float`/`double` anywhere in the file); `sinLerp` = table lookup + `((diff * interp) >> 6)` then `result >> 3` to 4.12; `cosLerp(angle)` = `sinLerp(angle + LIBNDS_QUARTER_ANGLE)`. trig_lut.h: `DEGREES_IN_CIRCLE (1 << 15)`, sinLerp/cosLerp return s16 4.12. licenses/LICENSE is the Zlib text; readme.md: "libnds is provided under the terms of the Zlib license".
- **GCC libgcc/config/arm/lib1funcs.S (releases/gcc-15) lines 250-312 and 1459-1468**: ARM946E-S (ARMv5TE) has no divide instruction, so a plain C `/` calls `__aeabi_idiv`. On EABI, divide-by-zero runs ARM_LDIV0/THUMB_LDIV0: `movgt r0,#0x7fffffff; movlt r0,#0x80000000` (saturate; 0 for 0/0; the NOT_ISA_TARGET_32BIT Thumb-1 variant passes 0) then `b __aeabi_idiv0`, whose weak default is just `RET`. So x/0 on the DS returns a value silently, while x86 traps.
- **GCC gcc/config/arm/arm.h line 2144; GCC docs Integers-implementation.html; local ch.c probe**: ARM: `#define DEFAULT_SIGNED_CHAR 0` (plain char unsigned). Host probe: plain `char` is signed on x86_64-w64-mingw32, `sizeof(long) == 4` on both (LLP64 vs ILP32). GCC guarantees "Signed '>>' acts on negative numbers by sign extension" on every target, so the `>> 12` / `>> 3` in mulf32/sinLerp are identical on both.

</details>

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Replace the claim with: "MSYS2 UCRT64 gcc 15.2.0 builds runtime/core for the host with `-std=c11 -O2 -fwrapv -fno-strict-aliasing -Wall -Wextra -Werror` and a `-fsanitize=undefined -fsanitize-trap=all` variant (verified 2026-09-24; the runtime-library mode fails with `ld.exe: cannot find -lubsan`, and `-fsanitize=address` likewise has no libasan). Conditions: (1) Spawn gcc with `C:\\msys64\\ucrt64\\bin` on PATH (or through `C:\\msys64\\usr\\bin\\bash.exe -lc` with `MSYSTEM=UCRT64`); invoked by absolute path with a bare PATH, cc1.exe cannot load libgmp-10/libmpfr-6/libisl-23/libzstd/zlib1 DLLs and gcc exits 1 with no message (mingw32-make.exe itself needs no PATH). (2) Under `-fwrapv` GCC's UBSan does NOT instrument signed +,-,* overflow or left shifts of negative values; the trap variant catches integer divide-by-zero and INT_MIN/-1 (ud2, Windows exit 0xC000001D), shift counts >= width or negative, static array bounds, misaligned and null dereferences. value.h's debug overflow trap must therefore be explicit (`__builtin_add_overflow` & co.), and the variant must keep `-fwrapv` because vendored libnds trig.c (atanLerp's `par <<= 4` on negative input) traps without it. (3) Bit-identity with the DS build holds only if runtime/core is compiled for ARM with the same `-fwrapv -fno-strict-aliasing -std=c11` (the BlocksDS default ARM9 CFLAGS are just `-mthumb -mcpu=arm946e-s+nofp -O2 -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections -Wall`) and char signedness is pinned: arm-none-eabi `char` is unsigned, x86_64-w64-mingw32 `char` is signed, so add `-funsigned-char` to Makefile.host (or ban plain-char arithmetic); `long` is 4 bytes on both but size_t/pointers are 8 bytes on host vs 4 on DS. (4) The host must implement divf32/div64 as a function modelling the 64/32-mode divider, never bare `/`: quotient truncates toward zero; den==0 returns -1 for num>=0 and +1 for num<0 (remainder=num); -2^63/-1 returns -2^63; only the low 32 bits of the 64-bit quotient are returned (divf32(INT32_MAX,1) == -4096). sqrtf32(uint32_t a) = floor(sqrt((uint64_t)a << 12)) by bit-by-bit integer sqrt, 32-bit result (negative Q20.12 inputs yield ~4194301..4194303). The division-equivalence test must cover den==0, INT_MIN inputs, the low-32 wrap, and both remainder signs. (5) Vendor BlocksDS libnds `source/arm9/trig.c` (SPDX Zlib; `u16 SIN_LUT[129]` 1.15, `s32 TAN_LUT[129]` 16.16, 6 lerp bits, sinLerp/cosLerp return 4.12) together with a math.h shim that supplies `inttof32(n) ((n) * (1 << 12))` and the host divf32, because atanLerp calls both. Host self-consistency was verified: -O2, -O0, UBSan-trap and -funsigned-char builds give identical results over exhaustive sin/cos/tan/asin/acos, 2M divf32 cases (checked against an __int128 truncation oracle) and 3M sqrtf32 cases (floor property)."

**Impact on the plan.** WS1 BuildService/`buildRuntime()` and `Makefile.host` must set PATH (prefix `C:\msys64\ucrt64\bin`) or go through bash -lc with MSYSTEM=UCRT64, otherwise the host build fails silently with exit 1 from Node; the harness must treat Windows status 0xC000001D as "UBSan trap" (Node reports status=3221225501, signal=null) and 0xC0000094/0xC0000095 as unsanitized divide faults. WS2 value.h: the "debug overflow trap" cannot come from UBSan under -fwrapv and must be explicit checked arithmetic; fixed.c needs a host divf32/div64/sqrtf32 model with the DS special cases and a math.h shim for the vendored trig.c (atanLerp dependency); Makefile.host should add -funsigned-char. WS3 DS Makefile: add `-fwrapv -fno-strict-aliasing -std=c11` for runtime/core objects (BlocksDS defaults omit them), or overflow/aliasing behaviour can legitimately diverge between the two -O2 builds. Risk register item 5 should list the four remaining divergence classes UBSan/-fwrapv do not cover: char signedness, size_t/pointer width (struct sizes, 256-byte instance block static_assert), unspecified argument evaluation order (e.g. `binop(pop(), pop())`), uninitialized memory and libc differences (qsort ordering, rand, snprintf %zu) -- these need coding rules plus the golden-trace diff, not compiler flags.

**Residual risk.** The ARM half was not executed: no arm-none-eabi-gcc exists on this machine (C:\devkitPro\devkitARM\bin has only as/nm/objcopy/objdump) and launching DeSmuME was out of scope, so the hardware divider/sqrt semantics rest on GBATEK, melonDS's DivDone/SqrtDone source and libnds's own constant-fold path, not on a hardware or emulator run; GBATEK does not literally say "truncates toward zero" or "floor". The DS build will use Wonderful's GCC 16.2 at -O2, whose code generation for the same C is only guaranteed identical in the absence of UB, which UBSan-with--fwrapv only partially polices (no overflow, shift-base, uninitialized-read or evaluation-order checks). Cheapest Phase-0 spike (about 1 hour once the toolchain is installed): compile scratchpad\ubsan-test\harness.c + vendored_trig.c + the host divf32/sqrtf32 models for ARM9 with the BlocksDS Makefile plus `-fwrapv -fno-strict-aliasing -std=c11 -funsigned-char`-equivalent settings, have the ROM also run the same 2M divf32 / 3M sqrtf32 inputs through the real libnds divf32/sqrtf32 (hardware divider) and print all four hashes through the nocash `DSD|` log on melonDS; they must equal the host's 5c3074dd / 340d865d / d1f1c5b5 / fec17052. If a flashcart is available, run the same ROM on hardware once to close the GBATEK-vs-silicon gap.

Constant-fold paths substitute C division `((int64_t)((uint64_t)(int64_t)num << 12)) / den` for the hardware, i.e. libnds treats the divider as C truncating division. inttof32(n) = ((n) * (1 << 12)).

BUT atanLerp does `par <<= TAN_BITSFRACTION - 12` on a possibly negative s32 (C11 UB) and calls `inttof32()` and `divf32()` from math.h, so the vendored file depends on the divider; asinLerp calls libc bsearch (unique-match comparator, deterministic). devkitPro path source/arm9/trig_lut.c no longer exists (404); BlocksDS file is source/arm9/trig.c.

## 12. mwccarm and dsd are not in the pipeline

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** The CodeWarrior mwccarm toolchain in the project folder cannot be used as a practical backend for new homebrew: no NitroSDK headers/libs exist, `stub_libs/libcf.a` is a stub, a from-scratch HAL is 8-14 person-weeks, the compiler is proprietary/FlexLM/32-bit and non-redistributable; and `dsd` (ds-decomp 0.11.0) `rom extract/build/config` require a retail-style ROM (0x4000 header, FNT, known banner version, nitrocode 0xDEC00621 ARM9 footer) and reject homebrew ROMs, so neither belongs in the build pipeline.

### Primary-source check: confirmed (high confidence)

**Correction.** (Optional precision only; claim stands.) In plan section 2.2 line 42, tighten to: "`dsd` (local binary ds-decomp-cli 0.11.0 on ds-rom 0.7.0; upstream latest v0.12.1 / ds-rom 0.8.0 as of 2026-09-07) re-links decompiled retail games: `rom extract` requires at least 0x4000 bytes of header (size_of Header), a banner with version 1/2/3/0x103, an FNT of >= 8 bytes and a NitroSDK `nitrocode 0xDEC00621` ARM9 footer (which ndstool never writes); `rom build` takes only an extract-generated config.yaml and `rom config` loads the previously extracted ROM plus a mwldarm ELF with BuildInfo/AutoloadCallback symbols. Verified 2026-09-24: three ndstool-built homebrew ROMs and the research test ROM all fail (slice panic, 'unknown banner version', or 'expected nitrocode 0xdec00621 in ARM9 footer but got 0x0')." In line 40, "8-14 person-week" should be labelled an estimate (research-mwccarm.md section 7), not a verified figure.

**Impact on the plan.** None: the decision (BlocksDS backend, mwccarm and dsd moved to gitignored vendor/ with no pipeline role) is supported by every checked fact. Had it been wrong, WS1 would have needed a second compiler path and WS1's packRom/verifyRom could have used dsd instead of ndstool; neither applies.

**Residual risk.** Only `dsd rom extract` was exercised empirically; a hand-authored ds-rom config.yaml feeding `dsd rom build` with a homebrew ARM9 was not attempted (source shows it still needs BuildInfo/autoload structures, so it is expected to fail, but that is inference). The 8-14 person-week HAL estimate can only be settled by building one, which the plan rightly does not do. No vendor EULA text for CodeWarrior for Nintendo DS was located; non-redistributability rests on the FlexLM/Freescale licensing plus decomp projects' explicit 'we cannot distribute' statements.

**Correction.** Claim stands; optional sharpening for the plan text: (1) 'dsd-windows-x86_64.exe is ds-decomp-cli 0.11.0 embedding ds-rom 0.7.0; on ndstool -h 0x200 ROMs it panics (exit code 101) rather than printing a clean error, so any future ROM-inspector panel must treat exit 101/panic text as "unsupported ROM"'; (2) 'rom build and rom config consume the extract directory, not a ROM file, but Rom::load() enforces the nitrocode check via build_info_mut(), so hand-writing a config does not bypass it'; (3) 'every mwccarm build in the bundle (1.2/base, 1.2/sp2p3, 2.0/sp1, 2.0/sp2p4) hangs on compile without LM_LICENSE_FILE; only -version runs unlicensed'; (4) 'BlocksDS libnds9.a/crt0.o cannot be linked by mwldarm (unsupported R_ARM_JUMP24 0x1D, no ARM-to-Thumb veneers, TLS relocations), so libnds cannot be borrowed - a CodeWarrior runtime means recompiling/porting libnds or writing a HAL'.

No Phase-0 experiment is needed to keep both tools out of the pipeline.

## 13. Main RAM and VRAM budget

**Claim as stated in the draft plan.** The DS memory/VRAM budget in the plan is feasible: 4 MB main RAM with ~3.0-3.3 MB usable by the app after libnds/crt0/heap overhead; a runtime binary of 0.4-0.7 MB; instance pool 512 x 256 B; a 1.5 MB room bump arena; 192 KB string/array arena; maxmod soundbank 0.5-1 MB loaded to RAM; 128 sprites per engine, 32 affine matrices per engine, 16 extended palettes per screen for OBJ, 4 BG layers per engine, and a 1200-pixel-per-scanline sprite limit (per GBATEK, the OBJ rendering cycle budget per scanline on the DS is 1536 cycles in normal mode / 1200 with H-blank interval free access).

### Primary-source check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Memory: "DS main RAM is 4 MB (0x02000000-0x023FFFFF). BlocksDS crt0 sets the ARM9 heap end to end-of-main-RAM minus 48 KB (0xC000), i.e. 0x023F4000 (sdk/sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0.s + libnds mpu_setup.s), and the heap starts at __end__ (end of the ARM9 image incl. secure area, crt0, 16 KB DLDI reserve, .text/.rodata/.data/.bss) or after the argv copy. Usable heap = 4048 KB - ARM9 image size; with a 0.4-0.7 MB image that is ~3.33-3.63 MB, so the plan budgets a conservative 3.0-3.3 MB. Note the linker region is ewram = 4M - 512k (ds_arm9.mem): the static image may not exceed 3.5 MB, but the heap grows past 0x02380000 (the ARM7 load area, which the ARM7 copies into its 96 KB IWRAM at boot). The ARM7 binary therefore costs no main RAM at run time. DTCM is 16 KB total and holds the ARM9 SVC/IRQ/user stacks; any DTCM-resident VM register stack must fit alongside them." Budget sum: "DSDB 300 KB + instance pool 128 KB + string/array arena 192 KB + room arena 1536 KB + resident maxmod data <= 1024 KB = <= 3180 KB, leaving 150-450 KB for malloc headers, FatFs/NitroFS buffers and argv at the 0.7-0.4 MB image sizes; keep at least ~150 KB of slack." Sprite line limit: "GBATEK documents only the GBA OBJ budget (1210 cycles, or 954 with H-Blank Interval Free; normal OBJ n cycles, affine 10+2n) and gives no DS figure; the '1536 cycles' on GBATEK's DS Video Stuff page is the 256-dot x 6-cycle scanline drawing time, not an OBJ budget, and '1200' appears nowhere in GBATEK. Secondary sources (DSHack wiki; DeSmuME GPU.cpp _SpriteRenderPerform cost model, threshold 2130) put the DS per-scanline OBJ budget at ~2124 cycles with DISPCNT bit 23 'OBJ Processing during H-Blank' clear (libnds default) and ~1530 with it set; cost = 2 per OBJ on the line + width for normal OBJs, or + 10 + 2*width for affine OBJs. DSDude keeps a conservative static limit of 1200 cost units per scanline (well under 2124) computed with that cost model; neither DeSmuME (check disabled) nor melonDS (no check) enforces the limit, so overflow only shows on real hardware." Hardware limits (unchanged, confirmed): 128 OBJs and 32 affine parameter sets per engine (libnds SPRITE_COUNT/MATRIX_COUNT), 16 x 256-colour OBJ extended palettes per engine (8 KB in VRAM F/G/I), 4 BG layers per engine, OBJ VRAM max 256 KB engine A / 128 KB engine B.

**Impact on the plan.** 1) Fix the rationale text wherever the '1536 / 1200 cycles per GBATEK' statement appears (contracts/runtime-limits.json comments, hardware notes, manual): the hardware budget is ~2124/1530 cycles from secondary sources, and 1200 is DSDude's conservative choice. 2) The IDE scanline meter must use the OBJ cost model (2 + width, or 2 + 10 + 2*width for affine, per OBJ intersecting the line), not a raw pixel count, otherwise affine sprites are under-counted (32 affine 64-px double-size sprites cost 32*(2+10+256) = 8576 cycles on one line). 3) Because DeSmuME's check is disabled and melonDS has none, the static check is the only guard; keep it. 4) Memory prose should cite the crt0-derived figure ('4048 KB minus ARM9 image') instead of the vague '3.0-3.3 MB after libnds/crt0/heap overhead', note the 3.5 MB static-image cap from ds_arm9.mem, and record that the max-corner budget (0.7 MB image + 300 KB DSDB + 1 MB resident soundbank) leaves only ~150 KB of slack; either cap resident maxmod data at ~0.75 MB or lower dsdbMaxBytes to keep the plan's stated ~300 KB slack. 5) Budget DTCM explicitly (16 KB shared by the C stacks and the planned 4 KB VM register stack). No workstream ordering changes.

**Residual risk.** Only measurable by building: the actual ARM9 image size of libnds + maxmod + FatFs/NitroFS + console + VM (0.4-0.7 MB is an estimate; check with arm-none-eabi-size / the .map file in the Phase 0 spike and print getHeapLimit-style figures via DSD|MEM). The DS per-scanline OBJ budget (2124/1530 cycles, 1 cycle/px, affine 10+2n, +2 per OBJ) comes from DSHack and DeSmuME's model, not GBATEK or a hardware test, and neither emulator enforces it, so the exact overflow point and its visual effect can only be confirmed on real DS hardware (or a targeted test ROM). maxmod's per-load allocation overhead and the FatFs/NitroFS buffer footprint on the heap are unmeasured. The extended-palette VRAM mapping (F/G for main OBJ/BG palettes, H/I for sub) must be exercised in the selftest ROM.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **GBATEK DS memory map (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-memory-maps.htm)**: "02000000h  Main Memory (4MB)"; "06400000h VRAM - Engine A, OBJ VRAM (max 256KB)"; "06600000h VRAM - Engine B, OBJ VRAM (max 128KB)"; "07000000h OAM (2KB) (Engine A, Engine B)". The plan's 128 KB OBJ VRAM per screen is within both maxima.
- **BlocksDS sdk sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0.s (raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/master/sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0.s, 'Setup heap limits' block)**: fake_heap_start = __end__ (DS mode) or end of the argv copy; then: "// The heap end is located at the end of main RAM, minus the space reserved at for libnds (48 KB, or 0xC000)." ... "ldr r1, =fake_heap_end / sub r8, r8, 0xC000 / str r8, [r1]". So heap end on a 4 MB DS = 0x023F4000.
- **BlocksDS libnds source/arm9/system/mpu_setup.s**: "// r8: End address of the cached RAM for this DS model (mapped at 0x02000000)" and "mov r8, #0x02400000" for the retail DS (0x02800000 debugger DS, 0x03000000 DSi). Confirms the 48 KB reservation is measured from 0x02400000.
- **BlocksDS libnds source/common/libc/sbrk.c**: sbrk() allocates from fake_heap_start up to fake_heap_end and returns ENOMEM beyond it; the heap limit is the runtime value set by crt0, not the linker region.
- **BlocksDS sdk sys/crts/ds_arm9.mem (pulled in by ds_arm9.specs '*link: ... -T ds_arm9.mem -T ds_arm9.ld')**: "ewram : ORIGIN = 0x02000000, LENGTH = 4M - 512k"; "dtcm : ORIGIN = 0x02ff4000, LENGTH = 16K"; "itcm : ORIGIN = 0x01000000, LENGTH = 32K". The linker caps the static ARM9 image (code/data/bss) at 3.5 MB, not 4M-48K; the heap still runs to 0x023F4000 via crt0. ds_arm9.ld also reserves a .secure area at the image start and a 16 KB DLDI area (PROVIDE(__dldi_size = 16384)).
- **BlocksDS docs Memory map (https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/docs/internal/memory_map/)**: "Main RAM starts at address 0x2000000", Retail DS 4 MB; last 48 KB reserved: 0x2FF4000 bootstub (0x5000 bytes), 0x2FFA000 HBMenu exception handler, 0x2FFE000 DSi .nds header, 0x2FFF000 libnds IPC region, 0x2FFFC80 user settings, 0x2FFFD9C ARM9 exception vector/top of stack; DTCM (16 KB) mapped at 0x2FF4000-0x2FF8000 and used for the ARM9 stack.
- **BlocksDS sdk sys/crts/ds_arm7.ld**: MEMORY { ewram : ORIGIN = 0x02380000, LENGTH = 12M - 512K; iwram : ORIGIN = 0x03800000 - __shared_wram_size, LENGTH = 65536 + __shared_wram_size } with PROVIDE(__shared_wram_size = 32768); ARM7 .text/.rodata/.data are '>iwram AT>ewram', i.e. loaded at 0x02380000 and copied into 96 KB IWRAM, so the ARM7 binary does not occupy main RAM at run time (that region becomes ARM9 heap).
- **Arithmetic from the above**: Heap = 0x023F4000 - __end__ = 4048 KB - ARM9 image (secure area + crt0 + 16 KB DLDI + text/rodata/data/bss) - argv. For a 0.4-0.7 MB image that is ~3.33-3.63 MB, so the plan's '3.0-3.3 MB usable' is conservative by ~0.3 MB. Plan allocations excluding the binary (DSDB 300 + instances 128 + strings 192 + room arena 1536 + maxmod 512..1024 KB) = 2668-3180 KB: fits the derived 3331-3638 KB with 150-970 KB slack, but at the max corner exceeds the plan's own 3.0 MB floor by ~108 KB.
- **GBATEK LCD OBJ Overview (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-lcd-obj-overview.htm)**: "1210 (=304*4-6) If 'H-Blank Interval Free' bit in DISPCNT register is 0"; "954 (=240*4-6) If ... is 1"; "n*1 cycles - Normal OBJs"; "10+n*2 cycles - Rotation/Scaling OBJs"; "Up to 128 OBJs ... per screen"; "32 OBJ Rotation/Scaling Parameter groups". The page contains NO DS-specific figure and never mentions 1536 or 1200.
- **GBATEK DS Video Stuff (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-video-stuff.htm)**: "Although the drawing time is only 1536 cycles (256*6), the NDS9 H-Blank flag is '0' for a total of 1606 cycles" - 1536 is the scanline drawing time, not an OBJ rendering budget; '1200' does not appear on any GBATEK page.
- **GBATEK DS Video BG Modes/Control (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-video-bg-modes-control.htm)**: DISPCNT "23  A+B  OBJ Processing during H-Blank (was located in Bit5 on GBA)"; BG mode table lists BG0..BG3 for modes 0-6 and "Engine B: Same as above, except that: Mode 6 is reserved ... and BG0 is always Text (no 3D support)" - 4 BG layers per engine confirmed.
- **DSHack wiki General Hardware Restrictions (https://wiki.dshack.org/Wiki.jsp?page=General+Hardware+Restrictions) - secondary source**: "The 2D engine allots 2124 or 1530 cycles for object rendering depending on whether OBJ processing during H-blank is enabled or not." "Each pixel of a normal OBJ takes 1 cycle, and each pixel of an affine OBJ takes 2 cycles. Affine OBJ have an overhead of 10 cycles, and OBJ that exist partially off the screen have an overhead of 2 cycles." (2124 = 355*6-6, 1530 = 256*6-6, the DS analogue of GBATEK's GBA formula.)
- **DeSmuME desmume/src/GPU.cpp, GPUEngineBase::_SpriteRenderPerform (lines ~2003-2210 of master) - secondary source**: "size_t cost = 0; for (spriteNum = 0; spriteNum < 128; ...) { if (cost >= 2130) { //out of sprite rendering time //printf(...) //return; } cost += 2; ... cost += (sprSize.width * 2) + 10; (rotscale) ... cost += sprSize.width; (normal)". Budget of 2130 cycles per line is modelled but the 'return' is commented out, so DeSmuME does NOT enforce it.
- **melonDS src/GPU2D_Soft.cpp DrawSprites**: Iterates all 128 OAM entries with no per-scanline cycle/pixel limit at all; overflow is invisible on melonDS too.
- **BlocksDS libnds include/nds/arm9/video.h and sprite.h**: "#define DISPLAY_SPR_HBLANK (1 << 23)" (off unless the program sets it); "#define SPRITE_COUNT 128" ("Maximum number of sprites per engine available."), "#define MATRIX_COUNT 32" ("Maximum number of affine matrices per engine available."); SpriteSize enum has exactly the 12 sizes; oamInit(..., extPalette) enables extended palettes for 8 bpp sprites.
- **GBATEK DS Video Extended Palettes (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-video-extended-palettes.htm)**: OBJ extended palette: "256 colors x 16 palettes --> extended palette memory (=4096 colors)", 8K per engine, must be in VRAM F, G or I; BG extended palette 4 slots x 8K (32K) per engine. 16 OBJ extended palettes per screen confirmed.
- **BlocksDS maxmod DS programming guide (https://blocksds.skylyrac.net/maxmod/md_documentation_2ds__programming__guide.html)**: "mmInitDefault() is used when your soundbank is in the filesystem"; "by keeping the soundbank in the filesystem, Maxmod will only load the parts that are required for the songs and audio effects that you're playing right in that moment"; mmLoad/mmLoadEffect "Loads ... into memory", mmUnload/mmUnloadEffect "frees memory". Loaded modules/samples live in ARM9-allocated main RAM, so 0.5-1 MB is a valid budget only if the whole bank is resident at once.
- **Plan files (unified-plan.md line 142, unified-meta.json C13 runtime-limits.json, research-hardware.md lines 16, 39, 112)**: Plan values: instancesMax 512 x 256 B = 131072 (128 KB), stringArenaBytes 196608 (192 KB), roomArenaBytes 1572864 (1.5 MB), soundbankMaxBytes 1048576, dsdbMaxBytes 307200, scanlineSpritePixels 1200 - arithmetic consistent. research-hardware.md itself records (line 112) that 'GBATEK gives no separate DS figure' and calls 1200 a conservative limit; the claim's parenthetical contradicts the plan's own research note.

</details>

### Engineering check: partially true (high confidence)

**Correction.** Memory: BlocksDS 1.24.0 links the ARM9 image into a 3.5 MB region (ds_arm9.mem: ewram 0x02000000, 4M-512k, ending at 0x02380000 where the ARM7 image is staged), but ds_arm9_crt0 sets fake_heap_end to end-of-main-RAM minus 48 KB (0x023F4000), so malloc can use everything above the binary. The ARM7 (arm7_maxmod.elf: 32.5 KB load image, ~47 KB resident in its 96 KB IWRAM) costs the ARM9 nothing but 544 B. Expect a 0.2-0.4 MB gc-sectioned ARM9 runtime (libnds9.a is 110 KB of text in total, libmm9 3 KB), giving ~3.35-3.85 MB usable; treat 3.0-3.3 MB as a guaranteed floor. Pool maxima (DSDB 300 KB + instances 128 KB + strings 192 KB + room arena 1,536 KB + soundbank 1,024 KB = 3,180 KB) fit, but grfLoadPath and maxmod mmLoad/mmLoadEffect malloc from the same libc heap, so expose one combined 'RAM' meter with >=150 KB reserved for those transients. Scanline sprites: GBATEK documents only the GBA OBJ budget (1210 cycles normal / 954 with H-Blank Interval Free; n cycles per normal-sprite pixel, 10+2n per affine sprite); it gives no DS OBJ budget (its '1536' is the DS visible-line draw time 256x6 under DISPSTAT, '1200' does not appear) and notes the DS moved the H-blank OBJ bit to DISPCNT bit 23. DeSmuME 0.9.13 models a 2130-cycle line (355x6) with +2 per OAM entry, +w per normal sprite and 10+2w per affine sprite, dropping higher-index sprites; melonDS enforces no limit. Keep scanlineSpritePixels 1200 as a conservative IDE warning weighted 10+2w for affine sprites, label it 'unverified on hardware', and confirm it on hardware in Phase 0. Instance blocks: store the 39 built-in variables as native 4-byte fields (via GETBI/SETBI); 8-byte cells would need 312 B, so 256 B blocks leave only ~10-12 user slots (or use 512 B blocks: 256 KB pool). DTCM: set __dtcm_data_size for the 4 KB VM register stack so the C stack grows away from it.

**Impact on the plan.** Low impact on architecture: every hardware count (128 OBJ, 32 affine, 16 OBJ ext palettes, 4 BGs, 128 KB OBJ VRAM per screen) and the RAM arithmetic hold, with more headroom than the plan assumes. Required edits: (1) C13/docs must stop citing GBATEK for '1536/1200' and mark the 1200-pixel figure as a conservative, hardware-unverified heuristic that also weights affine sprites 10+2w; the selftest/hardware notes in WS3 should measure it because melonDS (the default Play target) will never surface an overflow while DeSmuME drops sprites past 2130 cost units. (2) The runtime-limits contract needs a combined RAM budget (pools + loaded soundbank + grf transients) rather than five independent maxima, since all of them can be hit simultaneously. (3) WS2 value.h/instances.c must specify native-typed built-ins or a larger block, otherwise the 256 B block has no room for user variables. (4) runtime/Makefile should pass --defsym=__dtcm_data_size for the DTCM VM stack. (5) The 'runtime binary 0.4-0.7 MB incl. ARM7' wording should become '0.2-0.4 MB ARM9; ARM7 lives in IWRAM'.

**Residual risk.** Only hardware can settle the DS per-scanline OBJ budget (GBATEK, melonDS and DeSmuME disagree or are silent; DeSmuME's 2130 is a self-described guess). Cheapest Phase-0 spike: in the WS3 selftest ROM, line up N 64x64 normal sprites (and separately N affine sprites) on one scanline, step N with the D-pad, and note where sprites vanish on a DS/DS Lite via flashcart (also under DeSmuME 0.9.13 with and without DISPCNT bit 23), then set scanlineSpritePixels from the measured value. Second spike: build the selftest ROM with the real BlocksDS Makefile and read the .map / DSD|MEM output to confirm the ARM9 binary is <=0.4 MB and getHeapLimit()-getHeapStart() is >=3.3 MB with all static pools present; also measure the peak transient heap of grfLoadPath on the largest allowed GRF (1024-tile 8bpp BG, 64-frame 32x32 sprite) to size the combined meter's reserve.

<details><summary>Evidence</summary>

- **BlocksDS 1.24.0 package (downloaded blocksds-toolchain-1.24.0-1-x86_64.pkg.tar.xz, 3.06 MB, extracted to scratchpad/mem/pkg): sys/crts/ds_arm9.mem**: MEMORY { ewram : ORIGIN = 0x02000000, LENGTH = 4M - 512k; dtcm : ORIGIN = 0x02ff4000, LENGTH = 16K; itcm : ORIGIN = 0x01000000, LENGTH = 32K }. The ARM9 static link region (.text/.data/.bss, __eheap_end) is 3.5 MB (3,670,016 B) and ends at 0x02380000, where the ARM7 image is loaded. ds_arm9.specs links with -T ds_arm9.mem -T ds_arm9.ld --gc-sections; default Makefile compiles -mthumb -O2 -ffunction-sections -fdata-sections.
- **https://raw.githubusercontent.com/blocksds/sdk/master/sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0.s (lines 150-180) and prebuilt sys/crts/ds_arm9_crt0.o relocations (arm-none-eabi-objdump -r)**: "The heap end is located at the end of main RAM, minus the space reserved at for libnds (48 KB, or 0xC000)": `ldr r1,=fake_heap_end; sub r8,r8,0xC000; str r8,[r1]` (r8 = end of main RAM from __libnds_mpu_setup). fake_heap_start = __end__ (or endARGV). So malloc/sbrk can reach 0x023F4000 regardless of the 3.5 MB linker region; source/common/libc/sbrk.c confirms sbrk fails with ENOMEM only past fake_heap_end. Usable RAM = 4096 KB - 48 KB - binary, i.e. 3,331-3,638 KB with the plan's own 0.4-0.7 MB binary, so '3.0-3.3 MB usable' is a safe lower bound.
- **sys/crts/ds_arm7.ld + ds_arm7_crt0.s + objdump -h sys/arm7/main_core/arm7_maxmod.elf**: ARM7 sections are `>iwram AT>ewram` (iwram = 0x037F8000, 64 KB + 32 KB shared); crt0 comment 'Copy arm7 binary from LMA to VMA (EWRAM to IWRAM)'. arm7_maxmod.elf: .crt0 544 B at 0x02380000, .text 30,224 B + .rodata 2,232 B + .data 8 B (load image 32,484 B at 0x02380220, dead after copy), .bss 14,808 B; total IWRAM use ~47 KB of 96 KB. The ARM7 binary therefore does not consume ARM9 main RAM (the plan's binary estimate wrongly folds the 'ARM7 binary' into the 0.4-0.7 MB).
- **arm-none-eabi-nm --size-sort -S on libs/libnds/lib/libnds9.a and libs/maxmod/lib/libmm9.a (whole archives, before --gc-sections)**: libnds9.a: text 109,942 B, rodata/data 17,865 B, bss 7,830 B (FatFs + NitroFS + grf + console + oam/bg/video all included). libmm9.a: text 3,188 B. A gc-sectioned runtime (libnds subset + picolibc stdio/malloc + VM/engine) is realistically 0.2-0.4 MB, so 0.4-0.7 MB is a ~2x overestimate (conservative direction).
- **Arithmetic on contracts C13/3.3 of unified-plan.md**: Independent maxima: dsdbMaxBytes 300 KB + instances 512x256 B = 128 KB + stringArenaBytes 192 KB + roomArenaBytes 1,536 KB + soundbankMaxBytes 1,024 KB = 3,180 KB. Against 3,331-3,638 KB usable (plan's binary) or 3,648-3,848 KB (realistic binary) that leaves 150-670 KB for the libc heap. grf.c (blocksds/libnds source/arm9/grf.c lines 68/204/220) mallocs every decoded chunk and maxmod's mmLoad/mmLoadEffect malloc modules/samples on demand (BlocksDS maxmod DS programming guide: 'Maxmod will only load the parts that are required'), so those transients and the loaded soundbank both come out of that heap; feasible but should be a combined meter, not five independent limits.
- **Full GBATEK single file (https://problemkaputt.de/gbatek.htm, 5.0 MB, grep counts: '1210' 17 hits, '1536' 2 hits, '954' 10 hits; '1200' as an OBJ figure: 0 hits)**: The only OBJ cycle table is the GBA one: '1210 (=304*4-6) If H-Blank Interval Free bit in DISPCNT register is 0 / 954 (=240*4-6) if 1; n*1 cycles Normal OBJs; 10+n*2 cycles Rotation/Scaling OBJs'. The sole relevant '1536' is under DS DISPSTAT timing: 'Although the drawing time is only 1536 cycles (256*6), the NDS9 H-Blank flag is 0 for a total of 1606 cycles'. It is a line-timing remark, not an OBJ budget. GBATEK's DS DISPCNT lists 'Bit 23 A+B OBJ Processing during H-Blank (was located in Bit5 on GBA)' with no DS cycle figure. The attribution '1536 normal / 1200 H-blank-free per GBATEK' is therefore false; if the DS follows the GBA pattern, 1536 (256*6) would be the H-blank-free (visible-only) case and normal mode ~2124-2130.
- **DeSmuME GPU.cpp (TASEmulators/desmume master, _SpriteRenderPerform lines 2003-2210) and melonDS GPU2D_Soft.cpp / GPU2D.cpp (master)**: DeSmuME: `size_t cost = 0; ... if (cost >= 2130) { //out of sprite rendering time ... }`, `cost += 2` per OAM entry ('do we incur a cost if a sprite is disabled?? we guess so'), `cost += sprSize.width` per normal sprite, `cost += (sprSize.width * 2) + 10` per rot/scale sprite; sprites with higher OAM index are dropped once exceeded. melonDS: NumSprites is only counted, never compared to any budget (no limit emulated). So the IDE's 1200-pixel threshold is conservative relative to every known model (1200 < 1536 < 2130) but its numeric basis is unverified on hardware and melonDS will never show an overflow.
- **GBATEK DS VRAM control page (problemkaputt.de/gbatek-ds-memory-control-vram.htm) and research-hardware.md lines 37-41, 109-111**: 128 OBJ per engine, 32 rot/scale parameter groups, 4 BGs per engine, OBJ extended palette 8 KB per engine = 16 x 256 colours: confirmed. Bank plan is feasible: A main OBJ 128 KB, D sub OBJ 128 KB (sub OBJ max 128 KB), F/G MST 4 = BG ext palette (2 slots per 16 KB bank) and MST 5 = OBJ ext palette slot 0, H MST 2 = sub BG ext palette slots 0-3, I MST 3 = sub OBJ ext palette. Caveat: with E used as extra main BG, F alone covers ext palettes for only two main BGs (slots 0-1 or 2-3), and G must be the main OBJ ext palette.
- **unified-plan.md line 192 (built-in instance variables) vs line 288 (value.h 8-byte cells, 256-byte instance blocks)**: The plan enumerates 39 built-in instance variables (27 scalars + alarm[0..7] + 4 bbox). As 8-byte cells that is 312 B > 256 B; even 32 stored built-ins fill the block exactly. The 128 KB pool is fine, but the 256 B block only works if built-ins are native 4-byte fields (the GETBI/SETBI opcodes suggest so), leaving ~10-12 user 8-byte slots before the 8-entry overflow map.
- **sys/crts/ds_arm9.ld PROVIDE defaults**: __svc_stack_size 0x100, __irq_stack_size 0x100, __dtcm_reserved_size 0x40, __usr_stack_size 0, __dtcm_data_size 0. The plan's 4 KB DTCM VM register stack fits (about 15.8 KB free) but __dtcm_data_size must be set (e.g. --defsym) so .dtcm data sits above the user stack; with the default 0 the C stack grows down into .dtcm data and then into the main-RAM mirror at 0x02FF3FFF, which aliases the top of the heap region.

</details>

## Plan critiques

### Beginner experience (game-dev educator)

The plan gets the two lessons that killed DSGM and made GB Studio work: a prebuilt runtime so Play never needs a compiler, a logical instance list decoupled from OAM, live meters instead of link errors, docs generated from the same table the compiler uses, and a real human as the M5 gate. The Flappy journey (New Project -> PNG -> object -> ten lines -> room -> Play -> typo) is credible and the diagnostic voice ("I don't know a function called draw_sprit. Did you mean draw_sprite?") is right for a 12-year-old. Judged strictly from the learner's seat, though, the plan has one self-contradiction that would show up in the tutorial's first ten lines (the W031 touch lint fires on the sample's own obj_bird), one under-specified concept that produces a real trap (two screens share one coordinate space, so objects on different screens at the same x,y collide), and one false claim that hides a missing feature (a "block platformer" made of sprite instances hits 128 OBJs per screen, so the promised platformer-starter needs painted tile collisions, which GB Studio ships and this plan omits). Several day-one needs are absent from both the surface and the omission list: a Game Start event (nowhere to initialise a high score when room_restart re-runs Create), optional semicolons (GML does not require them, yet "missing semicolon" is listed as a top beginner error), targeted messages for the GML names every YouTube tutorial uses (keyboard_check, instance_create_layer, image_alpha), auto-padding of odd sprite sizes instead of an E4xx, in-app delivery of the tutorial/manual (docs are markdown in the repo with no stated way to reach them from the IDE), and a controls card for the emulator (melonDS has no default key bindings and the beginner is never told the mapping). The M5 gate is the right idea but needs a persona, n>1, a packaged build, a time-to-first-Play metric, bundled tutorial assets and a free-play segment. None of this changes the architecture; most fixes are contract lines (C1, C6, C9, C13) that should land before the phase0 tag, plus WS6/WS7 deliverables.

**Blocker gaps**

- **Diagnostics / tutorial consistency** (Section 1 (journey step 5), Section 4 (Flappy step.dss), Section 6 WS4 deliverables (W031), Section 9 risk 15, C6, C9). The tutorial's first code contradicts the checker. Section 1 step 5 drags obj_bird onto the TOP screen; section 4's objects/obj_bird/step.dss calls touch_pressed(); WS4's deliverables define W031 as "touch_* in an object whose screen is Top". So the very first program a beginner types (and the M5 tutorial) shows a warning squiggle, which teaches them to ignore warnings.
