The lint also encodes a wrong mental model: touch is a global input like a button, not a property of the instance's screen; only touch_in_instance(self) (and any on-instance touch event) can never fire for a top-screen object.
  *Fix:* Redefine W031 in contracts/diagnostics.md and WS4's DoD: fire only when touch_in_instance(self/id-of-a-top-screen-object) or an on-instance touch event is used by an object whose screen is Top. Plain touch_pressed/touch_check/touch_released/touch_x/touch_y are legal everywhere. Add a conformance fixture that compiles samples/flappy with zero warnings, and make "samples and templates compile with zero diagnostics" a CI check.

**Major gaps**

- **Language / events: touch event semantics undefined** (Section 4 Events, C6, WS4, WS7 manual). touch_pressed.dss / touch_released.dss / touch_held.dss are listed as object events but the contract never says whether they fire when the stylus touches THIS instance (GameMaker's Mouse Left Pressed) or anywhere on the bottom screen (GameMaker's Global Left Pressed, and what DSGM's Touch New Press did). Beginners making a touch button object will assume on-instance; beginners making a tap-to-flap game will assume anywhere. Whichever is chosen, half of them write code that silently never runs.
  *Fix:* In contracts/events.md define both: touch_pressed.dss (stylus down inside my bbox, bottom-screen instances only; a top-screen object with this event gets W031) and global_touch_pressed.dss (anywhere). Mirror for released/held. Document in the Two screens and touch chapter with one example of each.
- **Two-screen model: coordinate space and cross-screen collisions** (Section 3.3 Collision, Section 4 (Scopes, built-ins screen/view_x), C1 room.json, C6, WS6b room editor, WS7 manual). Section 3.3 says collision runs in "one logical coordinate space" across both screens, and section 4 says a room is one space with a view per screen. With both views at (0,0) (the default a room editor will produce), a wall on the top screen at (100,100) collides with the bird on the bottom screen at (100,100) even though they are on different physical screens. Conversely, a beginner who wants the bird to fall from the top screen into the bottom one has no supported way (screen is a manual property, there is no gap handling, and no rule says what the bottom view's y should be). DSGM's same-screen-only collisions were called a mistake, but the replacement is never specified for the learner.
  *Fix:* Add a room-level layout setting to C1: "Two separate screens" (default; each screen is its own 256x192 space, coordinates are screen-local, collisions and instance_place only match instances on the same screen) and "One tall world" (bottom view is locked to top view + 192 (+ optional gap), an instance's screen is derived from its y automatically, collisions are global). Freeze this in C1/C6 before phase0, make the room editor's stacked screens follow it, and make the manual's Rooms and views chapter open with it.
- **Scope: platformer template vs. no tiles / no solid** (Section 1 non-goals, Section 3.3 Backgrounds/Collision, C1, C3, C6, WS6b, WS7 templates). Section 1 says none of the omitted features "is needed for Flappy, a top-down game or a block platformer", and WS7 ships templates/platformer-starter. A block platformer built from obj_wall instances consumes one OBJ per visible block: a 16x12 grid of 16x16 blocks is 192 sprites against the 128-per-screen limit, and place_meeting-based movement needs those walls to exist as instances. With tile painting, solid and precise collision all omitted, the only workable pattern is invisible wall instances hand-placed over a hand-painted background PNG, which is exactly the clunky workflow that made DSGM levels painful. GB Studio's answer (verified at gbstudio.dev/docs/project-editor/scenes/collisions) is a Collision Tool that paints Solid / Top / Bottom / Left / Right / Ladder tiles over the PNG background at 8x8 resolution.
  *Fix:* Either (a) add a painted 8x8 collision grid per background (one byte per tile: none/solid/top-only) to C1 and C3, expose it as place_meeting(x, y, bg_solid) / position_meeting(x, y, bg_solid) plus a collision_solid.dss-style event or tile_solid_at(x, y), implement it in the room/background editor with pencil/fill/eraser, and make the platformer-starter template use it; or (b) drop platformer-starter and the "block platformer" claim from 0.1 and say so in the omission list. Option (a) is roughly a week for WS5+WS6b+WS2 and is the single feature that most widens what a beginner can make after Flappy.
- **Events: no Game Start / no persistent means no place to initialise once** (Section 4 Events, Section 1 non-goals (persistent), C6, C9 R5xx catalog, WS7 tutorial). The event set has room_start/room_end but no game_start/game_end, and persistent is omitted. Flappy's create.dss sets global.score = 0 and death calls room_restart(), which re-runs every Create. A beginner who adds a high score (the first thing they do after the tutorial) has nowhere to write global.best = 0 once: putting it in Create resets it every death, putting it in Room Start does the same, and reading global.best before assignment yields undefined and then an R5xx on undefined + 1. GameMaker's Game Start event (fires once, after all Create events of the first room, per manual.gamemaker.io Other_Events) exists precisely for this and is not in the omission list, so it is an unnoticed hole rather than a decision.
  *Fix:* Add game_start.dss and game_end.dss to C6 with GameMaker's timing (once, after the first room's Create events, before Room Start; game_restart re-fires it). Make the R5xx for arithmetic on undefined name the variable and suggest Game Start ("global.best has no value yet. Give it one in a Game Start event."). Add "keep a high score across restarts" as the tutorial's step 9 and a conformance fixture.
- **Language syntax: mandatory semicolons** (Section 4 Lexical and statements, WS4 DoD, C6). WS4's definition of done lists "missing semicolon" as one of the 20 most common beginner mistakes to diagnose. In GML a newline ends a statement and semicolons are optional (community consensus and the manual's style guidance; only for(;;) and do..until need them), so every GML tutorial a 12-year-old copies from YouTube omits them freely. Requiring them creates an error class the parent language does not have, and it will be the most frequent red squiggle in the M5 session.
  *Fix:* Make ';' optional in contracts/language.md: a newline terminates a statement when the expression is complete (no dangling operator or open bracket); the formatter inserts semicolons; the checker never errors on a missing one. Keep the friendly message only for the genuinely ambiguous cases (a line starting with '(' or '[' after an expression).
- **GML compatibility: known-but-missing names get only Levenshtein** (Section 1 non-goals, Section 4 Builtins, C2 builtins.json schema, C9, WS4 checker, WS7 manual). The product is sold as GML-flavoured, and beginners will paste code from GameMaker tutorials that uses keyboard_check(vk_left), mouse_x, instance_create_layer(x, y, "Instances", obj), image_alpha, draw_text_ext, ds_list_create, layer_*, sprite_get_width. The checker only offers did-you-mean over the builtins table, so keyboard_check would suggest nothing useful and image_alpha would be reported as an unknown variable. The prior-art report recommended keyboard_check/vk_* and instance_create_layer/_depth aliases; the unified plan dropped them without replacement.
  *Fix:* Add a compat table to contracts/builtins.json (kind: "unsupported" or "alias") consumed by the checker and language service: aliases for instance_create_layer/instance_create_depth (ignore the layer/depth argument, W lint) and keyboard_check/_pressed/_released with vk_left/right/up/down/space/enter mapped to btn_*; and a dedicated E2xx message for ~30 well-known GML names ("image_alpha isn't available on the DS in DSDude 0.1. Try visible = false or a second sprite."), each with a manual link. Put the list in the Differences from GameMaker chapter.
- **Sprite import: odd sizes fail instead of padding** (Section 1 step 2, Section 2.9, C3 (12 OBJ sizes), WS5 DoD). WS5's example E4xx is "spr_boss is 48x48; DS sprites must be one of 8x8 ... 64x64; use 64x64". A 12-year-old drawing in Paint produces 20x20, 24x24, 40x30 art constantly; turning that into an error with a list of twelve hardware shapes is exactly the leak the vision promises not to have ("nothing about OAM slots ... ever leaks"). Colour reduction is correctly "always a warning", but size is not.
  *Fix:* In the import dialog and asset pipeline, pad any frame up to the next of the 12 OBJ sizes with transparent pixels automatically (origin and bbox stay on the drawn pixels; the meter shows the real VRAM cost). Only frames larger than 64x64 produce an E4xx, phrased as "spr_boss is 100x100. DS sprites can be at most 64x64. Shrink it, or make it a Background." (metasprites composed of several OBJs are a v1.1 item).
- **Learnability: tutorial and manual have no delivery path inside the IDE** (Section 1 steps 1 and 4, WS6 deliverables, WS7 deliverables and DoD, M5, M6). docs/tutorial/flappy-bird.md and docs/manual/** are markdown files in the repo. The plan says F1 "opens its reference entry" but never says where (browser? dockview panel?), and nothing in WS6/WS7 puts the tutorial, the concept map or the error catalog inside the application. DSGM's number one review complaint was documentation; GB Studio's docs site is one click from the app and the app ships a sample project that plays before you learn anything. A beginner who has to find docs in a GitHub repo will not.
  *Fix:* Add to WS6/WS7: a Learn panel (dockview) that renders docs/tutorial and docs/manual markdown in-app with copyable code, opened on first launch; F1/hover "Learn more" opens the reference entry in that panel; Problems entries link to their errors.md entry; a Help menu with "Flappy Bird tutorial", "Differences from GameMaker", "Controls". Make the New Project wizard's Flappy Bird template the finished sample so first Play happens within one minute of install, then the tutorial rebuilds it from Empty.
- **Emulator controls are never shown to the beginner** (Section 2.6, Section 3.2 step 7, WS1 EmulatorManager, WS6 shell, M5). Section 2.6 notes melonDS ships with no key bindings and the IDE writes melonDS.toml before every launch, but nothing tells the beginner the mapping. Their first Play opens a window where nothing responds to WASD, and touch requires knowing that a mouse click on the lower half is the stylus. This is a guaranteed stall in the M5 session.
  *Fix:* Add a Controls card to the IDE: a toast/overlay on the first Play of a session ("Arrows = D-pad, X = A, Z = B, Enter = Start, click the bottom screen to touch"), a permanent Help > Controls page, the same mapping printed as the first Output line of every launch, and a settings page to rebind (writes both melonDS.toml and desmume.ini). Ensure the emulator window is brought to the foreground and the IDE does not steal focus when log lines arrive.
- **Audio: tracker-only music is not producible by a beginner** (Section 1 in-scope, Section 2.9 Sounds, C3 soundbank, WS5, WS7 templates, WS6 meters). Music is XM/MOD/IT/S3M only. A 12-year-old has MP3s from YouTube and no idea what a tracker is; dropping music.mp3 on Sounds is one of the first five things they will try and it currently dead-ends. WAV effects are fine, but the 1 MB soundbank cap and 22 kHz mono conversion are not surfaced as a meter either.
  *Fix:* Ship 3-4 CC0 tracker tracks and 8 CC0 effects with the templates ("Add from library" in the Sounds context menu); give mp3/ogg/m4a a dedicated E4xx ("The DS can't play MP3. Music must be a tracker file (.xm/.mod/.it/.s3m). Pick one from the built-in library or convert with ..."); add a soundbank meter to the status bar; put maxmod WAV streaming (mmStream from NitroFS) for long WAV music on the v1.1 list.
- **Object editor: one file per event turns one object into six tabs** (Section 2.7, Section 1 step 3, WS6 object editor deliverables, C12, C9). Per-event files are a good storage choice, but the plan's object editor is a properties form plus an event list that opens each .dss in its own Monaco tab. The Flappy bird spans six files (create, step, two collisions, alarm_0, functions). GameMaker Studio 2 stores events as separate files too, yet shows them stacked in one scrollable code window so the learner sees Create above Step. The rejected alternative ("fragile scroll-to-block text sync") is about storage, not display. functions.dss is also invisible: it is not an event, so "+ Add Event" never offers it and a beginner cannot discover where die() should live.
  *Fix:* In WS6's object editor, render all of an object's event files stacked in one panel (one Monaco model per file, headers like "Step - runs every frame", collapsible), with the event list acting as a jump bar; show Functions as a fixed pseudo-event at the bottom of the list that creates functions.dss on first click. Add E2xx "die() is a helper of obj_bird. To use it from obj_pipe, move it to Scripts." for cross-object calls. New event files should start with a one-line comment explaining when the event runs (the sample already models this).
- **Hardware leaks: flipping, rotation clipping, scale limits** (Section 3.3 Sprites, Section 4 sample comment, C6, C13, WS2/WS3, WS6 meters). Three affine behaviours are unspecified and each produces silent wrong rendering that a beginner cannot diagnose: (1) image_xscale = -1 (facing left, the most common thing after moving) must use the free OAM hflip/vflip bits, not one of the 32 affine sets, or a top-down game with 33 characters facing left stops flipping; (2) a rotated or scaled sprite clips at its corners unless the OAM double-size flag is set (GBATEK: without it "the edges of the rotated sprite will become invisible"), so the tutorial's tilting bird loses its wing tips; (3) image_xscale/yscale above 2 cannot be drawn by the hardware; and (4) what happens to the 33rd rotated instance on a screen is undefined.
  *Fix:* Add to C6/C13 and the runtime spec: xscale/yscale in {1, -1} with angle 0 map to flip bits and cost no affine set; any other angle/scale sets double-size automatically; scale is clamped to [1/16, 2] with W04x "DS sprites can only grow to 2x; use a bigger sprite"; when a screen exceeds 32 affine instances the extras draw unrotated and DSD|STAT reports it, surfaced as a red Affine meter. Remove "uses one of 32 affine slots per screen" from the tutorial's code comment; that belongs in the manual.
- **M5 acceptance gate design** (Section 1 (journey as acceptance test), Section 8 M5, Section 10 question 5, WS7 DoD, WS8). M5 is one tester, persona unspecified (a 30-year-old hobbyist and a 12-year-old differ enormously), on the developer machine (not the packaged installer), with only the tutorial measured. It does not measure time-to-first-Play, does not define "playable Flappy Bird", does not say where the tester gets bird.png/pipe.png/flap.wav, has no free-play segment (the DSGM failure mode is what happens after the tutorial, when the learner tries something the docs do not cover), and does not mention consent for recording a minor.
  *Fix:* Specify: at least two testers (one aged 12-15 with no coding background, one adult with some GameMaker exposure), run on the electron-builder installer output on a profile without MSYS2, tutorial assets bundled under Help > Tutorial assets, success = bird flaps on A and tap, pipes scroll, collision restarts, score draws. Measure separately: install to first Play of the sample (target < 5 min), tutorial completion (target 30, fail 60), then 15 minutes of free play with two prompts ("add a high score", "add a sound when you score") logged for confusions. Parent consent and screen-only recording for minors. Every confusion becomes an issue with an owner, as already planned.

**Minor gaps**

- **Numbers: beginner-visible formatting and edge rules unspecified** (Section 2.8, Section 4 Types, C6, WS7 manual). The int32/Q20.12 model is sound and explainable ("whole numbers, or decimals with about three places, up to about half a million"). But the visible consequences are not pinned down: string(x) of a fixed value could print 100.4998; GameMaker prints two decimal places for non-integers and none for integers (manual.gamemaker.io string()); a fixed array index (arr[i/2]) needs a rule; overflow behaviour differs between debug (trap) and release (wrap), which the manual must state in one sentence; the concept map's "very tiny fractions round a little" is the right voice but needs the two limits alongside it.
  *Fix:* In contracts/language.md: string() of a fixed value prints at most two decimals and drops trailing zeros (3.0 -> "3", 2.5 -> "2.5", 0.1 -> "0.1"), string_format for more; fixed array index floors with W04x; document "decimals bigger than 524,000 (or smaller than 1/4096) can't be stored" and that Play from the IDE uses the checking build (overflow message) while Export .nds wraps. Add these to the conformance corpus.
- **draw_rectangle has no runtime design; 3D engine is main-screen only** (Section 3.3, Section 4 Builtins, C2, WS2/WS3). draw_rectangle is in the builtins list but section 3.3 never says how it is drawn. The obvious DS route (3D quads) only exists on engine A, so it would work on one screen and not the other, which is a leak. Health bars are the classic beginner use.
  *Fix:* Either implement draw_rectangle on the per-screen text layer (8x8-snapped filled rectangles from a solid tile, cheap and symmetric) and document the snapping, or drop it from 0.1 and show the health-bar idiom with a stretched 8x8 sprite (image_xscale up to 2 on a wide OBJ) in the manual. Decide before builtins.json freezes.
- **Text drawing details leak or are missing** (Section 3.3 Backgrounds, Section 4 Built-ins/Constants, C6, WS3 font, WS7 manual). draw_text on a tile-based text layer snaps to an 8-pixel grid, so draw_text(x, y, ...) following a moving instance jitters in 8 px steps; the built-in font is ASCII only, so a 12-year-old typing an accent or emoji gets an undefined result; draw_set_color is "palette-indexed" but the constants list has no c_white/c_red/c_black, which every GML tutorial uses; touch_x/touch_y have no defined value when the stylus is up.
  *Fix:* Document the 8 px snap and offer draw_text only for HUD-style use in the manual; ship the 16 GML c_* constants mapped to a fixed 16-colour text palette; W lint for non-ASCII characters in string literals used with draw_text ("the DS font only has A-Z, 0-9 and punctuation"); define touch_x/touch_y as the last touched position and touch_check() as the guard.
- **Diagnostics voice: style guide, quick fixes and missing 'nothing happened' cases** (C9, WS4, WS6 Problems panel, WS7 language service). The E201 example is good, but the plan only fixes code ranges and the 20 compile-time mistakes. R510's text ("ran more than 200,000 instructions this frame") already uses jargon, and "wrong arity" will become a message unless a style rule forbids it. There is no quick fix for did-you-mean, no rule that a failed Play focuses Problems, and no warnings for the two most common silent failures: a room with no instances (black emulator) and an object with no sprite (invisible).
  *Fix:* Add to contracts/diagnostics.md: a banned-word list (instruction, token, identifier, operand, arity, expression, opcode, VRAM, OAM, palette slot), a template (what happened / what to do / where), codes shown after the message as a link, and examples for R5xx. Add W lints: room has no instances on either screen; object placed but has no sprite and no Draw event; sprite never used. Add a Monaco code action that applies the did-you-mean, and a toast "Fix 1 problem to play" that focuses Problems when compile fails.
- **Randomness: identical pipe heights every run** (Section 2.4 determinism, Section 4 Builtins math, C6, WS2). Deterministic traces are essential for testing, but if the runtime seeds identically every boot, the tutorial's irandom_range pipes are the same each Play and a beginner reads it as a bug. GameMaker's randomize() page could not be fetched (403), but the familiar GML behaviour is that beginners are told to call randomize() in Create; that ritual is not in the sample.
  *Fix:* Seed from the RTC/frame counter by default on the DS and only fix the seed when the ROM is launched with the trace flag or under dsdude-host; keep randomize() as a no-op alias for compatibility and mention it in the Differences chapter.
- **Music restarts on every room_restart** (Section 4 Builtins audio, C6, WS2/WS3). With persistent omitted, the tutorial's death path (alarm -> room_restart) re-runs the controller's Create, so audio_play_music restarts the track on every death. Beginners notice this immediately.
  *Fix:* Specify audio_play_music(mod) as a no-op when that module is already playing (audio_is_playing exists), and audio_stop_music as the explicit way to restart; document in the Sounds chapter.
- **Templates and samples are inconsistent across sections** (Section 1 step 1 and in-scope, WS7 deliverables). Step 1 of the journey offers templates Empty / Flappy Bird / Top-down / Touch minigame; WS7 ships templates/blank and templates/platformer-starter, and samples flappy / topdown-mini / touch-paint; section 1 in-scope says "three samples, two templates". A beginner sees whatever the wizard lists, so the lists must agree, and the tutorial assumes Empty while a Flappy template exists.
  *Fix:* One list in project-format's template registry: Empty, Flappy Bird (the sample, plays on first Play), Top-down, Touch paint, and Platformer only if the collision-grid gap is closed. Tutorial starts from Empty and says so.
- **Sprite import defaults** (Section 1 step 2, C1 sprite.json, WS5 preview API, WS6 import dialog). The dialog has an origin picker but no stated default; GameMaker's top-left default makes the tutorial's image_angle tilt rotate the bird around its corner. Frame detection (48x16 -> 3 frames) is a heuristic that misfires on a genuine 32x16 single-frame sprite (a real OBJ size). Bbox default is not stated.
  *Fix:* Default origin to centre for new imports (state it in C1), auto-bbox to the opaque bounds, make the frame count editable with an animated preview in the dialog, and treat an exact OBJ-size image as one frame unless the user changes it.
- **Saving: correct omission, but the substitute must be stated** (Section 1 non-goals, Section 4 Scopes, C6, C9, WS7 manual). Files/saves are omitted, which is defensible on DS homebrew (FAT/DLDI on flashcarts, melonDS needs a DLDI SD image, DeSmuME needs --slot1 R4 --slot1-fat-dir, per research-emulator.md). GB Studio ships Save Game / Load Game / If Game Data Saved events, so beginners coming from it will look for them. The plan never states that global.* survives room_goto/room_restart, which is the in-session substitute the tutorial depends on.
  *Fix:* State in contracts/language.md that globals persist across rooms and restarts and are cleared only by game_restart; put "Save high score to the SD card" on the v1.1 list with the DLDI/FolderSync melonDS config already documented; give a dedicated E2xx for ini_*/file_*/buffer_* names pointing to that entry.
- **Meters and labels leak vocabulary** (Section 1 step 5, C13, WS6 meters, Section 4 sample comment). Status bar shows "Palettes 1/16" and "Room RAM 12/1536 KB". Palettes conflates the 16 standard 4bpp palettes with the 16 extended 8bpp palettes (two different budgets per screen), and Room RAM means nothing to a learner. The tutorial code comment also mentions affine slots.
  *Fix:* Label meters in learner words with the hardware term in the tooltip: "Sprites on top 1/128", "Colours (top) 1/16" split into 16-colour and 256-colour budgets only when the second is non-zero, "Level memory 12/1536 KB"; keep hardware names in the Limits reference.
- **First run: downloads and SmartScreen** (Section 2.6, WS6 first-run wizard, WS8 deliverables, M6). WS8 says the first-run wizard "fetches the tools pack and melonDS", while electron-builder's extraResources bundles the tools pack; one of these is wrong. Downloading melonDS on first run adds a failure mode (school firewall, offline, antivirus) at the worst possible moment, and an unsigned NSIS installer triggers SmartScreen, which is the DSGM "buggy installer" experience in modern form.
  *Fix:* Bundle both the tools pack and melonDS in the installer (GPL is fine as separate processes with the license bundle; ~20 MB extra), keep the download path only for updating the emulator; budget for code signing or document the "More info -> Run anyway" path with a screenshot on the download page; make offline install part of the M6 clean-VM test.

**What the critic found right**

- Prebuilt runtime plus bytecode (GB Studio's architecture) so Play never needs a compiler on the learner's PC, Play is sub-3 s warm, and runtime faults say object/event/file/line instead of an ARM exception. This is the single decision that most separates it from DSGM's regenerate-main.c-and-die fate.
- The beginner-journey table is used as the literal M5 acceptance script and a human, not an agent, is the gate; confusions become owned issues.
- Hardware limits are live meters from one runtime-limits.json (GB Studio's A: 0/20 S: 0/96 pattern), colour reduction is always a warning with a preview, and the E4xx examples name the asset, the limit and a fix.
- A logical instance list decoupled from OAM, with per-screen shadow OAM built each frame, directly fixes DSGM's 128-instances-per-room, same-screen-only-collision flaw.
- Screen as a visible per-object property, two backgrounds per room and the stacked two-screen room editor copy the part of DSGM that demonstrably worked for beginners.
- '=' inside a condition compiles as '==' with W030, learning from DBAS's single-equals confusion; did-you-mean over builtins plus project symbols; compile-time detection of instance variables never assigned anywhere in the object (better than GameMaker's runtime error).
- One .dss file per event mirrors GMS2's storage and gives 'obj_bird / Step, line 3' locations for free; object-scoped functions.dss keeps the Flappy sample readable.
- The number model avoids doubles on a CPU with no FPU, uses 64-bit intermediates, traps overflow in debug with the expression named, and already anticipates the squaring-positions trap (W040).
- Per-frame instruction watchdog turns an infinite while loop into a friendly runtime error instead of a frozen emulator, plus the on-device red error box with START-to-restart and the SELECT console overlay.
- Reference, completion, hover and errors.md are generated from the same builtins.json the compiler uses, so documentation cannot drift, addressing DSGM's and NESmaker's top review complaint.
- The omission list is published in the manual's Differences from GameMaker chapter with a concept map in plain language, and the v1 surface is frozen against GML-parity scope creep.
- Sprite import dialog with original-vs-DS side-by-side preview, automatic 16/256 mode and a transparency-source choice hides palettes without pretending they do not exist.
- Templates and finished samples in the New Project wizard rather than NESmaker-style empty modules; WS7 (learnability) starts at day 3, not at the end.
- melonDS as default with DeSmuME as a second profile, the IDE writing the emulator config itself, and both nocash log protocols so show_debug_message reaches the Output panel on either emulator.
- The 20-most-common-beginner-mistakes checker DoD with one test per message and 'one mistake yields one diagnostic' is the right bar for a 12-year-old audience.

### Parallel execution (agent-fleet coordinator)

The plan's decomposition is sound in principle (contracts first, fixtures as executable contracts, host oracle, per-stream isolation, soft Phase-0 gate, ADR-pending rule), but as written it is not executable in parallel on this machine in the stated time. Three things break it: (1) the hardware: 11.3 GB of RAM cannot host 7-8 Claude Code instances plus three Electron dev IDEs, emulators and vitest/tsc workers; concurrency must run in waves of 3-5 with the Electron streams staggered; (2) the ownership map: about a dozen path collisions and orphans (tools/** vs gen-docs and fetch-vendor, apps/ide/** vs electron-builder.yml, generated files inside other owners' trees, samples/hello C code under WS7, the "fixtures/<ws>/" rule that no listed fixture path obeys, "never edit contracts/" vs per-file contract ownership) mean the mechanical hook would reject legitimate work or let two streams write the same files on day one; (3) Phase 0 as scoped (14 contracts incl. a 70-opcode ISA and 90 documented builtins, packages/dsdb, project-format, ipc-contract, AST skeleton, mock-host, 10 conformance programs, three samples) is 60-90 Claude-hours, i.e. 4-5 calendar days for a Claude WS0 and 2-3 weeks if the human writes it, and the hardest artefact (C2) written without implementation feedback will churn under both WS2 and WS4 while changes can only flow through WS0 at checkpoints. Serial dependencies the plan underplays: WS1's toolchain install gates WS3, WS5's real-tool tests, WS0's GRF/soundbank fixtures and M0; C2/packages/dsdb gate both WS2 and WS4 simultaneously; WS7's real language-service work needs WS4's binder and checker (weeks 2-3), not an AST freeze at D+3; WS6 needs C4 types and a MockBuildService on D+1 that WS1 only produces later; WS4's execution goldens need WS2's engine (weeks 3-6), not "day 7-10". Windows specifics are also unhandled: system-wide core.autocrlf=true with no .gitattributes will break the sh hook, contaminate Makefiles and risk golden/binary fixture corruption; root package-lock.json is touched by every dependency add (verified) and the chore(deps) rule cannot be enforced in a pre-commit hook (no message there); vitest.workspace.ts no longer exists in Vitest 5; shared %LOCALAPPDATA% emulator folder and %APPDATA% settings race across instances. Verdict: launch with a minimum-viable Phase 0 (about 2 days for a Claude WS0 with the user approving), WS0 as a persistent Claude instance, a corrected ownership map, .gitattributes and per-worktree DSDUDE_HOME isolation, a tiered contract-change protocol with 24h SLA for C2/C6/C11, and waves of 4-5 instances rather than 7-8.

**Blocker gaps**

- **Hardware capacity for concurrent instances** (Plan 2.10 (calendar/concurrency), 7.2 (recommended concurrency), 9 risk 12; meta decision 'Phase 0 length, fan-out gate, concurrency'). This machine has 11.3 GB RAM (Ryzen 7 5825U laptop, 16 threads, 84.7 GB free disk). The plan runs 7-8 Claude Code instances (each a Node process, ~300-500 MB), three of which (WS6, WS6b, WS7) run an Electron + Vite dev IDE (~1-1.5 GB each), plus melonDS windows (WS1, WS3, WS6, WS8), vitest worker pools and tsgo in every worktree, and `make -j16`. Realistic demand is 12-16 GB; the machine will swap and every stream's tests will time out. Seven worktrees x node_modules (Electron, monaco, pixi, playwright) is another 7-12 GB of disk, acceptable but slow to install.
  *Fix:* Run in waves of 4-5 instead of 7-8, ordered by the dependency graph: Wave 1 (D+1): WS1, WS2, WS4, WS5 (all headless Node/C); WS3 joins on day 2 once WS1's install is verified. Wave 2 (CP-B, D+7): WS6 (the first Electron stream). Wave 3 (CP-C, D+14): WS7; WS6b only if memory allows after measuring. Add to CLAUDE.md: `vitest --pool=threads --maxWorkers=2` per package, no watch mode, one emulator process per worktree, close the dev IDE when not testing. Consider running the headless streams (WS2, WS4, WS5) as remote/cloud Claude Code sessions if available. If the laptop has a free SO-DIMM slot, 32 GB total makes 7 concurrent feasible.
- **C2 (bytecode + builtins) as a Phase-0 freeze consumed by two streams** (Plan 5 (C2 row), 6 WS0/WS2/WS4, 7.3 CP-A handoffs, 7.4; meta contracts 'C2', workstreams WS0.owned_paths 'packages/dsdb/** (until CP-A)'). WS0 must write a 70-opcode ISA, calling convention, register-window/slot layout, `with` semantics, event ids, 9 sections, DBG table and a `.dsda` assembler/disassembler before either the codegen author (WS4) or the VM author (WS2) exists. Both start on D+1, both will discover mismatches (e.g. how `with` iterates, how `other` slots resolve, whether CALLN passes args in a window or on a stack, how strings are refcounted across RET) and both can only change C2 through WS0 at checkpoints (D+3, D+7, D+14, then weekly). Two streams carrying different `// ADR-pending` assumptions for a week is exactly the drift the ABI hash is meant to catch, but at integration time. `packages/dsdb` is also owned by WS0 until CP-A while WS2 needs its assembler for every fixture and WS4 needs its disassembler for every golden on day 1; any bug in it blocks both. The plan calls it ~300 lines; a table-driven assembler/disassembler with labels, 9 sections and symbolic operands is 800-1500 lines with tests.
  *Fix:* Ship a minimal C2 in Phase 0: container/cells/calling convention/event ids/`.dsda` grammar plus a single `contracts/opcodes.json` holding only the ~25 opcodes needed for hello and conformance v0 (LOADK/LOADI/LOADB/LOADUNDEF, MOV, ADD/SUB/MUL/DIV/IDIV/MOD/NEG, EQ..GE/NOT, JMP/JMPT/JMPF, CALLN/RET, CONCAT/TOSTR, GETGLOB/SETGLOB, HALT) with the rest listed as reserved names. Generate `runtime/gen/opcodes.h` and `packages/dsdb/src/gen/opcodes.ts` from that JSON so adding an opcode is a one-file change. Hand `contracts/dsdb.md`, `contracts/opcodes.json` and `packages/dsdb` to WS4 at the phase0 tag (not CP-A) with WS2 as mandatory co-signer, keep `builtins.json` ordinals append-only under WS0, and give C2/C6/C11 a 24-hour merge SLA in weeks 1-4 (WS0 merges these daily, not at checkpoints). Explicitly allow `git merge ws4-compiler` into `ws2-runtime` (and vice versa) for consumption between checkpoints.
- **Path ownership collisions and orphans** (Plan 3.4 (layout + rules), 6 (every 'Owned paths'), 7.5 preamble and per-stream prompts; meta workstreams[*].owned_paths, contracts C4/C5/C10/C14). Collisions: (1) `tools/**` (WS0) vs `tools/fetch-vendor.ps1` (WS1 from CP-A) and `tools/gen-docs/**` (WS7). (2) `apps/ide/**` (WS6, exceptions only editors/ and monaco/) vs `apps/ide/electron-builder.yml` (WS8); also `apps/ide/package.json` is WS6's but WS7 (monaco-editor) and WS6b (pixi) must add deps there. (3) `docs/reference/functions.md` is emitted by both `tools/gen-builtins.ts` (WS0) and `tools/gen-docs` (WS7), and `errors.md` is both a WS4 deliverable and a WS7 generated file, under WS7's `docs/reference/**`. (4) `packages/compiler/src/gen/builtins.ts` and `packages/language-service/src/gen/builtins.ts` are generated by WS0's tool inside WS4's and WS7's trees; the rule 'generated files are committed only by the generator owner' makes WS0 commit into paths the hook says WS0 does not own. (5) `samples/**` goes WS0 -> WS7 at CP-A, but `samples/hello` is a BlocksDS C program WS1 writes and maintains, and `samples/flappy` is WS4's primary compiler fixture, WS5's asset fixture and WS6's IDE fixture. (6) `fixtures/runtime/hello/{arm9.elf,hello.nds}` is produced by WS1 (deliverable), owned by WS3 (`fixtures/runtime/**`) and listed as WS0's C14 output. (7) `fixtures/ide/mock-host` is a C14/WS0 Phase-0 fixture and a WS6 CP-A deliverable. (8) `fixtures/bytecode/bench.dsda` is a WS2 deliverable but `fixtures/bytecode` is WS0's; nobody regenerates `hello.dsdb` when C2 changes. (9) `fixtures/conformance/**/*.dss` is WS4's but the expected `.log`/`.jsonl` outputs (produced by WS2's host runner) are orphans. (10) WS3's selftest needs GRFs 'from fixtures/assets/' which is WS5's after phase0. (11) `packages/lang/src/ast.ts` is WS0's until CP-A inside WS4's `packages/lang/**`, so the parser author cannot touch its own AST for 3 days. (12) `packages/*/CLAUDE.md` is WS0's in meta but 'each package owns its CLAUDE.md' in plan 3.4. (13) The shared preamble says 'Never edit contracts/' while WS2/WS3/WS4/WS5 own contract files after phase0, and C4/C10 (WS1) and C5 (WS6) final owners have no contract file in their owned paths at all (`contracts/toolchain-api.md`, `cli.md`, `ipc.md`). (14) The rule 'streams add fixtures only under fixtures/<ws>/' is contradicted by every listed fixture path (fixtures/compiler, fixtures/assets, fixtures/runtime-core ...). Orphans (no owner): root `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `.gitattributes`, `.editorconfig`, `.npmrc`, `.vscode/`, `.claude/settings*.json`; `scripts/` beyond four named files; `runtime/data/**` (8x8 font referenced by WS3's Makefile `BINDIRS := data`), `runtime/CLAUDE.md`, `runtime/package.json`; `docs/research/**` (8 existing files); `docs/status/`; `vendor/README.md` is inside gitignored `vendor/` (needs `!vendor/README.md` in WS0's .gitignore); `packages/toolchain/**` and `packages/cli/**` after week 3 when the WS1 instance 'becomes' WS8 whose owned_paths omit them; `.github/**` is WS8's from week 3 yet WS0's DoD requires a byte-identical gen-builtins check 'in CI'.
  *Fix:* Replace the prose lists with one machine-readable `tools/ownership.json` (path glob -> owner, with `from`/`until` checkpoint tags) from which CODEOWNERS is generated, and resolve as follows: WS0 owns `tools/gen-builtins.ts`, `tools/check-ownership.ts`, `tools/checkpoint.ps1`, `tools/phase0/**`, root dotfiles incl. `.gitattributes`, `README.md`, `LICENSE*`, `.claude/**`, `docs/research/**`, `docs/kickoff/**`; WS1 owns `tools/fetch-vendor.ps1`, `scripts/install-toolchain.ps1`, `scripts/smoke-test.ps1`, `samples/hello/**`, `fixtures/runtime/hello/**`, `fixtures/build/**`, `contracts/toolchain-api.md`, `contracts/cli.md`, and keeps them as WS8 (state 'WS8 = WS1 paths + tests/e2e, .github, electron-builder.yml, scripts/release.ps1, scripts/ci.ps1, CHANGELOG.md, docs/qa'); WS2 owns `fixtures/bytecode/**`, `fixtures/conformance/expected/**`, `runtime/CLAUDE.md`, `runtime/package.json`; WS3 owns `runtime/data/**`, `fixtures/runtime/**` except hello; WS4 owns `packages/lang/**` in full from the tag, `samples/flappy/**` and `samples/minimal/**` until M2 then WS7, `fixtures/conformance/**/*.dss`, `packages/compiler/src/diagnostics/catalog.ts` (the error catalog gen-docs reads); WS5 owns `fixtures/assets/**` and provides `fixtures/assets/grf/**` for WS3 in its first two days; WS6 owns `apps/ide/**` except `src/renderer/editors/**`, `src/renderer/monaco/**` and `electron-builder.yml`, plus `fixtures/ide/**` (mock-host is WS6's, not C14's), `contracts/ipc.md`; WS7 owns `packages/monaco-dss/**` (move Monaco glue out of apps/ide into its own workspace package so it has its own package.json), `tools/gen-docs/**`, all of `docs/reference/**` (gen-builtins stops emitting docs; gen-docs is the only docs generator), `samples/topdown-mini`, `samples/touch-paint`, `templates/**`, `docs/status/**` per stream file owned by that stream. Add a `generated` list in ownership.json (runtime/gen/**, packages/*/src/gen/**, docs/reference/**) that any commit may touch when the generator input changes in the same commit. Delete the `fixtures/<ws>/` sentence. Write `contracts/README.md` with the per-file owner table and change the preamble to 'edit only the contract files you own; everything else via ADR'. Preinstall monaco-editor, pixi.js, dockview etc. in Phase 0 so no stream needs to edit another's package.json.

**Major gaps**

- **Line endings and binary fixtures on Windows** (Plan 3.4 rules, 6 WS0 deliverables, 6 WS2 (host trace), 9 (no risk entry); meta WS0.deliverables). Git on this machine has system-level `core.autocrlf=true` (Git for Windows default) and the plan has no `.gitattributes`. Consequences with seven instances committing: `.githooks/pre-commit` (a sh script) checked out with CRLF fails under Git's sh ('$'\r': command not found'), so the mechanical ownership guard silently does not run; `runtime/Makefile` checked out with CRLF gives `NAME := dsdude_runtime\r` under MSYS2 make; text-like binaries (`.dsda` is fine, but small `.dsdb`, `.grf`, `.bin`, `.nds` fixtures without an early NUL can be misdetected as text and converted); vitest snapshots of `.dsda` and JSONL traces drift between LF and CRLF, and the host runner's `fopen(...,"w")` on MinGW writes CRLF in text mode, so 'expected trace' files written by WS2 on Windows will not match traces compared byte-wise or generated in CI on Linux. Biome's formatter also needs an explicit lineEnding.
  *Fix:* Phase 0 day 1: commit `.gitattributes` with `* text=auto eol=lf`, `*.sh text eol=lf`, `.githooks/* text eol=lf`, `Makefile* text eol=lf`, `*.mk text eol=lf`, `*.ps1 text eol=crlf`, and `*.dsdb *.grf *.bin *.nds *.elf *.png *.wav *.xm *.mod *.it *.s3m *.sav binary`; `.editorconfig` end_of_line=lf; biome.json `formatter.lineEnding: "lf"`; in CLAUDE.md require `git config core.autocrlf false` in each worktree (or set it in the shared repo config once); require the host runner and every golden writer to open files in binary mode (`"wb"`) and compare traces line-wise after stripping `\r`. Add a Phase-0 test that runs the hook and asserts it executed.
- **Phase 0 scope vs 2-3 days** (Plan 2.10, 6 WS0, 7.1, 8 (phase0 row); meta WS0.deliverables/definition_of_done, contracts C14, decision 'Phase 0 length'). Itemised, the Phase-0 list is roughly: monorepo + hooks + CODEOWNERS + 10 CLAUDE.md files 3-6 h; the eight small contracts 3-5 h; project-format schemas/load/save/migrations/tests 4-8 h; language.md + events.md 4-8 h; builtins.json with params/types/doc/example/allowedEvents for ~90 entries 3-6 h; gen-builtins with four emitters and a byte-identical check 3-5 h; dsdb.md with 70 opcodes/calling convention/sections/DBG/.dsda grammar 6-12 h; packages/dsdb assembler+disassembler+encoder+decoder with tests 6-12 h; hello.dsda/.dsdb 1 h; asset fixtures incl. an XM module 2-3 h; 10 conformance programs with hand-computed expected output 3-4 h; three samples 2-4 h; ipc-contract v0 2-3 h; AST skeleton 1-2 h; mock-host stub 2-4 h; kickoff prompts/tag 1-2 h. Total 60-90 Claude-hours: 4-5 calendar days for a Claude WS0 working with human review, and 2-3 weeks if the human authors contracts by hand. The gate also bundles WS1's 'hello.nds boots in melonDS from dsdude play', which depends on the untested silent BlocksDS install (risk 1) and on packages/cli + packages/toolchain existing; the plan's own fallback ('launch anyway on day 3') admits the gate is soft. Two contract items are unverifiable as specified: conformance expected outputs need the number-to-string formatting rule for Q20.12 (`string(1/3)` prints what?), which no contract defines; and hand-computing fixed-point results is error-prone without the runtime.
  *Fix:* Adopt this minimum-viable Phase 0 (about 25-40 Claude-hours, 2 days, WS0 = a Claude instance, user reviews at end of each day). Day 0 (user, 1 h): create C:\dev, git init, move vendor/ and docs/research, answer open questions 1, 3, 4. Day 1 (WS0): (a) monorepo with ALL pinned deps from section 2.5 installed into the right packages now, `vitest.config.ts` with `test.projects`, `.gitattributes`, `.editorconfig`, `.gitignore` (+`!vendor/README.md`), `.githooks/pre-commit` (biome + ownership by branch prefix) and `commit-msg` (lockfile rule), `tools/ownership.json` + generated CODEOWNERS, `tools/check-ownership.ts`, `tools/checkpoint.ps1`; (b) empty-but-green skeletons for every workspace package (src/index.ts exporting interface types, one passing test, 2-page CLAUDE.md with owner, contracts, isolation, env-isolation block); (c) small contracts copied out of PLAN.md: diagnostics.md, runtime-limits.json, log-protocol.md, cli.md draft, ipc.md channel list with zod stubs, `packages/toolchain/src/api.ts` = C4 types incl. `compileProject()`/`packAssets()` signatures and a 30-line MockBuildService; (d) C1 project-format.md + zod + load/save (no migrations: there are no v0 projects yet) + samples/minimal + samples/flappy v0 with script-generated PNGs. Day 2 (WS0): (e) language.md v0.1 (EBNF, precedence, promotion, the `string(x)` formatting rule, truthiness, with/other, arrays, event names) + events.md, owner WS4 from the tag; (f) minimal C2 as described in the C2 gap: dsdb.md v0.1 + opcodes.json (~25 opcodes) + builtins.json with name/kind/params/returns/category/allowedEvents/pure for all ~90 but doc/example only for the ~30 Flappy uses (rest 'TODO(WS7)', WS7 owns docs anyway) + gen-builtins/gen-opcodes + table-driven packages/dsdb + hello.dsda->hello.dsdb round-trip; (g) `tools/phase0/make-fixtures.ts` generating the 16x16x3 sprite, 256x192 background and a 1 s WAV; conformance v0 = 5 pure-computation programs with expected DSD|LOG output; (h) `docs/kickoff/wsN.md` and `git tag phase0`. Defer and hand out at the tag: C7 AST skeleton (WS4 D+1), C3 assetpack.md (WS5 D+1 from section 2.9), C11 dsd_platform.h (WS2 D+1), fixtures/ide/mock-host (WS6), samples/hello + fixtures/runtime/hello + fixtures/build (WS1), XM fixture (WS5), bench.dsda (WS2), conformance 6-10 (WS4), builtin docs/examples (WS7), migrations (WS0 when needed), full ipc-contract (WS6 D+1). The tag does not wait for WS1's emulator boot; that is WS1's own day-3 target.
- **Single human as WS0, merger, checkpoint runner and approver** (Plan 6 WS0, 7.2, 7.3, 9 risk 13; meta WS0.effort_estimate). The user is simultaneously contract author (2-3 days full time), the only merger for 7-8 branches, the checkpoint runner (2 h each), ADR reviewer, lockfile regenerator ('WS0 regenerates on conflict'), install consent-giver, the only pair of eyes on emulator windows (Claude cannot see a melonDS window), permission-prompt answerer for up to 8 instances, M5 tester recruiter and flashcart owner. Seven Claude instances each produce thousands of lines a day; one person cannot review that, so review must be mechanical and the human's job must shrink to decisions. The plan half-says WS0 'is the user's own steering session' but budgets it as the user's time.
  *Fix:* Make WS0 an explicit, persistent Claude Code instance running in `C:\dev\dsdude` on `main` (the 5th/6th instance in wave 1). It writes Phase 0, runs `tools/checkpoint.ps1` (merge each ws branch in dependency order with `--no-ff`, regenerate the lockfile, run `npm run check && npm test` and the host goldens, run `dsdude play samples/hello` via py-desmume headless, write `docs/status/checkpoint-N.md`), drafts each ADR decision with a recommended answer, and integrates `main` daily rather than weekly. The user's role: answer ADRs and open questions with yes/no, do physical/visual tasks (install consent, look at a melonDS window when a screenshot is ambiguous, flashcart, M5 tester), and approve the checkpoint report. Run every instance in the same permission mode with an allowlist committed in `.claude/settings.json` (owned by WS0) so the user is not the prompt-answering bottleneck. Budget the human at 1-2 h/day, not 2 h per checkpoint.
- **npm workspaces lockfile and the chore(deps) rule** (Plan 3.4 rules, 7.5 preamble; meta decision 'Repository location and multi-instance governance'). Verified in a scratch experiment with npm 11.13: `npm install --save <dep>` run inside a workspace package updates only the root `package-lock.json` (no nested lock), so every dependency any stream adds is an edit to the one file every other worktree also edits; with seven streams rebasing daily, lockfile conflicts are guaranteed and 'WS0 regenerates on conflict' becomes daily WS0 work. The enforcement mechanism is also wrong: 'the ownership hook allows the lockfile only in commits whose message starts with chore(deps):' cannot be implemented in a pre-commit hook, which takes no parameters and runs before the message exists; only `commit-msg` sees the message file. Additionally the plan says streams run `npm install`; if anyone uses `npm ci` in a worktree whose package.json and lock disagree it fails.
  *Fix:* (1) Preinstall the entire pinned stack from section 2.5 into the right packages during Phase 0 so streams almost never add dependencies in weeks 1-6. (2) Streams commit `package.json` changes only and never `package-lock.json`; WS0's checkpoint/daily merge script runs `git checkout --theirs package-lock.json && npm install` on main and commits `chore(deps): regenerate lockfile` once. (3) If a stream must commit the lock, enforce it in `.githooks/commit-msg` (which sees both the message and the staged file list), not pre-commit. (4) Document `npm install` (never `npm ci`) in worktrees and add `.npmrc` with `package-lock=true`, `save-exact=true`, `fund=false`, `audit=false`. (5) All worktrees share the npm cache, so parallel installs are safe; note ~1-1.5 GB node_modules per worktree.
- **Per-worktree runtime isolation (emulator config, settings, ports)** (Plan 2.6, 3.2 step 7, 6 WS1/WS6, 7.5 preamble; meta contract C4). melonDS 1.1's official build is portable: `melonDS.toml` lives next to `melonDS.exe` and `<rom>.sav`/savestates next to the ROM (research-emulator.md). The plan puts one shared copy in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DSDude\emulators\melonDS-1.1\` and has EmulatorManager rewrite `melonDS.toml` before every launch; WS1, WS3, WS6 and WS8 launching concurrently will overwrite each other's toml (GDB on/off, key bindings) mid-run. DeSmuME's `desmume.ini` beside its exe has the same problem. `%APPDATA%\DSDude\settings.json` is shared by the WS6, WS6b and WS7 dev IDEs. Three electron-vite dev servers and Playwright `_electron` runs contend for ports. `pacman`/`wf-pacman -Syu` by any stream after Phase 0 can replace msys2-runtime under other streams' running `make`.
  *Fix:* Introduce `DSDUDE_HOME` (default `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DSDude`), set per worktree in each kickoff prompt (`C:\dev\dsdude-ws3\.dsdude`); EmulatorManager installs/copies emulators and writes toml/ini under it (19 MB per copy is nothing), settings.json lives there in dev, and the dev IDE sets `app.setPath('userData', ...)` from it. Set `strictPort: false` and a per-worktree base port via env. Add to the preamble: 'never run pacman/wf-pacman after phase0; toolchain changes are WS1 ADRs applied at a checkpoint when no builds are running'.
- **Kickoff prompt self-sufficiency** (Plan 3.4, 7.2, 7.5; meta WS0.deliverables 'kickoff prompts'). A fresh instance given the preamble plus its stream line would still be missing: how its worktree and branch are created (`git worktree add ..\dsdude-ws4 -b ws4-compiler` appears only in section 3.4; nobody is told who runs it), how the ownership hook identifies the stream (branch prefix? git config? env var?) and that `core.hooksPath` must be set once, which files the contract IDs (C4, C9...) map to, the machine facts it needs (MSYS2 path, Wonderful env block, emulator location, Node 24 strips TS so `node tools/x.ts` works), that it cannot see emulator windows and must verify through `DSD|` log lines and py-desmume screenshots, where to write progress (no `docs/status/` exists), the env-isolation block above, the no-pacman rule, timeouts on every spawned process, and what to do at a checkpoint (push? stop? tag?). It contradicts itself ('Never edit contracts/' vs per-file contract ownership). It tells WS4 and WS5 to deliver `dsdude compile --json` / `dsdude assets --json` although `packages/cli` is WS1's. It has each instance read PLAN.md (~37K tokens) plus 14 contracts plus builtins.json before writing code, which is re-read after every context compaction. 'rebase on main daily' is risky once WS0 has merged part of a branch; a stream that rebases after a squash or conflict-resolved merge re-applies its own patches.
  *Fix:* Generate `docs/kickoff/wsN.md` per stream in Phase 0 containing: the exact worktree/branch commands (run by the user or WS0), `DSDUDE_WS=WSn` written with `git config --worktree` (enable `extensions.worktreeConfig`) and read by the hook, the env-isolation block (DSDUDE_HOME, ports), machine facts, the list of contract files with paths and versions, the stream's DoD and isolation strategy copied verbatim, a 'how you verify without eyes' paragraph (DSD| lines, `dsdude screenshot` via py-desmume, Read the PNG), a `docs/status/wsN.md` progress file the stream owns, the checkpoint ritual (commit, push branch, update status, stop touching main), and 'merge main into your branch daily; never rebase after a checkpoint'. Make the per-package CLAUDE.md the primary brief (2 pages) and point at PLAN.md sections by number instead of requiring a full read. Replace the CLI deliverables with a C10 plugin rule: each package exports `cliCommands` from its index and WS1's `dsdude` registers them, or each package ships its own `bin` and `dsdude` delegates.
- **Hidden serial dependency through WS1's toolchain install** (Plan 2.1 Plan B, 6 WS1/WS3/WS5/WS6, 7.1, 8 M0, 9 risk 1; meta WS3.depends_on, WS5.depends_on). The untested silent BlocksDS/Wonderful install (risk 1) is on the critical path of more streams than the plan states: WS3 (day 1, cannot build the selftest), WS5's real grit/mmutil integration tests, WS0's own Phase-0 GRF and soundbank fixtures for WS3's selftest (they need grit and mmutil, so WS0 cannot seed them until WS1 finishes on the same machine), WS6's M0 (needs WS1's real BuildService and fixtures/runtime/hello by D+7 although WS1 estimates 1.5-2 weeks to M0), and WS8. If Plan B (devkitPro) triggers, `runtime/Makefile` (WS3), `tools/fetch-vendor.ps1` (WS1) and the licensing story change via ADR, stalling WS3. The plan also has WS1 build hello with its own Makefile while `buildRuntime()` invokes WS3's `runtime/Makefile`, two Makefile conventions from day one.
  *Fix:* Make the install the first task of hour zero with the user present (already planned) and add a hard decision point at end of day 1: if the silent path fails, WS1 switches to the Wonderful manual method immediately (same BLOCKSDS layout, no Makefile change) and only escalates to devkitPro by ADR. Move GRF/soundbank fixture production for the selftest to WS5's first two days (`fixtures/assets/grf/**`, it owns fixtures/assets) or let WS3 generate them under `fixtures/runtime/selftest/**` itself. Have `samples/hello` use the same `rom_arm9` Makefile shape as `runtime/Makefile` so `buildRuntime()` has one convention. State M0's real precondition: WS1's `packRom()` + `EmulatorManager.launch()` + log capture, not the full BuildService.
- **WS7 dependency on WS4 (AST at D+3) and WS4 dependency on WS2 (execution goldens at D+7-10)** (Plan 5 (C7), 6 WS4/WS7, 7.3 CP-A; meta contracts C7, WS7.depends_on). Freezing C7 at CP-A means WS4 must publish a stable AST three days into writing a parser, before the binder and checker exist; the language service's useful features (unknown-variable squiggles, go-to-definition, scope-aware completion) need binder symbol tables and checker diagnostics, which arrive in WS4 weeks 2-3. Freezing the AST early guarantees either a premature freeze or a stream of C7 ADRs. Likewise 'execution goldens arrive with dsdude-host around day 7-10' is true only for pure-computation programs; anything touching instances, `with`, collisions, alarms, rooms or draw needs WS2's engine (weeks 3-6), so WS4's codegen for those constructs is untested at runtime until M2-M4 and its only golden is its own disassembly.
  *Fix:* Change C7 to a narrow `LanguageServiceHost` interface (`parse(text) -> {diagnostics, spans}`, `symbolsAt(file, offset)`, `completionsAt(...)`, `hover(name)`) that WS4 implements and WS7 consumes, published at CP-B (D+7) not CP-A; WS7 spends D+3..D+7 on builtins-only completion, Monarch tokenizer, docs, templates and gen-docs, which is what its prompt already lists first. Order the conformance corpus by runtime feature availability (v0 pure computation; v1 strings/arrays; v2 instances/events; v3 with/collisions/alarms; v4 rooms/draw) and make WS2's first-week deliverable the loader + VM + strings + `show_debug_message` so v0/v1 goldens exist by D+7; WS4's DoD then reads 'every conformance tier passes on the host as soon as that tier's runtime feature lands'. Consistent with the RAM waves, start WS7 at D+7.
- **Contract-change protocol weight and timing** (Plan 7.4, 7.3, 3.4 rules; meta decision 'Repository location and multi-instance governance'). Every contract change requires an ADR file with five sections, a version bump, a CHANGELOG entry, regenerated files and fixtures 'in the same PR', merged only by WS0 'normally at a checkpoint' (weekly after D+14). A builtins.json change (a parameter type, a default, a new allowedEvents entry) will happen 10-30 times in weeks 1-4 as WS4's checker and WS2's implementations meet reality; each one regenerates into four owners' trees, which the hook forbids for anyone but WS0, so every such change is WS0 work and waits up to a week, while streams accumulate `// ADR-pending` markers that nobody inventories. There are no PRs without a remote, so 'PR' should read 'branch merged by WS0'.
  *Fix:* Tier the protocol: T0 (doc/example text, comments, additive fields with defaults) = the owner commits directly with a CHANGELOG line; T1 (additive: new builtin appended, new opcode, new optional IPC field, new limit key) = owner commits + version minor bump + regenerate + CHANGELOG, WS0 reviews within 24 h at the daily integration; T2 (breaking) = ADR + co-sign + WS0 merge. Let the ownership hook accept generated paths in any commit that also changes their generator input. Add `tools/adr-pending.ts` that greps all branches for `ADR-pending` and lists them in the checkpoint report. Replace 'PR' with 'branch' in 7.4 unless a GitHub remote is chosen (open question 6).
- **Missing contract for the compile()/packAssets() programmatic APIs and MockBuildService availability** (Plan 5 (C4), 6 WS1/WS6, 7.3 CP-B; meta contracts C4, WS6.effort_estimate isolation). C4 defines BuildService, packRom, EmulatorManager, and CP-B says 'wire compileProject() and packAssets() into BuildService', but no contract defines the compiler's and asset pipeline's programmatic entry points (`compile(project, manifest) -> {dsdb, diagnostics}`, `build(project, toolPaths) -> {manifest, diagnostics}`), so WS1 cannot code against them until WS4/WS5 exist, and WS6's build worker cannot type its calls. `MockBuildService` and `createFakeToolchain()` are WS1 deliverables that WS6 needs on D+1 (its entire isolation strategy), but WS1's first days are the install and hello.
  *Fix:* In Phase 0 publish `packages/toolchain/src/api.ts` (types only: BuildService, BuildEvent, ToolPaths, EmulatorHandle, plus `CompileFn` and `PackAssetsFn` signatures) and a 30-line `MockBuildService` in the same package; WS1 implements against it, WS6 mocks against it from day 1, WS4/WS5 export functions matching `CompileFn`/`PackAssetsFn`. Record this as C4 v0.1 or a new C15.
- **Visual verification without a human: emulator windows** (Plan 6 WS1/WS3/WS5/WS8 DoD, 7.1, 8 M0; meta WS3.definition_of_done). Several definitions of done are visual ('selftest ROM shows 128 sprites per screen, two scrolling backgrounds', 'when the example ROM shows its background, the toolchain question is answered', 'sample assets display correctly in the selftest ROM'). melonDS and DeSmuME have no headless or screenshot CLI; a Claude instance cannot see the window, so these checks fall on the user, one more human bottleneck across WS1, WS3, WS5 and WS8. py-desmume (headless, screenshot) is scheduled only for WS8 in week 3, yet py-desmume 0.0.9 ships a CPython 3.13 win_amd64 wheel today (verified by pip download).
  *Fix:* Add `tools/screenshot.py` (py-desmume: load ROM, run N frames with scripted keys, save top/bottom PNGs) to WS1's first-week deliverables and expose it as `dsdude screenshot <rom> --frames N`; every visual DoD becomes 'screenshot at frame N matches golden PNG or passes a described check', and instances read the PNG with their image-capable Read tool. Keep melonDS for `DSD|` log capture and human-eye sessions.

**Minor gaps**

- **Vitest workspace file** (Plan 3.4, 6 WS0; meta WS0.owned_paths). The plan puts `vitest.workspace.ts` at the repo root (3.4, WS0 owned paths). Vitest deprecated the workspace file in 3.2 and removed the `workspace` option in 4.0 ('Remove deprecated workspace option in favor of projects', PR #8218); with the pinned vitest 5.0.1 the file is ignored.
  *Fix:* Use `vitest.config.ts` at the root with `test.projects: ['packages/*', 'apps/*']` and per-package `vitest.config.ts` files; update 3.4 and WS0's owned paths.
- **CODEOWNERS without GitHub and hook enforcement point** (Plan 2.10, 3.4, 6 WS0 DoD; meta WS0.definition_of_done). CODEOWNERS is a hosting-platform feature (review assignment/required reviews); without a GitHub remote it is only a data file, and even on GitHub 'require review from code owners' needs branch protection (free only for public repos). The plan's real mechanism is `tools/check-ownership.ts` in a pre-commit hook, which is advisory: any instance can `--no-verify`, edit the working-tree CODEOWNERS, or commit on a branch created before a CP-A handoff and see stale ownership. Time-based handoffs ('until CP-A') cannot be expressed in CODEOWNERS syntax. The plan does not say how the hook learns which stream is committing.
  *Fix:* Treat the hook as a convenience and make WS0's merge script the enforcement point: `git diff --name-only main...wsN-branch` checked against `tools/ownership.json` at main's HEAD, refusing the merge on violations. Identify the stream by branch prefix (`ws4-*`) or `git config --worktree dsdude.ws`. Encode `from`/`until` checkpoint fields in ownership.json and regenerate CODEOWNERS at each checkpoint. Keep CODEOWNERS only if a GitHub remote is chosen.
- **Windows path length and OneDrive on this machine** (Plan 2.10, 3.4, 9 risk 10, section 1 step 1 (New Project wizard)). Path length is largely fine: `LongPathsEnabled=1` is already set, worktrees at `C:\dev\dsdude-wsN` are short, and npm's flat layout keeps node_modules shallow; the residual risks are git (needs its own `core.longpaths=true` for >260-char working-tree paths such as Playwright snapshots or `dist/win-unpacked/resources/app.asar.unpacked/node_modules/...`) and long test names. OneDrive: moving the repo to C:\dev is correct, but on this machine both Desktop and Documents are OneDrive Known-Folder-Move targets, so a beginner's New Project defaults straight back into OneDrive where `build/`, chokidar and `<rom>.sav` writes will hit sync locks; the 8 files in the current `docs/research/` have no destination in the layout; and Claude Code's per-project memory is keyed on the cwd, so it resets after the move.
  *Fix:* Set `git config core.longpaths true` in the shared repo config during Phase 0; keep Playwright/vitest test names short. Default New Project to `%USERPROFILE%\DSDudeProjects` (not Documents), detect `%OneDrive%` prefixes and warn, and put `build/` under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DSDude\build\<project-hash>` rather than inside the project. Move `docs/research/**` into the new repo under WS0 ownership. Start every instance from its `C:\dev\dsdude-wsN` cwd so CLAUDE.md is picked up.
- **Root scripts and artefacts that cross owner boundaries** (Plan 3.4, 6 WS1/WS3/WS8; meta WS8.owned_paths). `npm run build:runtime` (WS3's deliverable) is a script in the WS0-owned root package.json; `runtime/dist/VERSION` embeds 'git SHA' of a commit that cannot contain its own hash; `runtime/dist/arm9.elf` is committed by WS3 on every build (binary churn, acceptable weekly); `vendor/README.md` (WS1) sits inside a wholly gitignored `vendor/`; the WS1 instance's identity switch to WS8 at week 3 is a new kickoff prompt after compaction, not a continuation, so WS1's remaining work (doctor, tools pack, DeSmuME profile) needs an explicit hand-over list.
  *Fix:* Add `runtime/package.json` to the workspaces so WS3 owns `build:runtime`; use the parent commit SHA or the tree hash of `runtime/` in VERSION; add `!vendor/README.md` to .gitignore in Phase 0; write `docs/kickoff/ws8.md` as WS1's paths plus the new ones with the WS1 leftovers listed.
- **Claude usage limits and instance overhead** (Plan 7.2; meta decision 'concurrency'). Seven or eight concurrently active instances, each reading a 37K-token PLAN.md and large contracts after every compaction, will consume tokens at a rate that may hit subscription rate windows; the plan does not say which billing mode the instances run under.
  *Fix:* Confirm the billing/plan limits before wave 1; keep per-package CLAUDE.md briefs short and point at PLAN.md sections rather than requiring a full read; run headless streams in waves as above.

**What the critic found right**

- Moving the repo out of OneDrive to C:\dev is correct and necessary: on this machine Desktop and Documents are both OneDrive Known-Folder-Move targets, and worktrees write through the shared .git.
- One git worktree per instance with short roots (C:\dev\dsdude-wsN) is the right isolation model; worktrees share the object store, hooks path and config, and LongPathsEnabled is already 1 on this machine.
- The fully pinned stack in section 2.5 makes it possible to preinstall every dependency in Phase 0, which is the cheapest way to defuse the lockfile problem.
- Fixtures as executable contracts (conformance corpus, hello.dsda/.dsdb, golden traces) and a single C core as the host/DS oracle are the right anti-drift mechanism for compiler vs runtime.
- One builtins.json with generated tables and an ABI hash refused at load turns silent bytecode drift into a readable error; append-only ordinals are the right rule.
- Diagnostic code ranges owned per producer (E1xx WS4, E4xx WS5, E6xx WS1, R5xx WS2) avoid catalog collisions without coordination.
- The soft Phase-0 gate ('if day 3 ends without the tag, launch anyway on the committed drafts') and the '// ADR-pending, keep going' rule are the correct stance for agents that must not block on chat.
- Each stream has an explicit, mostly credible isolation strategy (mocked child_process, hand-assembled .dsda, selftest ROM without the core, pure-TS quantizer with golden bytes, MockBuildService, builtins-only completion).
- Day-numbered early checkpoints (D+3, D+7, D+14) with time boxes are appropriate for catching pipeline problems before the calendar hides them.
- Handing contract files to the streams that implement them (assetpack -> WS5, log-protocol/limits -> WS2, language/events -> WS4, artifact -> WS3) is the right long-term ownership even if the timing (CP-A vs tag) needs fixing.
- Node 24 strips TypeScript natively (process.features.typescript = 'strip'), so `node tools/check-ownership.ts` in the hook needs no extra runtime, as the plan implicitly assumes.
- py-desmume 0.0.9 does ship a CPython 3.13 win_amd64 wheel, so the headless screenshot regression idea is sound and can be pulled forward from week 3 to Phase 0.
- Planning WS1 (toolchain install) from hour zero on the critical path, with the SDK example ROM as the first artefact, is the right de-risking order; mingw32-make and make both exist in the MSYS2 install as assumed.
- Explicit per-package package.json/tsconfig/vitest.config/CLAUDE.md ownership and cross-package imports only through src/index.ts is the right convention for agents that cannot negotiate in real time.

### DS and VM engineering (principal engineer)

The architecture is sound and the big bets are right: BlocksDS 1.24 + Wonderful, a prebuilt runtime ELF with a bytecode VM, ndstool re-packing at Play time, portable core + DS platform seam with a host oracle, melonDS default with DeSmuME fallback, dual nocash protocols, GRF assets via grit -ftr, extended palettes, one file per event. I verified against primary sources (GBATEK VRAM/ext-palette tables, blocksds/libnds grf.h and nitrofs_device.c, blocksds/sdk ds_arm9.ld/ds_arm7.ld, blocksds/maxmod effect.c, npm and PyPI) and the pins, the NitroFS fallback logic and the py-desmume cp313 wheel all hold. The problems are in the concrete numbers and contracts that phase0/CP-A will freeze: the VRAM bank table as written overlaps B and E and gives the main screen ext palettes for BG0/BG1 only; draw_rectangle/draw_text have no renderer design and a bitmap layer does not fit sub-BG VRAM; 256-byte instance blocks cannot hold the 33 built-in variables as 8-byte cells; the 4 MB budget assumes a bump arena that stock grfLoadPath/mmLoad (both malloc) bypass and sums to ~3.8 MB of a 3.95 MB machine; the s32 cell payload cannot hold x86-64 pointers so the host oracle is not 'deterministic by construction' without handles and -funsigned-char; the sprite frame stride is wrong for frames under 128 bytes with SpriteMapping_1D_128; the two-screen collision/touch/screen semantics are contradictory; the number model has undefined promotion-overflow, division-overflow, mod-on-fixed and atan2 gaps; per-room asset sets and several GML behaviours (dynamic slot lookup, event inheritance, with-unwinding, event_user) are unspecified. None of these invalidate the plan, but ten of them are major because they land in contracts C2, C3, C6, C11 and C13 and would be implemented divergently by WS2/WS3/WS4/WS5. Fix them before `git tag phase0`; the rest can be ADRs in week 1.

**Major gaps**

- **VRAM bank plan** (3.3 Runtime design summary (VRAM); WS3 deliverables; C13 runtime-limits.json; C11 dsd_platform.h; unified-meta key claim about the bank plan). Section 3.3 and the WS3 deliverables freeze 'B = main BG, E = extra main BG, F/G = main extended palettes'. GBATEK (verified): VRAM E as Main BG maps only at 0x06000000, and A-D as Main BG map at 0x06000000+0x20000*OFS; libnds VRAM_B_MAIN_BG is OFS 0 = 0x06000000, so B and E overlap and corrupt each other. F or G as Main BG extended palette covers slots 0-1 (OFS 0) or slots 2-3 (OFS 1); with G needed for the main OBJ extended palette, only BG0 and BG1 get extended palettes on the main screen, and BG0 is the plan's text layer, so exactly one game BG can use them (fragile, undocumented). E as Main BG ext palette covers all four slots (lower 32 KB used). Extended-palette banks are not CPU-writable while mapped (must map to LCD, write, remap), and the text/console layer, error box and any rectangle layer are not placed in any bank.
  *Fix:* Freeze in runtime-limits.json and the WS3 kickoff: A = VRAM_A_MAIN_SPRITE_0x06400000 (128 KB), B = VRAM_B_MAIN_BG_0x06000000 (128 KB), C = VRAM_C_SUB_BG_0x06200000 (128 KB), D = VRAM_D_SUB_SPRITE (128 KB), E = VRAM_E_BG_EXT_PALETTE (slots 0-3), F = VRAM_F_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE, G = VRAM_G_LCD spare (or texture palette later), H = VRAM_H_SUB_BG_EXT_PALETTE (slots 0-3), I = VRAM_I_SUB_SPRITE_EXT_PALETTE. Budget inside B and C per screen: game text BG map <= 8 KB (512x512) + <= 1024 8bpp tiles (64 KB) = 72 KB; UI/text layer 4bpp font + 16 solid tiles (~4 KB) + double-buffered 32x32 map (4 KB); keep >= 40 KB free. dsd_plat_sprite_load/bg_load write palettes through the LCD mapping. Drop 'E = extra main BG' (larger BGs are v1.1 anyway).
- **draw_rectangle / draw_text / draw_clear rendering** (3.3 Backgrounds; section 4 drawing builtins; C2 builtins.json; C11 dsd_platform.h; WS2 drawlist.c; WS3 selftest 'draw_text layer'; step 7 of the beginner table (red box)). draw_rectangle, draw_clear, draw_set_color and draw_text at arbitrary (x, y) are in builtins.json and the bottom-screen red error box depends on them, but the renderer is OAM sprites + tiled text BGs, C11 has no rectangle primitive, and no bitmap layer is planned. A per-screen bitmap BG cannot be added on the sub screen: ext-rotscale 8bpp bitmaps are 256x256 = 64 KB (16bpp 128 KB), sub BG VRAM tops out at 128 KB (bank C; H/I as sub BG overlap C's range), and 72 KB tiled BG + 64 KB bitmap + font exceeds it; clearing 64 KB per screen per frame also eats VBlank. Sprite-built rectangles cap at 64x64 (128x128 in double-size) and burn affine sets. The plan also never says that draw_text on a text BG snaps to the 8-pixel grid.
  *Fix:* Define a per-screen 'UI layer' for 0.1: BG0, 4bpp text, priority 0, one 8x8 font (95 glyphs) plus 16 solid tiles; draw_text and draw_rectangle snap to 8 px (documented as 'text and boxes snap to 8 pixels'); draw_rectangle writes solid tiles into map cells with the palette number as the colour (draw_set_color maps to the nearest of 16 UI palettes, 15 colours + transparent); draw_clear clears the map; the engine clears the UI map at frame start and commits a double-buffered 2 KB map per screen during VBlank alongside oamUpdate. Add dsd_plat_ui_fill(screen, cx, cy, cw, ch, colour), dsd_plat_ui_text(screen, cx, cy, str, colour) and dsd_plat_ui_clear(screen) to C11 and implement the error box and SELECT overlay on the same layer (not libnds consoleInit, which brings its own VRAM/palette). Defer a bitmap layer (needs a sub tile budget <= 640) or GL2D quads (main screen only) to v1.1.
- **Instance block layout** (3.3 Memory; WS2 instances.c and value.h; C2 OBJS section; C13 instancesMax). 3.3/WS2 fix 'instance pool 512 x 256-byte blocks' with 8-byte tagged cells. The built-in variable list in section 4 (id, object_index, x, y, xprevious, yprevious, xstart, ystart, speed, direction, hspeed, vspeed, gravity, gravity_direction, friction, sprite_index, image_index, image_number, image_speed, image_xscale, image_yscale, image_angle, visible, depth, screen, alarm[0..7]) is 33 values = 264 bytes as cells before any header, cached bbox, collision-grid links or a single user variable. The 128 KB pool figure and instancesMax 512 are derived from an impossible block.
  *Fix:* Store built-ins untagged in a typed C struct (s32 Q20.12/int fields, flags bitfield, grid links, cached bbox; ~150 bytes) accessed through GETBI/SETBI with fixed/int conversion, followed by user variables as 8-byte cells; make the block size a compile-time per-object layout with pool size classes (e.g. 192/320/576 B) or one 320-byte block; have the compiler emit E4xx 'obj_x has too many variables (max N)'; re-derive instancesMax and the pool bytes in runtime-limits.json.
- **Main RAM budget and allocator model** (3.3 Memory; 2.9 Assets (grfLoadPath); WS2 heap.c/rooms.c; WS3 platform loaders; C8 DSD|MEM fields; C13). Available main RAM is 4,194,304 - 49,152 (BlocksDS reserves the last 48 KB) = 4,145,152 bytes (~3.95 MiB). The 3.3 budget at its upper bounds is 0.7 MB binary + 300 KB DSDB + 128 KB instances + 192 KB strings + 1.5 MB room arena + 1 MB maxmod = ~3.81 MiB, leaving ~140 KB for the picolibc heap, stdio buffers, NitroFS and maxmod9 state. Verified in blocksds/libnds grf.h: grfLoadPath/grfLoadFile/grfLoadMem malloc() their gfx/map/pal buffers and the caller frees them, and maxmod's mmLoadEffect/mmLoad also allocate on the ARM9 heap, so the 'room bump arena reset on room_goto' governs nothing in 0.1 (no streamed frames, no pixel masks, no tile painting) while the actual big consumers (sound data, transient GRF buffers up to 128 KB each) are unbudgeted heap. The plan also never states what the arena holds.
  *Fix:* Rebudget: room arena 256-512 KB (staging only, or drop it until streaming lands), sound RAM cap 768 KB computed from the actual loaded effect/module sizes in assets.manifest.json, DSDB 300 KB, instance pool per the block fix, string/array arena 192 KB, and require >= 512 KB free heap at boot; read the real numbers from the link map (__end__ .. __eheap_end) at boot and print them in DSD|MEM; free GRF buffers immediately after the VRAM upload (LIFO order, no fragmentation) or fread the whole GRF into a scratch buffer and parse with grfLoadMem; make roomArenaBytes and a new soundRamBytes the single source in runtime-limits.json and the IDE meters.
- **Host-oracle determinism** (2.4; WS2 value.h/fixed.c/strings.c; C2 cell definition; risk 5). 2.4 claims host/DS determinism 'by construction' with a 64-bit MSYS2 UCRT64 gcc build, but the C2 cell is {u32 tag; s32 payload} with strings/arrays/instances as 'ref-counted pointers': an x86-64 pointer cannot fit in s32, so the host cell would differ from the DS cell. Other divergences not covered by -fwrapv + LUTs + the division test: plain char is unsigned on ARM EABI and signed on x86 (string_char_at, ord, comparisons, hashing); INT_MIN/-1 and x/0 trap on x86 while the DS divider returns defined garbage; qsort ordering of equal keys differs between picolibc and ucrt (depth sort, with/collision iteration order); rand()/time seeds; libc %-formatting for string(); sqrt via hardware unit vs software.
  *Fix:* Make cell payloads 32-bit handles (arena offsets / pool indices) on both targets and static_assert sizeof(cell)==8 plus struct offsets in both builds; compile both with -funsigned-char -fwrapv -fno-strict-aliasing; guard every division in the VM (zero and INT_MIN/-1) before touching the divider and define the results; implement the core's own PRNG (xorshift32, seedable from the CLI --seed) and its own stable sort and iteration order (instance creation order); format numbers in-core; add sqrt and atan2 equivalence fixtures next to the division test; run the host suite under both -O0 and -O2.
- **Sprite frame stride vs OAM 1D mapping** (2.9 Assets; C3 assetpack; WS5 stitching; WS3 OBJ VRAM allocator; unified-meta key claim on the stride). C3 and 2.9 define 'frames stitched vertically, stride (w/8)*(h/8)*64 bytes' (32 for 4bpp) and WS3 uses oamInit(&oamMain, SpriteMapping_1D_128, true). With a 128-byte 1D boundary the OAM tile index addresses OBJ VRAM in 128-byte steps, so a frame smaller than 128 bytes (8x8 8bpp = 64 B; 8x8, 16x8, 8x16 4bpp = 32-64 B) cannot be addressed individually: frames 1..n of such sprites would display the wrong tiles. The manifest's 'VRAM bytes per room' would also be under-counted for them.
  *Fix:* Define frameStride = roundUp(max(frameBytes, 128), 128) for SpriteMapping_1D_128 in contracts/assetpack.md; have the pipeline pad each frame in the stitched sheet (or post-process the GRF gfx block) and report the padded stride and VRAM bytes in assets.manifest.json; add an 8x8 3-frame 4bpp sprite to fixtures/assets and the selftest ROM.
- **Two screens in one coordinate space** (3.3 Collision; section 4 built-in variables and constants; C1 room.json; C6; WS6b room editor meters). 3.3 says collisions run with 'both screens in one logical coordinate space' while instances carry a built-in screen variable, objects have a Screen property, room.json has per-screen views, and touch_x/touch_y, outside_room and instance_create's screen are unspecified. With both views at (0,0) (the Flappy layout: game on top, HUD on bottom) a HUD instance at (10,10) and the bird at (10,10) would collide; a tall continuous playfield instead needs the screen property to change as things move. draw_set_screen implies a sprite present in both OBJ banks (A and D are separate) which the per-room VRAM accounting does not model.
  *Fix:* Write into contracts/language.md and events.md: (1) a room is one coordinate space and each screen shows its view rectangle; (2) instance.screen selects the engine and the view used to place its OAM entries; (3) a collision pair requires equal screen (DSGM precedent) unless room.json sets collisionsAcrossScreens = true; (4) touch_x = tp.px + view_x[bottom], touch_y = tp.py + view_y[bottom]; (5) outside_room uses the room rectangle; (6) instance_create(x, y, obj[, screen]) defaults to the object's Screen; (7) draw_set_screen may only draw sprites that are in that screen's VRAM set (E4xx statically, R5xx at runtime); templates: Flappy both views (0,0), 'tall' template bottom view (0,192). Add conformance fixtures for each.
