- **Number model edge cases** (2.8 Numbers; section 4 math builtins; WS2 value.h/fixed.c; C6 conformance corpus; W04x checker warnings). 2.8 defines Lua-style promotion but not its failure modes: promoting an int to Q20.12 shifts left 12, so any int with |n| > 524,287 (score * 1000, frame counters, millisecond timers) silently corrupts in a mixed comparison (`if (score > 0.5)`), mixed arithmetic (`n + 0.5`) and any `/` whose result is >= 524,288 (2000000 / 3). `mod` is declared integer-only although GML's mod and % work on reals (5.5 mod 2 = 1.5). Undefined: the round(2.5) tie rule, string(n) formatting (GML prints integral reals without decimals and others with two), real('abc'), negative zero. point_direction and the direction/speed built-ins need atan2, which libnds trig LUTs do not provide (sin/cos/tan/asin/acos only), and the plan names no implementation.
  *Fix:* Specify in language.md and value.h: mixed comparison in 64-bit ((int64)a << 12 vs b); mixed arithmetic in 64-bit with an out-of-range result raising R52x in debug and saturating in release; `/` whose exact result does not fit Q20.12 returns the truncated int quotient (documented: 'numbers with fractions are limited to about +-524,000'); fixed mod = remainder with the dividend's sign; round = half away from zero; string(): no decimals when the fraction is zero, otherwise two, never '-0.00'; add an integer atan2 (octant LUT + linear interpolation) to fixed.c with a host/DS equivalence fixture; each rule becomes a conformance program.
- **Per-room asset sets, loading and room transitions** (3.2 steps 4-5 and 8; 3.3 Sprites; C3 manifest; C2 ROOMS section; WS2 rooms.c; WS4 binder). 3.2 step 8 'load the first room's assets into VRAM' and C3's 'VRAM bytes per room' never define which sprites and sounds belong to a room: sprites are referenced by placed objects, by objects created via instance_create anywhere, by sprite_index assignments and draw_sprite calls, separately per screen. Sounds are loaded on demand by mmLoadEffect/mmLoad, so the first audio_play_music mid-game blocks on a NitroFS read (BlocksDS nitrofs reads through cardRead in FF_MAX_SS chunks; melonDS emulates cart timing, only DeSmuME is instant). room_restart (every Flappy death) reloads everything; room_goto called mid-event is not deferred; whether Destroy events fire at room end is unstated.
  *Fix:* Define in C3/C6/C2: roomAssets = closure over objects placed in the room plus objects named in instance_create reachable from their code; sprites = those objects' sprite fields plus sprite identifiers in their code, per screen (object.screen; draw_set_screen adds the other screen); sounds likewise; the compiler emits the table into DSDB ROOMS and the pipeline budgets VRAM/palettes/sound RAM per room from it. Runtime: defer room_goto to the end of the frame, fire Room End, do not fire Destroy (GML), blank the screens (setBrightness) while loading, skip the reload when the asset set is unchanged (room_restart, same-room goto), and raise R5xx 'spr_x is not loaded in rm_y' for an unlisted asset.
- **Compiler/runtime GML semantics left undefined** (section 4 Scopes; C2 opcodes and OBJS; C6; WS4 binder/codegen; WS2 events.c/collision.c). C2/C6 leave behaviours undefined that WS2 and WS4 will implement differently: (a) `inst.var` on a runtime instance id (`var n = instance_nearest(...); n.hp -= 1`) needs a per-object symbol-to-slot table at runtime; the plan's only mechanism is the 8-entry overflow map for names absent from the layout, and GETDYN/SETDYN have no lookup source; (b) event inheritance (a child without step.dss must run the parent's step, event_inherited() is absent), collision_<parent> matching child objects, and with/instance_place/instance_number on a parent including descendants; (c) with(): break/continue/exit/return must unwind through WITHEND, iteration over a snapshot of ids, instances destroyed mid-loop, other = outer self, nested with; (d) reading a never-assigned instance variable (GML errors; DSS slots exist from creation as undefined); (e) user_0..7 event files exist but event_user(n) is not a builtin; (f) choose/min/max are variadic but CALLN has no arity convention; (g) draw_text(x, y, number) and show_debug_message(number) must accept numbers although string + number is an error.
  *Fix:* Add to DSDB OBJS a sorted (symbolId, slot) table per object plus parent id and an ancestor bitset; GETDYN/SETDYN binary-search it, fall back to the overflow map, and reads of undefined slots raise R50x 'x was never given a value in obj_y'; define event inheritance and event_inherited() and ancestor matching for collision events, with, instance_* builtins; specify with-unwinding in C2 (WITHEND on every exit path, snapshot iteration, skip destroyed); add event_user; mark variadic builtins in builtins.json with min/max arity; make draw_text/show_debug_message coerce numbers; add one conformance fixture per item.

**Minor gaps**

- **VM register stack in DTCM** (3.3 VM; WS2 vm.arm.c; C13 registersPerFrame). BlocksDS ds_arm9.ld (verified) lays out the 16 KB DTCM as SVC stack (0x100) + IRQ stack (0x100) + user stack + .dtcm/.sbss, all shared. A 4 KB VM register stack = 512 cells = only 8 frames at the maximum 64-register window, and it shrinks the C user stack (printf, GRF loads, engine recursion) to ~11 KB with no watermark check.
  *Fix:* Size the VM stack at 8-16 KB in main RAM (the active window stays in the 4 KB D-cache) or keep only the current window in DTCM with spill on CALL; measure the C stack high-water mark in the selftest ROM and print it in DSD|MEM; keep the friendly R5xx call-depth error.
- **VM benchmark realism, ITCM budget, -mlong-calls** (2.3; 3.3 VM; M1; WS2/WS3 benchmark harness; risk 3). The 19-27 cycles/op figures came from an untagged 20.12 register VM; the real cells are tagged (two tag loads, compare and branch per arithmetic op), bytecode lives in main RAM (46 ARM9 cycles per 32-byte line miss) and typical ops are GETSLOT/SETSLOT on instance memory. 35K ops per frame at 100% CPU is 32 cycles/op, so the M1 gate is plausible only if the benchmark uses the real cell layout and op mix. The BlocksDS *.arm.c rule adds -mlong-calls (every call becomes a literal load + ldr pc), and libnds already places exception handlers in the 32 KB ITCM, so the ITCM budget for handlers plus hot builtins is unmeasured.
  *Fix:* Specify bench.dsda as a mix (40% arithmetic on tagged cells, 30% GETSLOT/SETSLOT, 20% compare+jump, 10% CALL/RET) with bytecode in main RAM and the register file in its final location; compile vm.arm.c with a custom rule without -mlong-calls; print arm-none-eabi-size of .itcm/.dtcm in npm run build:runtime with a <= 24 KB ITCM ceiling; keep 35K/frame as the gate but state the mix.
- **Sound: effect limits, RAM, streaming** (2.9 Sounds; 3.3 Audio; C3 soundbank limit; WS5; WS7 manual). Verified in blocksds/maxmod source/core/effect.c: mmEffectEx returns MM_SFXHAND_INVALID when mmAllocChannel finds no free channel (no stealing unless a channel was mmEffectRelease'd), and effects share the 16 hardware channels with the module's channels (an 8-channel XM leaves 8). mmLoadEffect/mmLoad allocate from the ARM9 heap; 22050 Hz 16-bit mono costs 44 KB/s of RAM per effect, so a few seconds of effects consume the whole 'maxmod 0.5-1 MB'. The soundbank <= 1 MB limit is a NitroFS size, not RAM. WAV/streamed music is impossible without mmStreamOpen, which the plan does not include, and the manual does not say 'music must be a tracker module'.
  *Fix:* audio_play_sound tolerates an invalid handle and counts dropped effects in DSD|STAT; call mmEffectRelease right after starting one-shot effects; default effects to 8-bit (or ADPCM) at <= 16 kHz in the pipeline and record RAM bytes per sound in the manifest; preload the room's effects at room start and the module before Room Start; document the channel sharing and the tracker-only rule in the manual and the E4xx catalog.
- **NitroFS read performance** (3.2 warm-Play timings; 3.3 Memory; C13 roomArenaBytes; risk 14). BlocksDS nitrofs_device.c (verified) reads through cardRead in FF_MAX_SS-sized chunks, each paying command and KEY1 gap overhead, so real carts deliver low single-digit MB/s at best and DLDI flashcarts ~1 MB/s; melonDS emulates cartridge transfer timing, so room loads are not free there either, only DeSmuME is instant. The 1.5 MB room arena implies 0.4-1.5 s per room change and the plan calls emulator loads 'effectively instant'.
  *Fix:* Add a 1 MB NitroFS read timing to the M1 benchmark on melonDS; set the default per-room read budget to <= 512 KB with a meter ('Room load ~0.3 s on a flashcart'); keep the skip-reload optimisation; document that DeSmuME hides load times.
- **ndstool packing and verification** (3.2 step 6; C1 gamecode; C4 verifyRom; WS1; risk 2). packRom verifies by parsing `ndstool -i` text; the -b icon must be 32x32 with <= 16 colours; project.json exposes gamecode but melonDS and DeSmuME detect homebrew (argv injection, DLDI auto-patching, save-type heuristics) by gamecode '####'; an empty -d folder produces no FNT/FAT and nitroFSInit fails with ENODEV. The '>= 0x8000' rule is correctly reasoned (libnds refuses FNT/FAT below 0x8000 because normal card reads cannot return the secure-area region) and is guaranteed by the 0x4000 header plus a >16 KB ARM9.
  *Fix:* Read the header directly in packRom() (u32 at 0x40/0x44/0x48/0x4C, 'NitroFS!' at fatOffset+fatSize, header CRC-16 at 0x15E) instead of scraping -i; quantise the icon in the pipeline; force gamecode '####' in 0.1 and hide or warn on the field; always emit game.dsdb so -d is never empty.
- **DeSmuME legacy nocash protocol with dynamic strings** (2.6; C8; WS3 platform layer; M0). DeSmuME's NocashMessage reads the message from the instruction stream immediately after `mov r12,r12; b; .hword 0x6464` (with %r0%..%pc% substitution), so a generic dsd_plat_log(str) carrying dynamic DSD|ERR/STAT/MEM lines cannot use a fixed signature in ROM code; the signature must be ARM-mode while the runtime is -mthumb; and SWI 0xFC (DeSmuME's pointer-based log) is unsafe on real hardware and other emulators. M0's 'DSD|LOG|hello from both emulators' passes with a constant string and would then fail for every real line.
  *Fix:* Implement the DeSmuME path as a RAM-resident ARM stub: copy [mov r12,r12; b +len; .hword 0x6464, 0; string bytes; bx lr] into a 512-byte main-RAM buffer, DC_FlushRange + IC_InvalidateRange, call it through an ARM function pointer; escape '%'; keep 0x04FFFA18 for melonDS/no$gba; make the M0 and selftest checks use a dynamic string.
- **OAM builder policies** (3.3 Sprites; C3 sprite sizes; WS2 drawlist.c; WS5 E4xx catalog). The shadow-OAM design omits policies that affect determinism and beginner UX: the depth sort must be stable and identical on host/DS; OBJ priority relative to BGs is undefined so depth cannot place sprites behind the background or under the text layer; hardware rotates about the OBJ centre while GML rotates about the origin (double-size offsets too); more than 32 affine or 128 visible instances per screen has no drop rule; xscale = +-1 with angle 0 should use hflip/vflip instead of an affine set; sprites outside the 12 OBJ sizes are rejected with E4xx instead of padded.
  *Fix:* Specify in C6/C11: stable sort by (depth, id); priority map (UI layer 0, sprites 1-2, game BG 3, with depth < 0 above the UI text if desired); origin-correct affine placement (rotate the origin-to-centre vector, subtract the double-size half-extent); drop the lowest-priority extras and count them once per second in DSD|STAT; flip fast path; pipeline pads sprites <= 64x64 to the next OBJ size and errors only above 64x64.
- **IDE build worker / emulator process ownership** (3.1/3.2 build worker; C4 EmulatorManager; C5; WS6). The emulator child is spawned inside the utilityProcess build worker; if the worker is restarted (crash, hung tool) or the IDE crashes, the melonDS PID is lost and orphans accumulate (no Job Objects without a native module). chokidar 5.0.0 is ESM-only (verified type: module) while main/preload are CJS; fine when electron-vite bundles it, broken if it is externalised. Graceful taskkill makes melonDS rewrite melonDS.toml on exit (harmless since the IDE rewrites it before each launch, but the writer must run after the kill completes).
  *Fix:* Keep EmulatorManager in the main process (or persist PID + start time under %LOCALAPPDATA%\DSDude and reconcile on startup and before-quit with taskkill /T); add chokidar to the bundled (non-external) list in electron-vite config; await process exit before writing melonDS.toml.
- **Palette accounting** (beginner table step 5; C3; C13; WS5 budget checks; WS6 meters). The 'Palettes 1/16' meter and extPalettesPerScreen 16 conflate distinct resources: 256-colour sprites use the 16 OBJ extended palettes, 16-colour sprites use the 16 standard 16-colour OBJ palettes in palette RAM, 8bpp backgrounds use BG extended-palette slots, and 4bpp backgrounds and the UI layer use the standard BG palette.
  *Fix:* Track four counters per screen (obj16, obj256, bg16, bg256) in assets.manifest.json, runtime-limits.json and the status-bar meters; word the E4xx message per pool.

**What the critic found right**

- BlocksDS 1.24 via Wonderful into the existing C:\msys64 is the correct toolchain choice for a redistributable IDE; the licensing analysis (libnds Zlib, maxmod ISC, GPL host tools as separate processes, devkitPro's redistribution/trademark policy) is accurate.
- Bytecode VM inside a prebuilt runtime ELF with ndstool re-packing at Play time is the right execution strategy; ndstool only reads ELF program headers, so Play needs no compiler, and the ITCM/ARM-mode/computed-goto direction is correct for a 67 MHz core with a 8-16 MHz effective bus.
- The FNT/FAT >= 0x8000 and 'NitroFS!' magic reasoning matches blocksds/libnds nitrofs_device.c, and the two-ELF 0x4000 header plus any ARM9 larger than 16 KB guarantees it.
- DeSmuME vs melonDS NitroFS behaviour is a non-issue as planned: verified that BlocksDS nitroFSInit tries argv[0] via fatInitDefault and, when that open fails and the path came from argv (DeSmuME's homebrew argv), falls back to slot-1 card reads; melonDS supplies no argv and goes straight to card reads.
- Printing through both the 0x04FFFA18 register protocol (melonDS/no$gba) and the legacy mov r12,r12 signature (DeSmuME), plus an on-device console overlay, is the right coverage given the official DeSmuME build ignores the registers.
- Portable core + dsd_platform.h seam with a host trace runner is the right way to let two instances own the runtime and to give the compiler an execution oracle; UBSan trap mode is the correct choice on UCRT64 (no libubsan).
- Q20.12 fixed point as the single fractional representation with 64-bit multiply intermediates, libnds LUT trig with degrees at the boundary, and no doubles anywhere is correct for the ARM946E-S; the hardware divider and C truncation agree so the division-equivalence test is the right guard.
- A logical instance list decoupled from OAM with per-screen shadow OAM rebuilt every frame and committed at VBlank fixes DSGM's 128-instances-per-room mistake; 128 sprites and 32 affine sets per engine and the 1D_128 mapping over 128 KB per screen are the right hardware facts.
- Extended palettes for 256-colour sprites and backgrounds (one 256-colour palette each, 16 per engine) is the closest thing to 'just import a PNG' and the bank sizes/allowed uses quoted from GBATEK are correct.
- grit -ftr GRF assets loaded with stock grfLoadPath, mmutil -d soundbanks with mmInitDefault('nitro:/soundbank.bin'), and the BlocksDS default rom_arm9 Makefile are exactly what the SDK's own examples do; the -mRtf (not -mRtpf) note for 8bpp backgrounds is right.
- The pinned npm stack exists at the stated versions (Electron 44.4.5, electron-vite 5.0.0 with Vite 7.3.6 inside its ^7 peer range, React 19.3.0, TypeScript 7.0.2, monaco-editor 0.57.0, pixi.js 8.21.0, dockview 8.3.1, zod 4.6.5, vitest 5.0.1, @playwright/test 1.63.0, electron-builder 26.15.3, etc.), and py-desmume 0.0.9 ships a cp313 win_amd64 wheel, so the headless e2e screenshot plan is viable without a compiler.
- One file per event, contracts frozen at phase0 with ADR-only changes, append-only builtins ordinals with an ABI hash refused at load, mechanical path ownership, a repo outside OneDrive, the M1 cycle-counted VM gate, and a human beginner as the M5 gate are all sound engineering and governance choices.
- The per-frame instruction watchdog (R510), friendly R5xx errors with object/event/file/line, and the 'one mistake yields one diagnostic' rule are the right beginner-facing runtime design.
- The frame order (input, Begin Step, Alarms, Button/Touch, Step, built-in motion, collisions, End Step, animation, Outside Room, Draw, VBlank commit) matches GameMaker closely enough for the tutorial content and is implementable at 60 fps with native motion/collision loops.
